# Research: competitors, experts, gaps

Compiled 2026-09-03. Companion to `docs/DESIGN.md`. Everything here is evidence for the spec, not a replacement for it.

## Method

Three passes.

**Primary reading.** 27 named sources were fetched and read: platform and press investigations, federal and international agency pages, NGO and campaign material, and two live vigilante Discord communities. Where a site blocked automated fetching (Bloomberg 403, IMDb 403, Child Rescue Coalition Cloudflare, FBI 403, ourrescue.org Cloudflare, APA Incapsula) the entry records how it was actually read: an archive.org snapshot fetched with curl, the publisher's own PDF, a syndication original, or search-snippet coverage. Anything read as coverage rather than directly is labeled so, and nothing from a coverage-only read is treated as verbatim.

**Landscape survey.** Eight areas were swept for products, companies, open-source projects, standards, datasets and agencies: platform trust and safety, parent apps, investigator tools, open source and research, UX patterns, legal and regulatory, reviewer tooling and wellness, and 2025-2026 case files. Roughly 190 entries were logged with status, pricing or access, strengths, weaknesses and relevance. Two further areas, academic and practitioner experts, are in section 3.

**Verification.** 24 load-bearing claims, the ones a design decision or a build-order item rests on, were each run through three independent lenses: a primary-source check, a recency check for 2026 staleness, and a skeptic pass whose job was to refute the claim. Six survived unchanged. Eighteen came back partially confirmed, with a correction written from the primary text. Those corrections are in section 4 and are folded into sections 5 and 8. A critic then reread the whole assembly for gaps, which is section 7.

**Honesty note.** This is a September 2026 snapshot of a landscape that moved four times in the last twelve months. Roblox open-sourced three safety models in August 2026, ROOST shipped Coop 1.0 in June 2026, Discord changed its privileged-intent threshold on 2026-06-10, the EU derogation lapsed for four months in 2026, and the Australian eSafety undertaking created an audit-procurement category that did not exist before August 2026. Vendor pricing, model availability, and case law will drift. Several figures below could not be verified from a primary source and are marked. Two research sessions hit an exhausted web-search budget and fell back to direct fetches; where that limited a check, the entry says so.

---

## 1. What the sources say

### Roblox and platforms

**Child safety on Roblox** (Wikipedia, living article, content current through August 2026). https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_safety_on_Roblox Read directly. A chronological survey: 30+ US arrests since 2018 (24 by July 2024, 6 in Jan-Jun 2025), 100M+ DAU in Q2 2025, ~40% of players under 13 in 2024, ~3,000 moderators in 2024 up from ~1,600 in Oct 2020. Exploitation cults (764, CVLT, The Com, Spawnism, the last carving a symbol into children's skin in July 2025) coordinate largely on Discord. Safety rollouts: under-13 DM auto-block and content labels Nov 18 2024, Trusted Connections with facial or ID verification July 2025, mandatory age verification for chat from Jan 1 2026 globally, with age-estimation errors misidentifying children as adults within hours of launch. Legal wave: Roblox sued Ruben Sim for $1.6M and settled at $150,000 in Nov 2021; sent Schlep a cease-and-desist and IP ban in Aug 2025, followed by a 10%+ stock drop and a petition from Rep. Ro Khanna. Settlements: Nevada $10M programs plus $2.5M awareness (Apr 2026), Alabama $12.2M and West Virginia $11.08M (Apr 2026), Mississippi $9M plus $5M penalty (June 2026), South Dakota $8.6M split $5M education / $2.6M ICAC / $1M consumer protection (July 2026). Australia threatened an A$49.5M fine (Feb 2026). State suits: LA Aug 2025, KY Oct 2025, TX Nov 2025, IA and TN Dec 2025, IN May 2026 (a 70-page complaint), AR June 2026. Country bans from UAE 2018 through Egypt Feb 2026. Brazil classified it 18+ under the Felca Law in Mar 2026; Fairplay and NCOSE filed an FTC complaint May 2026; Durbin and Hawley opened a Senate investigation Aug 2026. Robux exchange cited at $105 per 30,000. *Relevance:* confirms DESIGN section 1 and 2. The catches came from YouTuber vigilantes, and the platform response to decoys is bans and lawsuits, which validates the no-decoy rule and the single NCMEC path. Adds a non-sexual coercion pattern (self-harm cults on Discord) the bot's signal catalog must cover, and argues for treating age bands as probabilistic.

**Roblox's Pedophile Problem** (Bloomberg Businessweek, Olivia Carville and Cecilia D'Anastasio, 2024-07-22, updated 07-23). https://www.bloomberg.com/features/2024-roblox-pedophile-problem Read via the 2024-07-25 Wayback snapshot fetched with curl and stripped of HTML; direct fetch and archive.ph were blocked. An eight-month investigation built on the Arnold Castillo case: a Paterson NJ developer running Sonic Eclipse Online as DoctorRofatnik, employing roughly two dozen kids, sending sexual messages to a 12-year-old employee in 2020, then in 2022 grooming a 15-year-old Indiana girl through Roblox, Instagram, Cash App gifts and Amazon deliveries before paying an Uber driver $1,000 to move her 350 miles. She was missing 8 days; he got 15 years after an Aug 2023 plea. Numbers: 78M DAU at publication, 36M daily in Sept 2020 with more than half under 13, 50,000+ chat messages per second, ~3,000 moderators against TikTok's 40,000 at 3x the DAU, 13M games, a stated goal of 1 billion daily users, $3.5B bookings in 2023 with developers taking 30%, 13,316 CyberTipline reports in 2023 against 2,973 in 2022 (under 0.04% of all NCMEC reports), 1,300+ law-enforcement requests answered in 2023. More than 20 current and former trust-and-safety staff described growth outranking safety; 8 said so directly. The 2022 moderation guide did not contain the word grooming, and automated grooming detection beyond text filters did not exist before 2022 (Roblox disputes). Robux and gift cards are the standard lure: McElroy (48, Ellis KS, registered offender, 8-year-old victim, 20+ images for thousands of Robux, "Anything for Robux"), Penczak (45, North Port FL, 13-year-old boy, three years, 13-year sentence, password shared so he could load gift cards and then read the boy's messages). Gift-card price points from police reports: $25 for 2,000 Robux, $100 for 10,000. Evasion tokens traded on darknet forums: leVe, ghost emoji, disc emoji, "cord", "erp". A reporter in Brookhaven RP posing as a 4-year-old got "leVe the game and lets chat" within seconds among 21 players. Kirra Pendergast's session with 75 students in Lennox Head found ~60 playing Roblox, ~12 asked to be boyfriend or girlfriend, 2 offered Robux for inappropriate acts. Simon has 1.2M subscribers and was banned at least 100 times. Roblox took 4 days to close Doc's account and file with NCMEC after Simon's report; the game came down on a Sega copyright claim, not a safety action; the Sonic Eclipse Discord closed only after Businessweek asked for comment. *Relevance:* the richest public source of ground-truth trajectories. Confirms economic bait, the normalizer lexicon, the probe-then-migrate pattern, alt-account clustering, and the developer-with-child-employees fan-out profile. Adds password sharing as a distinct economic-bait signal and supports shipping the Discord bot first.

**Roblox banned a YouTuber for hunting child predators on its platform** (Tubefilter, James Hale, 2025-08-13, syndicated on IMDb News). https://www.imdb.com/news/ni65426974/ IMDb returned 403; read at the Tubefilter original, supplemented with Rolling Stone (Miles Klee, 2025-08-25) marked as coverage. Roblox permanently banned Schlep, 22, with close to 1M subscribers across two channels, and threatened legal action over new accounts. The cease-and-desist grounds are the exact prohibitions Guardian inherits: simulated child-endangerment conversations, sharing or soliciting PII, and steering users off-platform to Discord. Schlep claims six arrests, says he filed support tickets and mailed binders of chat logs to San Mateo, and that Roblox acted only after videos went public. Roblox supplied about ten prosecutor quotes against vigilante hunting, one used: DA Christopher de Barrena-Sarobe, "people don't get to be Batman." Coverage adds: ~40% of users under 13, of the six arrests one was not prosecuted and two were heading to pleas, a #FreeSchlep petition passed 230,000 signatures, Khanna's petition exceeded 100,000, timeline Aug 7 2025 FBI/NCMEC statement, Aug 8 cease-and-desist, ~Aug 15 stricter romantic and sexual content rules, and 12+ private suits plus the Louisiana AG suit. The framing case is a California lawsuit where a 10-year-old was groomed on Roblox, moved to Discord, and kidnapped by a 27-year-old. *Relevance:* confirms CLAUDE.md rule 2 in the platform's own words, and the ignored-ticket complaint is the argument for an auditable submission trail.

**Roblox has booted paedophile hunter Schlep. Who's watching out for the kids now?** (ABC News Australia, Virginia Trioli, 2025-08-30). https://www.abc.net.au/news/2025-08-30/roblox-banned-predator-hunter-schlep-gaming-kids-watching/105711644 Read directly. Opinion column. Schlep (Andrew, Texas) has 800,000+ subscribers and reports at least six arrests; Roblox cited "simulated child endangerment" and taking conversations off platform, and said "actions taken by vigilante groups increase the risk of more users being exposed to bad actors." Trioli notes Roblox's stated protections are only "keyword identification in their online chat systems and age-limited sign-up rules," that Roblox is exempt from Australia's under-16 social media ban because it is classified as a game, and that expecting the eSafety Commissioner to police it is "like expecting a well-intentioned entomologist to police a swarm of locusts." Schlep's claimed NCMEC and Arkansas police cooperation is unconfirmed by any public police statement. *Relevance:* the grooming path it describes (in-game contact, DM, off-platform move, meetup) matches the stage model, and the regulatory gap is the one an operator-side kernel fills without the vigilante posture.

### Law enforcement and agencies

**What tactics can be used to catch online predators?** (APA Monitor on Psychology, Judicial Notebook, Vol. 53 No. 1 p. 35, Jan/Feb 2022). https://www.apa.org/monitor/2022/01/jn WebFetch returned empty and curl hit an Incapsula challenge; read from APA's own full-issue PDF (https://www.apa.org/monitor/2022/2022-01-monitor.pdf, 11.9 MB) with pdftotext. Cynthia Calkins and Sarah Lynch of John Jay use *Ohio v. Deuble* (2021): an officer posed as a 15-year-old "Bella Jane," Deuble sent unsolicited sexual material and arranged a park meeting, and officers identified him by texting the decoy account and watching who reacted, then confirming from a notification on his unlocked home screen. He was one of only two people in the park and fit the description; the trial court found probable cause and the Ohio Court of Appeals reversed under *Riley v. California* (2014), finding an expectation of privacy in the notification screen. The US Supreme Court denied Ohio's petition. Deuble is a tier II Ohio registrant. DOJ reported over 25,200 investigated technology-driven sexual offenses against minors in 2018; a 2017 survey found people underestimate the risk of not using a screen lock. *Relevance:* even a sworn officer's decoy sting produced a reversal, which reinforces the no-decoy and no-LE-tasking rules, and the notification-screen holding argues for keeping evidence excerpts off device notifications in the parent app.

**The route to locating child predators is often through identification of their victims** (ICE Homeland Security Investigations, 2015-07-29). https://www.ice.gov/news/releases/route-locating-child-predators-often-through-identification-their-victims Read directly. HSI's Victim Identification Program launched at the Cyber Crimes Center in late 2011. Analysts enhance imagery and mine incidental details (prescription bottles, vehicle make and model, background objects) to place a victim, then hand leads to a field office. Operation Round Table: 14 arrests, 251 victims across 39 states and 5 countries, a Tor-hosted site where offenders posed as women to coerce boys. Stephen Keating: 15 children, 110 years, $1.2M restitution. Victim identifications rose from 927 in FY2013 (589 girls, 338 boys) to 1,036 in FY2014 (522 girls, 514 boys). C3 completed a renovation on July 22 and ran a two-week victim-ID training with 14 field offices. *Relevance:* confirms the victim-ID framing of the investigator surface, and the incidental-detail workflow is exactly what Guardian would assist on structured text and case notes rather than imagery.

**Hunting online predators** (RCMP Gazette, Amelia Thatcher, 2017-07-05). https://rcmp.ca/en/gazette/hunting-online-predators Read directly. Corporal Jared Clarke, five years on Saskatchewan's ICE team, 12 years policing, carries 75 to 100 files a year while one hands-on case (Russell Wolfe, Saskatoon, 14 local victims, open since 2014) consumes hundreds or thousands of investigative hours. Work splits into reactive files (parents, detachments, the National Child Exploitation Coordination Centre in Ottawa) and proactive files (peer-to-peer tracing material to an IP to a subscriber to a warrant, plus online covert communication where he poses as a child or as a fellow offender). Named obstacles: apps that change day to day and vary by region, full-device encryption when locked or powered off, and the psychological toll of increasingly graphic content. His advice to front-line officers is that there is no offender profile (offenders have included teachers and police officers) and that improper seizure destroys evidence permanently. *Relevance:* confirms the triage bottleneck and the evidence-chain design, and the fast-changing regional app landscape is the argument for a versioned lexicon with the version shown on every score.

**Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force Program** (OJJDP, US DOJ; FY2025 figures). https://ojjdp.ojp.gov/programs/internet-crimes-against-children-task-force-program Read directly. 61 task forces created in 1998, linking more than 6,200 federal, state, local and Tribal agencies. FY2025: nearly 347,000 investigations, more than 17,000 arrests, approximately 73,000 professionals trained, on $33.9M of federal funding, down from $39.9M in FY2024 and $42.4M in FY2023 (FY2022 $39.6M, FY2021 $37.7M). OCEPI was established in 2023. Training and TA partners include SEARCH (platform and device technology), NW3C, the Innocent Justice Foundation (a funded reviewer mental-health and wellness program), Fox Valley Technical College (undercover operations), and NCMEC. The public route to a local unit is icactaskforce.org/TaskForceContacts. Resources include June 2023 sextortion victim and law-enforcement guides and the 2023 National Strategy. *Relevance:* confirms the DESIGN section 1 numbers and sharpens them with the funding decline. Undercover work is an ICAC training track, and reviewer wellness is a funded federal line item, both of which support Guardian's constraints.

**Violent Crimes Against Children** (FBI program page; Wayback snapshot carries a news feed through 08.25.2026). https://www.fbi.gov/investigate/violent-crime/vcac Direct fetch returned 403 to WebFetch and curl; read from the web.archive.org id_ copy via curl, gunzipped. Five investigative priorities: child abductions, contact offenses including CSAM production and sextortion, online exploitation networks, CSAM trafficking, international parental kidnapping. Child sexual exploitation cases are worked by Child Exploitation and Human Trafficking Task Forces in all 56 field offices, many undercover, coordinated with DOJ-funded ICAC task forces. FBI personnel assigned to NCMEC review CyberTipline reports, identify suspects and forward them to field offices, screening for four categories including online enticement of children for sexual acts. Jurisdiction dates to the 1932 Lindbergh Law and covers children of tender age, usually 12 or younger, with no ransom demand, state-line crossing, or 24-hour wait required. CARD teams were established October 2005; the Endangered Child Alert Program began in 2004; VCACITF (formerly Innocent Images International) became operational in 2004 with 68 investigators from almost 46 countries and a five-week US training. NCMEC's hotline is 1-800-THE-LOST. The Child ID app stores data on device only and transmits only when the user emails it. *Relevance:* confirms the single reporting path. FBI staff sitting inside NCMEC means a CyberTipline report reaches federal investigators without any direct-to-police channel, and the Child ID app is a federal precedent for the parent app's on-device posture.

**Take Action** (DHS Know2Protect, page updated 2026-05-20). https://www.dhs.gov/know2protect/take-action Read directly. Parent-facing guidance with age-split conversation advice, a Top 10 Tips2Protect list, and a stage description of grooming and sextortion. Statistics: 40% of kids have a tablet by age 2, nearly 1 in 4 have a personal cellphone by age 8, up to 95% of 13-to-17s use social media and more than a third almost constantly, top 2025 teen apps YouTube, TikTok, Snapchat, Instagram. Tactics named: fake profiles posing as similar-aged peers, trust building via compliments, gifts, e-gift cards and in-game credits, moving to an encrypted platform, then sexual conversation, image exchange and possible meeting, with the whole sequence able to "happen in one sitting, over minutes or hours." Sextortion payment rails: prepaid cards, peer-to-peer apps, cryptocurrency. Evidence instruction: "do not alter, change or delete." Reporting: the Know2Protect tipline 1-833-591-KNOW (5669) and report.cybertip.org. Downloads include a Family Online Safety Agreement, an Internet Safety Checklist and a Build T.R.U.S.T. guide. Message from Secretary Markwayne Mullin. *Relevance:* confirms the parent-surface choices and maps directly onto the migration-ask, economic-bait and payment-demand signals. The "minutes or hours" note supports fast-window trajectory scoring rather than multi-day windows alone.

**Parents, Caregivers, and Teachers: Protecting Your Kids** (FBI). https://www.fbi.gov/how-we-can-help-you/parents-and-caregivers-protecting-your-kids Coverage only: direct fetch returned 403, archive mirrors and r.jina.ai were blocked, and the browser pane hit a Cloudflare interstitial that was left alone. Assembled from search snippets of the page and sibling fbi.gov pages. The framing is that FBI child cases most often begin either with an adult building an online relationship and later arranging to meet and abuse, or with coercing a child into producing sexual images through manipulation, gifts or threats. Linked material: Safe Online Surfing for grades 3-8, the Child ID app, Chasing the Dragon (with DEA), and an IC3 PSA on violent online groups (676, 764, CVLT, Court, Kaskar, Harm Nation, Leak Society, H3ll) targeting minors aged 8 to 17 to extort self-harm and CSAM. Sibling FBI pages give 7,000+ financial sextortion reports and 3,000+ victims in one year, primarily boys, with more than a dozen suicides, and a 20% rise in minor financial sextortion reports in the six months to March 2023. Reporting: 1-800-CALL-FBI, tips.fbi.gov. *Relevance:* confirms the two endings in the threat model and adds the 764-style violent-group pattern, which needs its own signal (self-harm coercion, cult references) beyond stages 1-6.

**DHS Know2Protect Campaign, Tim Tebow Foundation Launch "No Time to Waste" PSA** (DHS, 2026-06-16). https://www.dhs.gov/news/2026/06/16/dhs-know2protect-campaign-tim-tebow-foundation-launch-no-time-waste-psa-urging Read directly. Announces a PSA fronting Pledge2Protect. Cites NCMEC's 2025 volume at 21.3 million reports containing 61.8 million images, videos and files. Operation Renewed Hope IV, held in February, brought together 34 agencies from 29 countries and produced more than 500 victim-identification referrals and more than 200 positive identifications; more than 850 children have been identified, located and rescued since ORH began in 2023. Five online safety steps are hosted at know2protect.gov. *Relevance:* confirms the DESIGN section 1 scale figures and adds the 61.8M file count and ORH IV outcomes. Reinforces that triage, not detection, is the federal bottleneck.

**Mike Waltz, Tim Tebow launch effort to combat online child exploitation** (Fox News Digital, Jasmine Baehr, 2026-07-18). https://www.foxnews.com/politics/mike-waltz-tim-tebow-launch-effort-combat-online-child-exploitation-happening-their-backyard Read directly. A briefing at the US Mission to the UN on 2026-07-18, anchored on a US-led UN resolution targeting online sextortion, recent international operations and new congressional funding, none of which the piece numbers. Waltz names the gap abroad as missing laws, missing extradition treaties, and untrained prosecutors and investigators. Tebow's framing is that it is happening in every family's backyard and that leaving a child online unprotected is "similar to dropping them off at a playground knowing that pedophiles are circling." The Tim Tebow Foundation was founded in 2010. *Relevance:* policy tailwind for sextortion detection and support for the parent app's locally framed, parent-in-the-loop posture. Evidence bundles headed for international referral should carry jurisdiction metadata, since extradition and prosecutorial capacity are the named bottleneck.

**Tim Tebow helps advance bill for DHS to stop child sex abuse** (WORLD News Group, The Sift, Liz Lykins, 2026-05-20). https://wng.org/sift/tim-tebow-backed-bill-fighting-child-sex-abuse-advances-in-senate-1779282760 WebFetch returned 403 and archives were blocked; a curl with a browser user-agent returned the full text. Corroborated with Heartlander, WJLA, Fox News and TTF, marked as coverage. A child-exploitation provision advanced out of a Senate committee on 2026-05-19 inside the GOP DHS reconciliation bill (the Secure America Act); Heartlander names the committee as Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs and the vote as 8-5 with 57 amendments. The provision allocates $108.5 million to hire 200 child-exploitation investigators and forensic analysts at DHS, against a current staff of seven per Sen. Hawley; Heartlander breaks the 200 into 40 forensic analysts, 30 Victim Identification Lab investigators and 130 additional analysts and investigators, while WJLA gives 10 current C3 victim-ID specialists and a proposed 80. Rep. Laurel Lee introduced it in the House in January 2026. Tebow testified to Hawley's Judiciary subcommittee in March 2026 citing DOJ data of 338,000 unique IP addresses sharing CSAM within six months and as many as 89,000 unidentified child victims, with only a handful of cases investigated. Coverage says the Act stalled in late May and the $108.5M passed the Senate June 5 and the House June 9 2026. *Relevance:* pins the Renewed Hope numbers with a date and confirms federal money is going to victim-ID capacity, not platform detection. The 7-versus-10 discrepancy is why DESIGN's "7 to 10" hedge is correct.

**20 Ways You Can Help Fight Human Trafficking** (US State Department, Office to Monitor and Combat Trafficking in Persons). https://www.state.gov/20-ways-you-can-help-fight-human-trafficking WebFetch returned 403 and archives were blocked; the page loaded in the browser pane and was read via get_page_text. Twenty actions for individuals and groups. Reporting is split by urgency: the 24-hour National Human Trafficking Hotline at 1-888-373-7888 for suspected victims, 911 for emergencies. Victims are eligible for services and immigration assistance regardless of citizenship. Consumer resources are ResponsibleSourcingTool.org, the DOL List of Goods Produced by Child Labor or Forced Labor, and Comply Chain. Hashtags #endtrafficking and #freedomfirst. Role-specific guidance covers parents (learn how traffickers target and recruit youth), youth (learn recruitment tactics and how to exit a suspicious situation safely), schools (develop identification and reporting protocols), health care, journalists and attorneys. A related article on the page is dated 2019-04-24. *Relevance:* confirms the official reporting lanes to surface alongside the CyberTipline for non-platform reporters, and the audience-segmented structure maps to per-surface messaging.

**Ten Things You Can Do To Help End Human Trafficking** (HHS Office on Trafficking in Persons, current as of 2026-06-08). https://acf.gov/otip/about/ways-endtrafficking WebFetch returned 403; a curl with a browser user-agent returned the page. Ten general-audience actions. The National Human Trafficking Hotline is 1-888-373-7888, text HELP to 233733 (BEFREE), or chat at humantraffickinghotline.org/chat, free, confidential, 24/7. Traffickers are described as targeting people in a difficult situation, lacking resources, or missing important social connections; protective factors named are safe housing, health and mental health services, employment, childcare and education. Programs referenced: SOAR to Health and Wellness, NHTTAC, the Look Beneath the Surface campaign. Suggested actions include a human trafficking school safety protocol and hiring people who have experienced trafficking. *Relevance:* federal guidance for laypeople routes everything through a hotline and trained professionals, never confrontation, which supports the single reporting path. Adds the hotline strings for trafficking-pattern bundles.

**Stop Human Trafficking Donation Service** (Texas HHSC via the Texas.gov fee-payment portal, copyright 2026). https://feepay.txapps.texas.gov/hhsc/stop-human-trafficking-donation-service/ Read directly. A state donation page funding trauma support, wraparound services, secure shelter and treatment for youth survivors through the Human Trafficking Resource Center. Contact 512-206-4544, HTRC_Finance@hhsc.state.tx.us. Defines trafficking as exploitation of women, men and children for commercial sex or labor against their will. No tactics, statistics or investigative process. *Relevance:* no bearing on detection. Useful only as an example of the state victim-services layer that sits downstream of a report, and as a reminder that survivor-centered framing is the register public bodies use.

### NGOs and campaigns

**Investigator Shares 6 Steps Predators Take to Groom Kids Online** (Child Rescue Coalition, Boca Raton; page references a 2020 award, so 2020 or later). https://childrescuecoalition.org/educations/investigator-shares-6-steps-predators-take-to-groom-kids-online/ Coverage only: WebFetch 403, archive.org and archive.ph blocked, and the browser pane hit a Cloudflare verification interstitial that was left alone. Reconstructed from search snippets of the page and secondary coverage (incacs.org, ksat.com). Jay Houston, CRC Director of Training and Mississippi ICAC Task Force Commander, CRC 2020 Instructor of the Year, lays out six steps: contact via a fake account posing as a child, typically in DMs on Instagram, Facebook Messenger or Snapchat; general rapport; a move to private messaging with probing of the child's personal life and how involved their caretakers are; suggestive and sexual questions about relationships and experience; a soft image ask ("I sure would like to see a picture of you!") escalating to explicit images; then sexual videos, where sexploitation begins, plus arranging meeting locations. Snippets number the steps inconsistently, so the exact ordering is unverified. CRC's Child Protection System is free to over 12,000 law-enforcement investigators in over 90 countries. *Relevance:* this is the source of DESIGN section 3's stage taxonomy, so the sourcing caveat matters. CRC splits images and videos into separate final steps and puts the DM move at the very start, whereas Guardian treats migration as stage 4 and folds coercion into stage 6. The order weights are a Guardian design choice, not something this page dictates, and nothing here should be quoted verbatim without a manual check.

**We are fighting against human trafficking** (Tim Tebow Foundation, anti-trafficking ministry overview). https://timtebowfoundation.org/ministries/anti-human-trafficking-1 Read directly. Three pillars: prevention, rescue (funding partners who run physical rescue missions), and survivor care. Figures attributed to ILO, Walk Free and IOM: almost one in every 150 people in the world is trafficked, and trafficking rose by nearly 10 million people in five years per a 2022 report. NCMEC is cited for nearly 85 million images and videos of child sexual abuse received in 2021. The ministry is active in more than 12 countries. Linked stories cover Operation Renewed Hope (hundreds of victims), Renewed Hope II ("In Pursuit of the 50,000+ Unidentified Children"), a 2025 Bangkok operation with Children's Shield of Thailand (50 children, 70 offenders) and the Renewed Hope Act of 2024. EIN 27-4345913. *Relevance:* confirms TTF as a funder of victim-identification partners rather than a detection or vigilante operator, which is the posture the investigator surface serves. The 50,000+ backlog is the triage bottleneck DESIGN section 1 already cites.

**Anti-Human Trafficking & Child Exploitation Stories** (Tim Tebow Foundation story index; newest cards reference the Renewed Hope Act of 2026, one card dated February 2025). https://timtebowfoundation.org/stories/anti-human-trafficking Read directly. Twelve story cards across three activity types. Renewed Hope Act advocacy: a provision funding 200 new victim-ID investigators, with the backlog cited as more than 89,000 image series for the 2026 act against 50,000 for the 2024 act. US missing-child operations with the National Child Protection Task Force: 25 children located in the first Northeast Florida operation, 71 in New York's Capital Region, 47 in Erie County across 22 law-enforcement agencies with the state DCJS. Operation Renewed Hope III with ICE and international partners tentatively identified 386 victims. The February 2025 Bangkok operation identified 50 children and 70 offenders; a global live-stream operation generated leads to 197 buyers with 32 investigators. Overseas survivor housing: 10 boys at Compassionate Hope Tebow Village in the Philippines, 24 survivors at the TTF Protection Center in Thailand, a second Tebow Okoa Philippi campus in Uganda. *Relevance:* confirms the growth of the unidentified-victim backlog from 50,000 to 89,000+ in two years and that rescue work runs through law enforcement and vetted task forces. Survivor identity is at least as sensitive as offender data in any triage UI.

**Disrupt: Fighting Human Trafficking in the USA** (Tim Tebow Foundation; safe-home figures as of Spring 2025). https://timtebowfoundation.org/disrupt Read directly. A $3M US campaign framed as "Defend the Vulnerable and Disrupt the Demand," with three lines: technology that scans newly posted online sex ads to find exploited children, undercover sting operations against buyers of sex with minors run with law enforcement, and survivor aftercare through 46 safe homes globally including 9 in the US with 22 in development, plus the Her Song ministry. Statistics: 2.6 videos or images of child abuse shared every second, over 300,000 sex ads posted online daily, close to a third of all trafficking happening within families. A Shared Hope International scorecard grades states from 31% (Alaska) to 90% (South Dakota, Tennessee) across six assessment areas. Recruitment pathways listed: "Romeo" boyfriends, family members, addiction or homelessness, social media and online games, messaging apps, foster care, disabilities, false job offers, gangs. Monthly giving at $19, $40, $75, $250. The page says it withholds rescue photos and operational detail to protect survivors and investigations, and offers no hotline or reporting path. EIN 27-4345913. *Relevance:* confirms that roughly one third of trafficking is familial, so parent-side detection alone misses a large share, and that the downstream marker of recruitment is the victim surfacing in an online sex ad. Also confirms that even a law-enforcement-partnered nonprofit runs undercover buyer stings, which Guardian does not.

**Our Rescue** (formerly Operation Underground Railroad, 2026). https://ourrescue.org/ Coverage only: the homepage and every subpage returned a Cloudflare 403 to WebFetch and to curl with a browser UA, and archive mirrors were blocked. Assembled from the organization's PR Newswire releases, Wikipedia and August 2026 press. Founded October 2013 by Tim Ballard, who resigned in 2023 after an internal investigation and sexual misconduct allegations; now Minneapolis-headquartered under CEO Derek Benner (April 2025, former ICE/HSI). March 4 2026 it hired Jim Cole, founder of HSI's Victim Identification Program, with ~37 years of law-enforcement experience. ICAC Connect launched June 23 2025 to supply tools, forensics, training scholarships and rapid grants to all 61 ICAC units, citing that 90% of CSAM cases originate in cyber tips, that Arlington TX receives up to 100 tips a month, and that units run 50+ cases a month over capacity. The Interstate Justice Coalition formed July 28 2025 with Skull Games Solutions and We Fight Monsters; its first surge (Aug 21-22 2025, MS, TN, OK, AR, 16 agencies) identified 32 victims, recovered 26, and made 15 arrests using OSINT and DataPilot forensics. August 7 2026 it received a sole-source HHS contract with a $244,034,658 ceiling and $158M obligated through Feb 2028, roughly seven times its $36.9M asset base, drawing heavy criticism. Revenue history: $6.9M (2016), $22.3M (2019), $45M+ (2020), $50M (2023). DOJ warned ICAC task forces against partnering with it in February 2016; Vice reported in 2020 and 2021 that no rescues were independently verified, and Anne Gallagher called the operations "arrogant, unethical and illegal." Ballard's 2022 compensation was $546,548; Benner's is $413,387. *Relevance:* the archetype of the raid-and-rescue posture Guardian's constraints reject, and a reputational-risk counterexample rather than a partner model. The useful signal is ICAC Connect's own framing that units are drowning in cyber tips at 100 a month.

**How To Help Stop Child Trafficking** (UNICEF USA). https://www.unicefusa.org/what-unicef-does/childrens-protection/end-child-trafficking WebFetch returned 403 and archives were unreachable; the page loaded in the browser pane and was read via get_page_text. Traffickers generate approximately $39 billion annually from the exploitation of children alone; over 150 million children are in child labor with nearly half in hazardous work; one in four victims of modern slavery are children, mostly girls; almost one third of all detected trafficking victims are children. Quotes UNODC's Yury Fedotov on armed groups using trafficking to spread fear and supply fighters. Regional patterns named: Rohingya refugees in Bangladesh, Central American migrants bound for the US, women and girls in Europe, sub-Saharan Africans and Middle East refugees. The only actionable block is a three-channel contact set: call 1-888-373-7888, text 233733, web chat, plus NCMEC at 1-800-THE-LOST (843-5678) and the CyberTipline. The page hedges that rising numbers may partly reflect better detection and reporting. *Relevance:* confirms the single reporting path from a third direction and adds the NHTH as a resource line in trafficking-pattern bundles. The detection-versus-reality hedge is the caveat Guardian's dashboards need when alert counts rise after a version change.

**How Can I Protect My Child from Trafficking?** (Safe House Project, Elizabeth Fields, 2025-08-29). https://www.safehouseproject.org/blog/how-can-i-protect-my-child-from-trafficking/ Read directly. Risk factors: unstable home, prior abuse, isolation, unsupervised online time, LGBTQ+ identity, foster care or juvenile justice involvement, disabilities. Three trafficker tactics: grooming (trust, need identification, then control and secrecy), online relationships where the trafficker poses as a peer and escalates to explicit imagery or sextortion, and coercion or isolation. Age-banded conversation guidance for 3-5, 6-9, 10-13 and 14-18. Online safety practice is explicit that monitoring tools should be used collaboratively with child input and devices kept in shared spaces. Reporting: law enforcement or child protective services, the Simply Report app for anonymous tips, and the organization's hotline 1-833-5-BESAFE (survivor support 7 AM to 7 PM EST daily). *Relevance:* the direct basis for the overt, kid-visible parent posture, and the trust-needs-control-secrecy arc matches the stage trajectory. The sextortion-after-imagery pattern supports the critical-signal rule that forces tier T2 or above.

**Human Trafficking Training** (Polaris Project). https://polarisproject.org/training/ Read directly. A free, self-paced online course, Human Trafficking 101, in English and Spanish (Capacitación sobre Trata), with six modules: defining trafficking, how it happens, understanding the victims, how traffickers operate, recognizing trafficking, and what to do next. Video modules, case studies on both sex and labor trafficking with quizzes, and survivor stories. Corporations can adapt it via corporateengagement@polarisproject.org. Survivor Jennifer Lyons is quoted: "I didn't know what sex trafficking was." Polaris frames recognition as "knowing the story behind a situation rather than just knowing the signs." The page also promotes Ruth, a trauma-informed AI chatbot. No statistics or hotline numbers appear. *Relevance:* the story-not-signs framing is external support for emitting contextual evidence bundles and pair trajectories rather than sign-based labels, and it argues for a short educational layer plus an explicit next step on the parent and moderator surfaces, in Spanish as well as English.

### Vigilante communities

**PredCord** (Discord server invite landing page, undated, invite does not expire; fetched 2026-09-03). https://discord.com/invite/J95xTTx The invite page itself shows only the server name; the public invite API at discord.com/api/v10/invites/J95xTTx with_counts=true returned guild metadata, and two search passes added thin coverage. Approximately 1,569 members with 167 online, no description set, verification level 1, NSFW level 0, zero boosts, Community and Discoverable features enabled, invite pointing at a channel called "live," created by a user named Preddann. A server-directory snippet describes it only as a community "dedicated to discussions related to predator catching" with about 1,552 members. Nothing on the page or in coverage documents its methods, rules or outcomes. *Relevance:* confirms a live, discoverable, roughly 1,500-member vigilante community organized around a public "live" channel. Guardian's mod-channel alerts must be the opposite: private to the operator's mod team, phrased as risk tiers with evidence, never as a catch.

**Predator Catching | Catching, Watching and Exposing** (Discord public server directory listing; server created 2025-04-07). https://discord.com/servers/predator-catching-catching-watching-and-exposing-1358800547322396702 Read directly. 3,334 members with 215 online at fetch time, categorized under "Other," English, styled "Like a chill coffee shop." It calls itself the largest predator catching server on Discord, says it stops sextorters, takes down "pedophile servers," helps beginner predator catchers get started, and runs channels for "Predator catches, Predator fights, Amateur catches," plus report channels for anybody who needs help with a predator. No owner, rules or verification level are exposed. *Relevance:* confirms that sizable communities operate on the decoy, expose and fight posture DESIGN sections 2 and 9 reject, and that there is real demand on Discord for sextortion help, which supports the Discord-first build order. Also flags that catcher role-play traffic exists and may need handling in the teen-romance and base-rate controls.

---

## 2. Landscape

Eight areas. Each has a table (name, what it does, who it serves, status in 2026, relevance to Guardian) followed by the themes that fall out of it.

### 2.1 Platform trust and safety

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Safer by Thorn (Safer Match, Safer Predict, Safer Enterprise) | Hash matching against 172.5M+ verified CSAM hashes (SaferHash images, SSVH video), a CSAM image classifier, and a CSE text classifier emitting per-message labels with confidence: CSA discussion, child access, CSAM, has minor, self-generated, sextortion, not pertinent, plus a grooming label (sub-indicators: planning offline meetings, encouraging self-harm or emotional dependency, romantic or sexual relationship with a minor, soliciting personal info or images, moving to less moderated platforms). Enterprise is self-hosted with a moderation UI, wellness features and NCMEC/RCMP reporting. 2025: 415.4B files and 318.59M lines of text processed, 1.3M lines flagged, 5.3M potential CSAM files, 80+ platforms | Content-hosting and messaging platforms: Shopify, Bumble, Niantic, OpenAI, Giphy, Quora, MediaLab, Vimeo, Patreon, Slack, Bluesky, Stability AI | Alive. Text classifier 2024, grooming label 2025-11-12, resold via Hive 2026-04-07 | Closest competitor at the platform SDK layer and simultaneously an input, since DESIGN already assumes the customer runs Safer or PhotoDNA. Its label list is the best public reference for a stage taxonomy; its "stack labels to accounts" advice validates actor skew without implementing trajectory |
| Hive Moderation (CSAM Detection API + CSE Text Classifier, powered by Thorn) | Resells Thorn's stack. Combined CSAM API runs hash matching (returns matchDistance) plus a classifier returning pornography/csam/other summing to 1, media deleted after processing. Text classifier returns Thorn's seven labels in a pertinent_labels block above an internal threshold. English and Spanish | Hive's enterprise moderation customers: marketplaces, social, dating, genAI apps | Alive. Thorn partnership April 2024, expanded Nov 2024 and April 2026 | Competitor for the per-message classifier and a possible input. Pricing shows where the line sits: commodity moderation $0.50 per 1,000 text calls, child safety contact-sales |
| ActiveFence (operating as Alice) | activefence.com now 301s to alice.io. Sells WonderSuite for GenAI safety: WonderBuild red-teaming, WonderFence runtime guardrails, WonderCheck drift detection, plus a "Rabbit Hole" corpus of billions of abusive samples. Legacy UGC moderation (ActiveOS/ActiveScore) and Spectrum Labs' behavior library are no longer described publicly | Frontier AI labs and large platforms: Google, Anthropic, Meta, TikTok, AWS, NVIDIA, Amazon, Cohere, Udemy | Alive, rebranded; acquired Spectrum Labs Sept 2023; footer still reads ©2026 ActiveFence | Former direct competitor that has left the mid-market gaming-chat lane. Player Risk Categories (child grooming, violent radicalization) shipped GA 2023-08-21 and score repeated patterns over time | Games and social VR (Activision Call of Duty, Rec Room); now contact centers | Alive; $30M raised; public pricing | Adjacent competitor and possible partner. Guardian has no voice path. Its user-report correlation (join player reports to model flags before human review) is worth copying |

Delivered as a managed service | Social, gaming and generative AI trust-and-safety teams; brands | Acquired by Kroll May 2022; Crisp brand retired into Resolver in 2024 | Precedent and possible partner for the investigator surface. Not something a small Discord owner can buy |
| Microsoft Community Sift (formerly Two Hat) | Text, image, video and username moderation across 22 languages with per-language models, "Unnatural Language Processing" for leetspeak and Unicode evasion, topics (cyberbullying, hate, grooming, threats, self-harm) scored on a risk scale, rules combining account age, country and signup method, moderator dashboard plus webhooks, purpose-built grooming models. A 2024 generative-AI experiment detected conversations transitioning positive to negative over time, with FBI, NCMEC and Crisis Text Line partnerships; it never shipped | Xbox, Electronic Arts, Supercell, Kabam, Flip; 100B+ interactions a month | Reported by two competing vendors as being phased out in 2026, with Microsoft honoring existing contracts and not renewing. Not confirmed by Microsoft: the product page is live with no retirement notice and it appears on no Microsoft Lifecycle end-of-support list | The de facto standard for kids' games for a decade, with the only documented vendor attempt at conversation-trajectory detection. | Bodyguard.ai | Text (45+ languages), image (VLM plus human), video and audience-intelligence moderation for comments and chat; hate, harassment, fraud, toxicity; sub-100ms | Media, sports and social: Yubo, France TV, M6, Real Madrid, Juventus, GetStream | Alive | Irrelevant as a competitor. Yubo as a customer shows platforms buying toxicity moderation still lack grooming coverage |
| Utopia Analytics | Fully automated moderation trained per customer on that customer's own historical moderation decisions; language-agnostic; profanity, bullying, fraud, spam, sexual harassment, discrimination, emails, phone numbers, unacceptable links; two-week onboarding | Marketplaces and news comment sections (Adevinta's Jofogas.hu, Iltalehti) | Alive | Irrelevant, except that per-customer fine-tuning on the customer's own review outcomes is a selling point Guardian's fusion model can also claim |
| Sightengine | Self-serve image, video, live-stream, text and audio moderation API plus AI-image, deepfake and age-estimation models. Not a competitor |

Another data point that generalist moderation startups are exiting |
| Checkstep | Moderation platform: no-code policy management, ModBot automation with human queues, text/image/video/audio scanning, incident and review analytics, and a DSA transparency-report generator. No child-safety, grooming or NCMEC features described | Instant Gaming, Star Stable (a kids' horse game), Trustpilot, Build a Rocket Boy, Daily Mail, JustGiving | Alive | Weak competitor for the review surface at EU-regulated mid-size platforms; possible integration target |
| Roblox Sentinel, PII Classifier v2, Voice Safety Classifier v3 (via ROOST Model Community) | Released 2026-08-19. Sentinel (Apache-2.0): contrastive rare-class detection, each text embedded (default all-MiniLM-L6-v2) and scored as a ratio of similarity to positive versus negative exemplar indices, with recent per-source scores aggregated by skewness (default) or top_k_mean, percentile_score, softmax_weighted_mean, max_score; outputs rare_class_affinity_score, per-observation scores and explanations with nearest exemplar snippets; simulation tooling sweeps 324 configs in under 3 minutes; ROC-AUC 0.894 to 0.996 with optimal config. In production Roblox samples chat in one-minute snapshots, tracks per-user skew, routes to analysts, and filed ~1,200 NCMEC reports via Sentinel in H1 2025 (35% of detected cases), rising to nearly 70% of cases in the 12 months to 2026-08-07. PII v2: XLM-RoBERTa-large 0.6B, multi-turn input with the target speaker tagged t, labels privacy_asking_for_pii (0.60), privacy_giving_pii (0.55), directing_users_off_platform (0.10), 189 languages, F1 88.82% public / 90.52% internal (v1 63.41). Voice v3: 320M params, 30 languages, 8 categories, 61% recall at 1% FPR | Any platform with an ML engineer; the ROOST community | Alive | Input and precedent, already assumed by DESIGN. The per-pair trajectory is the layer Sentinel does not have, and no exemplars ship, so index quality is the adopter's cost |
| Discord AutoMod and teen safety | AutoMod is a server-level per-message rules engine: KEYWORD (max 6 rules per guild, 1,000 keywords of 60 chars, 10 regexes of 260 chars), SPAM, KEYWORD_PRESET (profanity, sexual content, slurs), MENTION_SPAM (up to 50), MEMBER_PROFILE; actions BLOCK_MESSAGE, SEND_ALERT_MESSAGE, TIMEOUT, BLOCK_MEMBER_INTERACTION; guild messages only, never DMs. Teen Safety Assist (Nov 2023, designed with Thorn) alerts a teen on a first-time DM from a flagged sender and blurs or blocks sensitive media. Teen-by-default globally March 2026; age assurance via k-ID in Australia, a UK Persona experiment terminated after backlash, AI age inference. Discord acquired Sentropy July 2021 and open-sourced Osprey through ROOST; it is a Lantern member | Server owners and teen users | Alive | The Discord bot's operating environment. Guardian's alert should look like an AutoMod card; the bot should not duplicate keyword filtering AutoMod already does. DM-request detection in public channels is the complementary gap |
| Cyacomb (Forensics, Safety) | Block-level Contraband Filter hashing that matches known CSAM without being reversible to the source set. Forensics: device triage for police, IWF 1.7M-hash filter, Project VIC and Interpol filters, UK CAID national mobile rollout in 2026. Safety: filter at upload, privacy-assured matching, secure reporting where legislation requires | Law enforcement primarily; platforms via Cyacomb Safety | Alive | Input option for the operator-side hash verdict and a precedent for shipping hash sets to untrusted edges. Images only |
| Tech Coalition Lantern (and Pathways) | Cross-platform signal sharing for online child sexual exploitation and abuse. Content signals: CSAM hashes, URLs, offender keywords and codewords. Members assess signals themselves. 2,047,982 signals and 353,806 enforcement actions through 2025 (164,575 accounts, 163,112 URLs, 26,119 content). 21 participants including Block, Discord, Dropbox, Google, MediaLab, MEGA, Meta, Microsoft, Niantic, Photobucket, Quora, Reddit, Roblox, Scribd, Snap, Twitch, Western Union, X, Yahoo, Yubo, Zoom. A financial pilot (Aug 2024 to H1 2025: Cash App, Mega, Meta, PayPal, Snap, Western Union) shared 1,935 signals and triggered 108 investigations. Pathways is the free on-ramp for smaller UGC companies, 80 participants in 2025, with a free PhotoDNA sublicense | Tech companies and, since 2025, US financial institutions. NGOs, governments, law enforcement and vendors are ineligible | Alive; 2026 priorities are APAC expansion and financial-sector integration | The standard to design toward. Signal types map almost one to one onto stage 4 and stage 6. Guardian cannot join as a vendor, which matches the opt-in cross-customer design |

Founders Dave Willner and Samidh Chakrabarti | Character.AI, Runway, Stanford, Koko, Cantina | Alive; free tier plus open weights | Input candidate: a CoPE labeler per stage could bootstrap the stage classifier before fine-tuning, and could mine new lexicon tokens from T2 cases |

| Clavata (now Moonbounce) | Policy engine for AI apps evaluating every prompt, output and tool call with allow / block / rewrite / escalate / label actions; 60+ prebuilt policies; customer Janitor AI at 20M decisions a day | LLM application builders | Alive; rebranded | Irrelevant except as a companion-AI precedent |
| OpenAI Moderation API (omni-moderation-latest) | Free multimodal endpoint returning flagged, per-category booleans and 0-1 scores including sexual/minors (text only). Used by ROOST Coop as its grooming and solicitation text signal | Any developer | Alive; free | Input candidate for a stage-5 prior and a cheap eval baseline. Single input, no stages, no trajectory |
| Yoti | Facial age estimation and document verification; more than a billion age checks. Minecraft (UK, from Feb 2026) gates chat and social features on Yoti checks; Fortnite since 2023; Yubo | Games, social, adult sites, retailers | Alive | Input: a source of age bands. Play and purchase stay open, only social features are gated, which is the pattern Guardian's bands should assume |
| k-ID | Age assurance and youth-compliance infrastructure: AgeKit (free age classification), AgeKit+ (privacy-preserving verification), AgeKey (reusable credential), Family Connect (portfolio-level parental consent, "up to 96% parent consent rates"), a Compliance Development Kit tracking 200+ jurisdictions with auto-updating feature gates. Discord's Australian vendor | Discord, Bandai Namco, Konami, Capcom, Quora; 100M+ users | Alive | Partner and input: the obvious age-band source for the platform SDK and a consent primitive for the parent app. Verify-at-point-of-use is the pattern to copy |
| Persona | Facial age estimation behind Roblox's global chat gate (live Jan 2026) with six bands from under 9 to 21+; users chat only with their band and younger. Reported MAE 1.4 years under 18 per the UK ACCS; 338M age-check records. Discord ran a UK experiment that was terminated after backlash | Roblox, Discord (UK test), identity-verification customers | Alive | Input and a caution: account resale and estimation errors mean bands are a prior, so the age-gap multiplier needs a confidence weight |
| NCMEC CyberTipline ESP Reporting API | RESTful XML API for registered ESPs: POST /submit opens a report, /upload attaches files, /fileinfo adds metadata, /finish commits, /retract cancels; HTTPS with NCMEC-issued credentials; test host exttest.cybertip.org. Incident types include online enticement, sextortion, child sex trafficking and tourism, unsolicited obscene material to minors. Docs last updated 2026-08-26 with batched viral report validation, generative-AI annotations and device-ID capture | Providers under 18 USC 2258A | Alive; free, credentials only by request | The standard Guardian's report service implements. Guardian files as the customer's agent, so the credential belongs to the customer |
| Stanford Internet Observatory, "The Strengths and Weaknesses of the Online Child Safety Ecosystem" (2024) | 66 interviews with law enforcement, NCMEC staff, platform employees, prosecutors and defense attorneys. Finds near-identical reports leading to opposite outcomes, incomplete platform reports, NCMEC unable to modernize, and legal constraints binding both NCMEC and police | Policymakers, platforms, vendors | Published April 2024; cited by ROOST's 2026 roadmap | Precedent for the evidence-bundle emphasis and the investigator triage surface |

**Themes.**

Everyone classifies the unit they receive, a message, a file or an utterance, and nobody sells per-pair progression. Roblox Sentinel is the only public system scoring an actor's distribution over time; Thorn's label stacking, Modulate's escalation patterns, and Community Sift's unshipped 2024 conversation-transition experiment are the nearest commercial gestures.

Media detection is a free commodity and text is where the money and the gaps are. Four free or open hash and classifier sources (PhotoDNA, Arachnid Shield, Google Content Safety and CSAI Match, HMA plus NCMEC hash lists) mean nobody should pay for known-CSAM matching. Paid differentiation is text: Thorn's grooming label resold via Hive, Lasso's "grooming lines," Stream's "platform circumvention." All per-message, all English and Spanish heavy.

CyberTipline reporting is now a feature every serious vendor ships (Thorn Enterprise, Cinder, Lasso, ROOST Coop) but report quality is unsolved. Stanford says identical-looking reports diverge, ROOST cites 8% incomplete submissions in 2024, and the ESP API is still XML with manual credentialing. An evidence bundle with a timeline, stage annotations, model versions and hash-chained custody is the open lane, not the submission itself.

The money is enterprise contact-sales at the top and self-serve per-op pricing at the commodity bottom. Nothing is priced for a 40-person Discord server or a 50k-MAU indie game, which is the segment DESIGN targets.

The generalist vendor cohort is leaving or consolidating: Spectrum Labs into ActiveFence into Alice, Intrinsic into Variance, Unitary into workflow agents, Clavata into Moonbounce, Crisp into Kroll into Resolver, Community Sift reportedly sunsetting, WebPurify sold to a BPO. Mid-market gaming chat, Community Sift's old base, is being caught by chat-infrastructure vendors whose moderation is keyword plus LLM per message.

Open source is the new floor. ROOST lets a small platform assemble hash matching, a reviewer console with wellness defaults, and a CyberTipline path for free. What is not free: labeled grooming exemplars, calibrated thresholds at real base rates, and a per-pair trajectory model.

Practitioner complaints cluster on four things: classifier noise from spam and roleplay, weeks of relabeling when policy changes, missing operational tooling (a live false-negative log, a sandbox for rule changes, filter logs sliced by game and language), and age-assurance backlash.

Age bands became platform infrastructure in 2026 (Roblox six bands via Persona in January, Discord teen-by-default in March, Minecraft via Yoti in February), and cross-platform behavioral signal sharing exists but is closed to vendors. Guardian consumes bands and emits Lantern-shaped signals for customers to forward.

UI patterns worth stealing: Coop's case card, Lasso's queue row, Cinder's action vocabulary, AutoMod's alert card, Sentinel's nearest-exemplar explanations, Modulate's user-report correlation. Patterns to avoid: binary flags without a precision dial, thresholds hidden inside the vendor, and any UI string that names a person rather than a risk tier.

Alerts carry a category, severity, snippet, source app and recommended next steps. Bark for Schools covers 3,700+ districts and scanned ~11B messages from ~7.5M US children in 2025 | US parents of tweens and teens; K-12 districts | Alive; ~$67M raised, last round $30M June 2022 | Closest competitor and the primary precedent for the parent surface: overt, snippet plus category, no full transcript. Its false-positive record is the base-rate warning made real (a parent quoted by MIT Technology Review: "Ninety-nine percent of the alerts are garbage") |

The parent app and iOS SDK customers can source the age band from the OS instead of asking |

Sideloaded apps hid their presence, three sent sensitive data unencrypted, half had no privacy policy, and 8 of 20 matched stalkerware indicators of compromise | Regulators, app-store policy, researchers | Published 2025 | Input and legal ammunition for the overt-only rule. Their indicator list is a checklist Guardian's Android app should fail on purpose |
| Pamela Wisniewski / MOSafely | Research program arguing for resilience over restriction, releasing open-source risk-detection algorithms and testing joint parent-teen oversight where the child sees the same dashboard. Findings: parental controls associated with more, not less, risk exposure; kids' app reviews 76% one-star; ~1 in 7 kids grateful, ~1 in 5 feel privacy-stripped, ~1 in 10 describe broken trust | Product designers, policy, academia | Active | The strongest evidence base for the kid-visible, summary-first design and the reason the parent app is last |

**Themes.**

Everyone ships the same alert atom: category chip, severity, quoted snippet, source app, timestamp, delivered by push, email and SMS, with a parent-side severe-only filter as the main noise control. The differentiators are only whether a full transcript sits behind the snippet and what the category is called. Nobody ships a per-alert "this was wrong" feedback loop that visibly retrains anything.

The market has two poles on transcripts and Guardian's posture sits in a gap. Metadata only: Apple, Google, Snap, Instagram. Alert plus snippet: Bark, Gabb, Securly Home, Helmit. Transcript by default or toggle: Qustodio, MMGuardian, Pinwheel, Troomi, BrightCanary, and the screenshot products. Tone-only summary: Aura. Guardian's pattern description with no snippet is closest to Aura's card, which reviewers rate as too vague to act on, so plan a gated excerpt reveal rather than none.

iOS is the wall and every vendor's data path is a workaround: MDM over USB, VPN, a charger that scans on plug-in, a desktop backup scan, custom-keyboard keystroke capture, parent-entered child credentials, or a locked Android phone. Apple's Screen Time API exposes usage, not content, and Communication Safety is on-device image-only and never notifies the parent.

Nobody in the consumer set labels carefully. Bark, MMGuardian, Troomi, Helmit, Kidas and Aura all use predator language in taxonomy or copy. GoGuardian is the exception and the best model: five behavioral phase names with only the top phase forcing notification.

Nobody publishes precision, and false positives are the number-one complaint. Documented examples: "headache, Nurofen" as Drug/Alcohol, basketball "shots" as Violence, unsaved group-chat numbers as Risky Contacts / Critical, a Bible verse containing "naked," song lyrics, college and LGBTQ sites. Content-scanning apps get 48% positive reviews versus 74-78% for location-only apps, and no commercial monitoring app has a controlled trial showing reduced harm.

The money is in bundles and hardware. Standalone apps cluster at $50-$140/yr; growth is in locked kid phones at $25-45/month all-in, in security bundles, and in schools at $2.75-3.90 per student per year with a 24/7 human-review upsell at $8.20. A detection kernel is more valuable embedded (Kidas inside Aura, Bark inside Pinwheel) than as a 21st standalone app.

Overtness is rare in practice and strongly supported by evidence. Only Bark, Qustodio, Canopy, Norton, the OS vendors and the research prototypes treat the child as a participant. A child-visible tier view and a child-side dispute button would be near-unique.

Regulatory and trust events set the floor. The App Store Accountability Acts plus Apple's Declared Age Range API give a free OS-attested four-bracket age band. Life360's data-sale story, Aura's 2026 breach and the Kaspersky US ban show that a parent-app vendor's worst outcomes are data handling and provenance, not detection misses.

### 2.3 Investigator tools

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Magnet Griffeye (Analyze DI Pro, Griffeye Intelligence Database) | Media-centric triage for seized collections: hashes every file against Project VIC, CAID, GID and real-time NCMEC matching, stacks duplicates, pre-categorizes known material, runs the Brain CSA classifier plus Thorn's CSAM classifier and T3K CORE, face recognition, motion-based video filtering. GID shares hash intelligence across teams and countries | Federal, state and local forensic examiners and ICAC units; the de facto standard for CSAM categorization | Alive under Magnet (Griffeye acquired Aug 2023). Axiom 10.0 (2026-04-20) added native GID integration | Partner and precedent. Guardian should emit VICS-compatible JSON and hash lists rather than a competing viewer. Its dedupe-then-review loop is the model for tip triage |
| Magnet Axiom | Full-device acquisition and artifact analysis, processing recovered media against Project VIC hash sets, exporting unknown media to Griffeye and importing categorizations back, and parsing chat artifacts where enticement evidence on a seized phone surfaces | Digital forensics labs; ICAC examiners | Alive; 10.0 released 2026-04-20 | Partner. A Guardian export should be importable next to Axiom's chat artifacts. It does no enticement scoring on the chat it parses |
| Cellebrite (Inseyets, Pathfinder, Guardian, Guardian Investigate) | Inseyets: device extraction with NCMEC's known-CSAM hash database integrated. Guardian: cloud-native evidence management (AWS, CJIS, FedRAMP High) with in-browser review, prosecutor and defense sharing, chain-of-custody, chat summarization and OSINT enrichment. Guardian Investigate: agentic AI answering natural-language questions across case evidence and drafting court narratives | ~7,000 law-enforcement and enterprise customers; ~3M investigations a year | Alive; Guardian Investigate GA 2026 | Partner and precedent. Note the product-name collision. Their agentic nerve center is where LE tooling is heading; Guardian's bundle should be an input to it |
| Spotlight (Canary NGO; formerly Thorn Spotlight) | Ingests online commercial-sex advertising and lets investigators prioritize leads, map relationships among ads, people, phone numbers and locations, and collaborate across jurisdictions | Law-enforcement agencies with statutory authority for child victim identification | Alive under new owner; transferred from Thorn 2024-05-10, led by Kristin Boorse | Competitor and partner for the sex-ad monitor, and the precedent for free-to-verified-LE distribution. July 2026 added context labels (nudity, apparent maturity, sexual content) keyed to the INHOPE Universal Classification Schema v2. Thorn Detect (2025) is a forensic-side classifier for LE; 900+ agencies use Thorn's victim-ID tools | 80+ platforms; LE agencies | Alive. 2025: 415.4B files, 5.3M suspected CSAM files, 318.59M text lines, 1.3M flagged lines | Competitor on the platform SDK; context labels are effectively a tiering layer. STAR (UK) clusters networks | 200+ public-safety agencies on three continents including 30 DHS field offices and the FBI; 5,000+ users; 1.3B records indexed | Alive; CEO testified to House Oversight 2025-12-10 | Competitor and precedent for the investigator ad monitor; potential data partner. Dedupe: 29.4M images submitted, 48% unique; 26.3M videos, 28% unique. Analysts labeled 14.6M files; 12.1M hashes shared with 78 ESPs; 172,000 removal notices at a 2.6-day average takedown. The CMT delivers reports to all US ICACs, FBI, HSI and 170 countries in 8 languages | Registered ESPs, the public, law enforcement | Alive; Grassley oversight letter 2026-03-02 demanded the names of poorly reporting ESPs | The reporting path. The evidence bundle should be designed backward from what LE actually sees in Sections A through C, plus NCMEC's proposed mandatory fields |
| Stanford Internet Observatory 2024 CyberTipline report | 66 interviews. Only ~50% of 2022 reports actionable; 36M reports in 2023, 92% international, 1M in a single day from one meme; platforms omit who, what, where and when, submit hash-only, and use inconsistent IPs and timezones; NCMEC runs a circa-2004 API, cannot use cloud, and cannot hire engineers at nonprofit pay; law enforcement cannot tell which of two identical-looking reports leads to a rescue; international LE gets no content and GDPR blocks IP-to-identity | Policymakers, platforms, ICAC | Report stands; SIO itself was dismantled June 2024 | The requirements document for the investigator triage surface and for what the platform SDK must put in a report |
| NCMEC Take It Down and the TAKE IT DOWN Act | A free, anonymous hash-list service for people who were under 18 in nude or sexual imagery: the image is hashed on-device and never uploaded, and the hash joins a list that 12 participating platforms scan on public surfaces. Its on-device hashing is the exact model for Guardian's media posture |
| CAID (Child Abuse Image Database, UK) | National database of images, videos, hashes and metadata from all UK forces, live since Dec 2014. The Crime and Policing Act 2026 lets Border Force compel device unlock for CAID scanning, with trials yielding intelligence on 70% of examined devices | Precedent only. Its stated design goal, shifting the balance from reviewing images to identifying victims, is the right frame for triage |
| INTERPOL ICSE database | Central image and video comparison database (6.7M items) used by specialized investigators in 75+ countries to connect victims, abusers and places and avoid duplicate work. Nearly 65,200 victims identified to date; of unidentified material, over 60% prepubescent, 84% explicit sexual activity, 65% girls | Vetted national victim-ID units | Alive | Precedent. Series-level dedupe, not file-level, is the first question, and Guardian's tip clustering should be actor or series level too |

Trace an Object publishes cropped background objects from CSAM and takes anonymous public tips: 10,000+ contributions in the first week, ~28,000 tips and 26 children rescued to date | EU member-state law enforcement; the public | Alive | Precedent for incidental-detail extraction and for public tip intake that exposes nothing about a victim |

Shield is a free hash-verdict source; the analyst network is the model for distributed human review, and "human sees only the residual" is a first-class metric |
| DeliverFund (P.A.T.H. on Kaseware, NightWatch, ROC biometrics) | Analyst-driven trafficking intelligence: analysts enter phones, emails and usernames and the system auto-links them across cases, ingesting warrant returns as searchable text. ROC donated its biometric SDK in May 2025. 2026: pre-World Cup training for 200+ officers, an NFL Draft operation flagging 130+ ads, 1,000+ intel reports around Super Bowl 2026 | 8,000+ officers across 1,200 agencies | Alive | Partner and precedent. Identifier-first linking is the pattern the investigator surface needs, though Guardian's salted per-customer IDs mean it only works after an investigator supplies the raw handle |
| Skull Games (Task Force, SGX) | Vetted volunteer OSINT analysts (prior experience or 250+ hours of academy training) in regional teams under Team Leaders with LE liaisons, doing year-round small-team work plus quarterly surges. Each analyst works passive OSINT to establish identity, trafficking indicators, trafficker and location, and reports end with a discovery path so LE can obtain warrants. No contact with subjects, no independent LE notification | LE partners nationwide | Alive; new analyst cohort recruited Mar 2025 | Precedent for the civilian-analyst posture that survives legal review, and a potential analyst pool. Its rules mirror Guardian's never-DM rule |
| OSINT frameworks used by victim-ID analysts | Maltego for link graphs, SpiderFoot for automated correlation across 200+ sources, IntelX for breach and paste search, the Bellingcat toolkit, Authentic8 Silo for attribution-safe browsing; LE consoles (NOVA, Cellebrite Guardian) wrap the same lookups. CEPOL's 2026 victim-ID course lists image analysis, AI and OSINT as core skills | Victim-ID analysts at NGOs, ICAC units, CEPOL trainees | Alive; OSINT market ~$12.7B in 2025 | Input: the tools an investigator will use on Guardian's leads. Guardian should export graph-shaped entity lists. None are purpose-built, and none log query scope |
| Cinder and Hive CyberTipline integrations | Cinder ships a Python SDK covering every NCMEC endpoint, an XSD converted with xsdata, a per-customer metadata form pre-filling constant fields, automatic mapping from chat-message properties to incident time and reported person, and media streamed from the customer CDN to NCMEC in chunks with hashes compared in memory so nothing is stored. Hive puts a Report to NCMEC action inside a CSAM Review Feed that never renders CSAM, with an optional enrichment callback injecting sensitive user data at submit time, three retries, a fileViewedByEsp flag and mandatory escalation reasoning | Mid-size platforms without T&S engineering | Alive | Competitor on the platform SDK's reporting feature and the direct precedent for Guardian's report service |

**Themes.**

Everyone is hash-first and media-centric. Griffeye, Axiom, Cellebrite, CAID, Project VIC, ICSE, Arachnid, Take It Down and NCMEC's own intake all start by resolving known hashes so a human sees only the residual: NCMEC reports 48% of images and 28% of videos unique, Project VIC claims up to 85% pre-classified, CAID cut a case from 3 days to 1 hour. Guardian's hash-only rule is not a handicap; the hash plus VICS JSON is the ecosystem's join key.

Nobody triages enticement. Every LE tool is either seized-media or ad-side. Enticement and sextortion reports (1.4M in 2025, up 158%) arrive as chat excerpts in a CyberTip and are ranked by hand at NCMEC and the task force, while the only grooming classifiers run platform-side and never reach the investigator. Stanford's "two identical reports, one leads to a rescue" gap is unfilled.

Report quality is still the bottleneck and the fix so far has been legislative, not technical. Since April 2024: the REPORT Act (1-year preservation, enticement and trafficking mandatory), report bundling used only by Meta, the Tech Coalition Pathways hub, NCMEC reporting guidelines, and the TAKE IT DOWN Act. Still true in 2026: an XML plus basic-auth API, ~10% of reports unroutable, no algorithmic prioritization at NCMEC.

Where the money is: commercial forensic suites sold per seat to LE, federal appropriations for victim ID (Renewed Hope $108.5M and 200 positions, ICAC shrinking to $33.9M, Canada CAD 18.2M, UK CAID re-procurement), and philanthropy. LE-facing nonprofits give tools away, so a paid investigator tool is a hard sell; revenue lives platform-side.

Access is gated the same way everywhere: LE-only, verified statutory authority, training, and a signed agreement. Guardian's investigator surface must match that gate and plug into existing tools via VICS JSON rather than asking analysts to adopt another viewer.

What practitioners complain about: volume without ranking, duplicates and viral memes, missing IP and location data, encryption shrinking reports, AI-generated CSAM swamping victim ID ("how much time do we spend trying to identify somebody that doesn't exist?"), 10 analysts against 89,000 series, analyst trauma, opaque tooling that fails in court, and NCMEC's legacy stack.

Civilian and NGO analysts survive legally by staying passive and LE-owned: no contact with subjects, no decoys, leads packaged with a discovery path, LE files the warrant. That is the same wall as the private-search rule and the argument for keeping the investigator surface a consumer of LE data, never a source of tasking into the platform kernel.

### 2.4 Open source and research

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PAN-2012 Sexual Predator Identification (PAN12) | Shared-task corpus: identify predator users among all users, and identify the predator lines most distinctive of bad behavior. Built from Perverted Justice decoy logs (11,350 conversations) mixed with krijnhoetmer IRC (50,510), irclog.org (28,501) and Omegle (267,261); conversations over 150 messages dropped. Metrics F0.5 and F3. Test set has 254 predator users among roughly 218,700 (about 0.12%); 66,927 training and 155,128 test conversations. Winning run reached P 0.980, R 0.787, F1 0.873; the best per-line F3 was 0.476 with most teams near 0.05 | Academic NLP; the benchmark in 10 of the computational studies counted by the 2025 systematic review | Alive as an archival benchmark on Zenodo (record 3713280), no explicit license. Data is 2006-2012 | Input and precedent: usable only as one held-out set. Any F1 above 0.95 on it should be read as saturated |
| Perverted Justice chat logs (PJ / PJFI archive) | The upstream source of nearly every grooming positive in the literature: transcripts of convicted adults talking to adult volunteers posing as minors, 2003-2019. Used directly by 16 studies and indirectly by PAN12, ChatCoder2, PANC, PJZ, SCoRL and the Purdue fuzzy-risk work | Researchers, LE, legal requests | Sunset: decoy operations ceased at the start of 2019 (623+ convictions claimed) and the logs were removed from public view. Access is by email request to archivist@pjfi.org | Input, indirect. Treat it as the provenance flag on any training row: models trained on decoy data score lowest on real-victim chats |
| ChatCoder2 (CC2) | 497 complete predator chats from Perverted Justice, 155 with per-message labels for three phases (exchange of personal information, grooming, approach). The only public source of stage-labelled grooming messages and the positive set for PANC and SCoRL | Early-detection and stage-classification researchers | Effectively unavailable: chatcoder.com answers 200 but /data.html returns 404 as of 2026-09-03 | Input, needed to rebuild PANC. Plan for it being unobtainable and budget for your own stage labelling |
| PANC and VTPAN (Vogt, Leser, Akbik, ACL 2021) | Defines early sexual predator detection: raise a warning as early as possible on a growing chat prefix. PANC combines ChatCoder2 full-length positives with PAN12 negative segments; introduces latency-weighted F1. Train 19,351 segments (1,753 positive, 9.06%), test 13,159 (1,426 positive, 10.84%), 497 positive chats averaging 2,075 messages. Best SBERT-base: F1 0.89, P 0.82, R 0.96, speed 0.91, F-latency 0.81; SMobileBERT F1 0.80 | Researchers benchmarking early warning | Alive; preprocessing code on GitLab with a banner saying research only and that models trained on this data will not detect real grooming attempts | Input and precedent. Use the F-latency metric in the eval harness and copy the do-not-deploy warning into the README |
| PJZ / PJZC and the BF-PSR framework | Two conversation-level grooming datasets from PJ chats, #ZIG IRC and chit-chat data, plus seven behavioral features (conversation start time, intervention words per user, participant count) bolted onto an early-classification framework. Claims more than 40% effectiveness gain over five competitors when only 10% of a conversation is visible | Early-detection researchers | Alive but frozen: repo created 2021-11-26, no LICENSE file | Input: a cheap second held-out set and a source of hand-crafted behavioral baselines. Do not ship anything trained on it without resolving the license |
| SCoRL: Speed Control Reinforcement Learning (NAACL 2025) | Re-labels PANC at the turn level using Luring Communication Theory and trains a RoBERTa-base policy with a reward trading speed against accuracy. Test set 69 positive of 11,802 chats (0.58%). Turn-level results: F1 0.641, P 0.475, R 0.983; latency-F1 0.365 versus 0.062 for the SBERT sliding-window baseline; identifies the risky utterance in 85.5% of cases | Researchers | Paper alive; the code and data repo is README-only, marked "[Not Finished Yet]," last pushed 2025-06-01 | Precedent: adopt its turn-level evaluation and speed reward for the T1/T2 trigger. Do not plan on its data |
| Fuzzy grooming-risk evaluation of encoders (Bihani and Rayz, Purdue, 2025) | Maps twelve annotated grooming strategies to a continuous risk score with fuzzy membership, then tests fine-tuned bi-encoders on chats where the child side is a law-enforcement officer, a real victim, or a decoy | Researchers | Alive (Feb 2025 preprints); no code or data released | Precedent, and the direct evidence for the normalization layer. Overall F1 LEO 0.70-0.71, victim 0.67-0.68, decoy 0.59-0.60; significant-risk F1 collapses to 0.26-0.27 on decoy chats; OOV token share runs 20.7% to 30.8% |
| LLaMA 3.2 1B versus SVM on PAN12 with tone (Hamm and McKeever, Frontiers in Pediatrics 2025) | Fine-tunes LLaMA 3.2 1B and TF-IDF SVMs on PAN12 (160,773 conversations, 5,753 predatory, 3.5%), split by inferred tone | Pediatrics and clinical audience | Alive (June 2025) | Precedent: a 1B model reaches P/R/F1 0.99 on PAN12 in 2-4.5 GPU hours versus SVM F1 0.94, so the small-encoder budget is realistic. Groomers were positive-toned in 46.9% of chats versus 25.9% benign. Says nothing about PPV at 0.01% |
| Federated learning plus differential privacy for eSPD (Chehbouni et al., AAAI 2025) | Trains early detectors on PANC across simulated clients with Flower, comparing centralized, federated and DP variants | Privacy researchers; platforms that cannot centralize message data | Alive | Precedent for the parent app and per-customer isolation.
