Federated F1 0.82 versus centralized 0.75; user-level DP-FedAvg at epsilon 1 keeps F-latency at 0.63; 3% FPR at an inflated base rate is still catastrophic at 0.01% |
| Context determination and message-level analysis (Street et al., Nottingham Trent, 2025) | Two-stage pipeline: RoBERTa message-level adult/child classification, then actor-level aggregation with message and actor significance thresholds. Trained on PJ, cross-tested on PAN12 | Researchers | Alive (Nov 2025) | Precedent: independent support for scoring the pair rather than the message. Actor-level F1 0.973 on PJ and 0.960 cross-dataset (1,106 TP, 26 FP), while message-level accuracy drops from 88.8% to 67.2%. Aggregation carries the signal |
| Contrastive chat embeddings for grooming (Borj et al., IWSPA 2023) | RoBERTa plus supervised SimCSE embeddings feeding an SVM on PAN12 | Researchers | Alive | Precedent for the per-actor skew embedder. F1 0.96, fusion F1 0.97; subword embeddings handle misspellings |
| Roblox PII Classifier v2 and benchmark | XLM-RoBERTa-Large multi-label classifier over three intents with published thresholds, fixed instruction prefix and multi-turn history with the target speaker tagged t, 512 tokens left-truncated, 189 languages. Companion benchmark: 39,202 fully synthetic English conversations (14,684 unsafe) with adversarial evasions and hard negatives (server codes, song IDs, roleplay addresses) | Any chat platform | Alive; v1 2025-11-04, v2 2026-08-19, Apache-2.0 | Input, already named in CLAUDE.md. Reported F1 90.52% internal versus v1 63.41; v1 gave 98% recall at 1% FPR on English production text and F1 94.34% in production against 45.48% on public datasets, the clearest public statement of the production gap. Runs at 6.1B messages a day, 200k QPS peak, p90 under 100ms |
| Roblox Sentinel (v1 Aug 2025, v2 Aug 2026) | Python library for real-time detection of extremely rare text classes: embed, score similarity against positive and negative indices, aggregate a user's recent scores by skewness or five other functions. v2 adds from_texts index building, subsample without re-encoding, persisted corpus for explanations, and a simulation module reporting ROC-AUC, recall@N, F1/FPR, Cohen's d and KS | Platforms with their own exemplar sets | Alive; 115 stars, Apache-2.0 | Input: the skew-not-mean idea the design is built on. No weights and no grooming exemplars ship; the only example data is SPLC extremist text, and Roblox's negative index has ~13,000 exemplars you must recreate |
| Roblox Voice Safety Classifier v3 | 0.3B transformer distilled from a larger teacher, trained on 300k hours of voice, 8 heads including a dating/romantic head, 30 languages, 16 kHz WAV up to 15s | Voice-chat platforms | Alive; Apache-2.0; 72k+ downloads of the family since 2024 | Precedent for how to report metrics: 61% recall at 1% FPR overall, per-language from 29.5% (Czech) to 75.2% (Polish), English 67.9%. Not an input in phase 1 |
| Roblox Guard 1.0 | Instruction-tuned Llama 3.1 8B classifying prompts and responses to Roblox's text-generation API against their policy taxonomy, with RobloxGuard-Eval | Platforms adding LLM features for kids | Alive; RAIL-MS license, eval data CC BY-NC 4.0 | Irrelevant to the kernel; guards AI chat, not human-to-human grooming |
| ROOST and the ROOST Model Community | Nonprofit founded early 2025 with Google, OpenAI, Roblox and Discord as founding members to make open source the default for trust and safety. Runs Coop, Osprey, the Model Community (Roblox models, gpt-oss-safeguard, Mistral Shieldstral, Zentropi CoPE) and the awesome-safety-tools directory. Technical Design Committee seated Dec 2025 | Small and mid platforms; non-Western practitioners are a stated focus | Alive and growing | Partner and precedent. Its own 2025 review admits the most urgently needed open-source building blocks, especially for child safety, remain unavailable, and no openly licensed novel-CSAM classifier is being built |
| ROOST Coop | TypeScript review and enforcement platform: queues with counts, priorities and SLA status; a rules engine that auto-actions or routes to humans; REST and GraphQL APIs; ClickHouse or Snowflake analytics; built-in NCMEC reporting; HMA, Google Content Safety and OpenAI moderation integrations | Platforms needing a human-in-the-loop queue; Notion, Kyodo, Musubi | Alive; V0 2026-02-10, 1.0 2026-06-09, Apache-2.0 | Partner and precedent. Guardian's review app should emit Coop-compatible jobs or copy its review-card pattern rather than rebuilding a generic queue. It has no conversation timeline, pair view or stage annotations |
| ROOST Osprey | Real-time event rules engine in SML with user-defined functions, a labels service for stateful entity tagging, and a query and charting UI. Runs hundreds of millions of events a day at Bluesky; also Discord and Matrix.org | Safety engineers and analysts | Alive; 474 stars, Apache-2.0; 2026 roadmap adds a no-code rules UI, shadow mode and batch backfill | Partner: the critical-override rules are Osprey-shaped, and a Discord customer running Osprey should be able to consume Guardian tiers as labels |
| gpt-oss-safeguard (20b / 120b) | Bring-your-own-policy safety reasoning models: paste a written policy and the model classifies content against it and returns its reasoning. 21B (3.6B active, fits 16 GB VRAM) and 117B | Trust and safety teams doing LLM filtering or offline labelling | Alive; released 2025-10-29, Apache-2.0 | Input for a later phase: a candidate second-stage explainer for T2 bundles, never the tier authority. No child-safety evaluation published |
| Meta Hasher-Matcher-Actioner 2.0 | Reference implementation of hash-based copy detection with banks of known content and actions; connects to the NCMEC hash sharing program, GIFCT, StopNCII and ThreatExchange. Rewritten from Terraform/AWS to Flask plus Postgres plus Docker | Any platform needing known-content matching; Coop integrates it | Alive; 1,383 stars, BSD-3 | Partner: the operator-side component producing the hash verdict Guardian consumes. Requires holding bytes at hash time, which Guardian must not do |
| Thorn Safer Predict text classifier (also sold as Hive's CSE Text Classifier API) | Closed B2B text classifier returning per-label scores for CSAM, child access, sextortion, self-generated content, CSA discussion, has minor and grooming. Customers set precision levels per label and stack labels to target high-risk accounts | 80+ Safer customers; Hive's dashboard customers | Alive | Competitor at the platform SDK layer for large customers and a possible input if a customer already licenses it. No public precision, recall or base-rate figures and no model card |
| Tech Coalition Lantern | Cross-platform signal sharing including coded language and financial sextortion indicators; 38 participants; 2M+ signals shared 2023-2025 leading to ~354k enforcement actions | Vetted tech companies and financial institutions; NGOs, governments and LE are ineligible | Alive | Partner later: the opt-in cross-platform sharing feature. The lexicon must be versioned so shared coded-language lists can be imported |
| LLMs as grooming detectors (Prosser and Edwards, EICC 2024) | 6,000+ interactions testing six LLMs on general safety advice, identifying grooming in decoy-child conversations, and targeted advice | Anyone tempted to use a chat LLM as the detector | Alive | Precedent and a clear negative result: no model was clearly appropriate, behavior was inconsistent across prompts, and open-source models produced harmful answers. The citation for keeping tier decisions in a small supervised encoder plus rules |
| Korean grooming detection (SAC 2025; KcELECTRA plus OCR, CMES 2025) | Early detection on a Korean translation of PAN12 with a memory method retrieving prior similar sentences instead of a sliding window, plus a Human-Model-Ratio metric; separately KcELECTRA fine-tuned on Korean social-media chat with OCR to read chat screenshots, accuracy 0.953 | Korean platforms and researchers | Alive (2025 papers); no released datasets | Precedent only. Memory-retrieval context is a cheap idea for the pair feature window; the accuracy figure is not a grooming number |
| Spanish grooming work (ONVIOS with University of Valencia, Oct 2025; Lorenzo-Dus et al., Corpus Pragmatics, June 2026) | ONVIOS announced a case-classification model by offender and victim typology plus a technological tool for early identification for police use. Separately, analysis of 70 authentic Spanish LE groomer-child chat logs found over half of initial requests occur within the first five turns, with imperatives dominant and wrapped in politeness | Spanish police; corpus linguists | Alive; no dataset released | Precedent and possible investigator partner. Real victim-offender data, and the first-five-turns finding supports the velocity term |
| Community grooming models on Hugging Face | Search on 2026-09-03 finds erikaecl/hansen-grooming-distilbert (Apache-2.0, PAN12 plus NPS Chat, no metrics), ShellyMelly/grooming-detector, Gemma LoRAs, Kristo4/pan12-sexual-predator-chronological (CC-BY-4.0 per-message re-cut), CristinaMierla/PAN12_predatorTask_romanianTranslation, and one "africa-sextortion" dataset that is 10 synthetic rows | Hobbyists and students | Alive but unevaluated | Irrelevant as inputs; useful as evidence that no vetted open grooming weights exist and no sextortion model exists in any language |
| Systematic reviews 2025 | An interdisciplinary review inventories the datasets in use (PJ, PAN12, PAN13 blogs, NPS Chat, LiveMe, ChatCoder, Literotica) and counts PAN12 in 10 studies and PJ in 16; a Universidad de Talca meta-analysis pools 26 of 40 studies (accuracy BiLSTM 95%, MLP 92%, SVM 88%; F1 SVM and MLP 0.79; precision Random Forest 99%) | Anyone writing a related-work section | Alive | Precedent: documents that stage-based detection is only 9.09% of computational studies, and that the field has decoy bias, English-only data, unaddressed class imbalance and tactic drift since 2012. Neither review found a single deployment evaluation |
| Model cards for child-safety AI (Francois, Mitchell, Jernite, Rao, Matias, 2026-04-02) | Argues that CSAM, grooming and self-harm detectors ship with no performance documentation, names PhotoDNA, Thorn Safer and Google Content Safety API as dominant undocumented tools, notes fewer than five novel-CSAM classifiers exist, and proposes closed evaluation servers run by NCMEC or IWF | Policymakers, ROOST, vendors | Proposal; no eval server exists yet | Precedent: it sets a transparency bar Guardian can clear cheaply by publishing a model card with recall at fixed FPR and the base-rate simulation |
| NCMEC 2025 sextortion figures | More than 50,000 financially motivated sextortion reports in 2025 (137 a day), up from 36,000 in 2024; 94% of public reports came from the child or a parent; offenders move to private and encrypted apps faster, some with no NCMEC reporting path | Public, platforms, LE | Alive | Input for the base-rate simulation. There is no public sextortion corpus in any language, so the MinHash script index must be seeded from case filings, journalism and reviewer-confirmed cases |

**Themes.**

Everyone trains on the same 2003-2019 decoy corpus. PJ feeds PAN12, ChatCoder2, PANC, VTPAN, PJZ/PJZC, SCoRL, the Korean and Romanian translations, and every hobby model on Hugging Face. F1 on it is saturated and the eSPD authors themselves ship a do-not-use banner. The only real-victim results are unreleased and show worse numbers.

Nobody publishes precision at a production base rate. PAN12 is 0.12% positive at author level and 3.5% at conversation level, PANC 9.8%, SCoRL 0.58%; industry reports volumes or internal ROC-AUC, never PPV. The single exception is Roblox voice v3. A model card with recall at fixed FPR plus a base-rate simulation is a differentiator, not a chore.

The field is moving from per-conversation to per-turn and per-actor, but the labels do not exist. SCoRL has 77 turn-labelled chats and its repo stalled; ChatCoder2's 155 phase-labelled chats sit behind a 404; message-level accuracy collapses cross-dataset while actor-level aggregation holds. Guardian's six-stage per-pair labels will have to come from its own reviewers.

Open weights exist only for adjacent tasks: PII and off-platform intent, voice, LLM guardrails, and hash matching. There is no open grooming-stage or sextortion model from any credible source. The exemplar and lexicon sets are the proprietary asset.

Coded language and out-of-vocabulary tokens are the documented failure mode. A versioned normalization lexicon fed by confirmed cases is the cheapest defense and nobody open has shipped one.

Where the money is: closed B2B text classification for large platforms, and philanthropically funded open infrastructure for queues, rules, hash matching and reporting. The gap ROOST names in writing is child-safety models.

Non-English and sextortion are empty. Non-English work is machine-translated PAN12 or police-held Spanish corpora; only Roblox's synthetic-data PII model claims broad language coverage. No public sextortion chat corpus exists despite 50,000 NCMEC reports in 2025.

### 2.5 UX audit

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bark (parent alerts and dashboard) | Alert = category chip, severity, source app, quoted snippet, timestamp, plus recommended next steps, delivered by push, email and SMS. Alerts page filters by unread/read, child, platform, concern type, severity, date and saved bookmarks. Per-category sensitivity All / Severe / None; Ignore Alert silences a specific user, group chat, song or subscription unless it is a severe issue; suicide and predator alerts cannot be turned off. Media flagged as potential child nudity is deleted from Bark servers by law and replaced with a gray placeholder | Parents; K-12 via Bark for Schools | Alive | Steal: the severe-always-alerts floor under every mute, Ignore-per-contact with an undo list, the gray placeholder for hashed-and-deleted media. Avoid: no structured decision beyond read/unread, so nothing feeds back into the model |
| Qustodio | Near-real-time Activity Timeline with plain wording ("Searched for X", "OnlyFans.com has been blocked"); alerts sit in the same timeline as blocked-site events; setup about 15 minutes per device | Parents | Alive | Steal: the timeline as the primary view, one sentence per event. Avoid: silent failure when a monitored channel produces nothing. Testing found no alerts for inappropriate text content and no indicator that a picture arrived |
| Aura (Kids / Online Wellbeing) | Desktop daily summary plus a mobile Balance tab analyzing messaging tone and social interaction without reading messages, producing a Social Persona card and a Week in Review with percentage changes | Families in a security bundle | Alive | Steal: the persona and summary framing that describes behavior rather than quoting the child. Avoid: weekly-only cadence for safety signals, which parents read as the product not working |
| Modulate ToxMod | Incident record = audio clip, transcript, category, timestamp, severity, kept on file so appeals can be reviewed. Player Risk Categories (Child Grooming, Violent Radicalization) score patterns over time; the grooming category fires on consistently suspicious or suggestive questions to underage players even without sexual content. Delivered API and webhook first into existing case tools | Game studios' trust and safety teams | Alive | Steal: a longitudinal player-risk object separate from per-incident alerts, mirroring per-actor skew versus per-pair trajectory, and transcript with timestamp anchors next to the raw signal |
| Hive Moderation Dashboard | No-code rules (class plus score range to action), a Post Review Feed of tiles with the flag reason at the top and an escalation icon, batch decisions where unselected tiles get the default or No Action, customer-defined webhook actions, a user card with top categories and flagged-post percentage, a CSAM Review Feed that never displays CSAM with a Report to NCMEC action, a fileViewedByEsp toggle, Mark as High Priority with mandatory reasoning, queue-health Slack alerts (stale tasks, booted tasks, percent acted on per moderator, failed actions), and an appeals feed showing the original decision | Mid-size platforms | Alive | Steal: default action plus No Action on unselected items, the explicit escalation icon, never rendering the media, stale-task and throughput alerts to a channel, and the appeals view. Docs mention no keyboard shortcuts, no blur toggles, and no reviewer wellness at all |
| Cinder | Review card with avatar, handle, type, age, status badge, the text, and a metadata line. Decisions are policy labels by keypress with a printed apply and undo hint; queue header shows a live count. Queue settings: Target Handling Time, Multi-Review Settings (consensus before a decision is final), Escalate Jobs, Change Queue, User Reports Visibility, Flaggable Field Guided Review, training queues. Safety Tools let admins set images and videos blurred and grayscale by default | Trust and safety ops teams | Alive; Series B 2026 | Steal: number-key policy labels with a visible apply/undo hint, SLA as a queue property, multi-review consensus for T3, reports visible only if admins allow, and blur plus grayscale set at the queue level |
| Checkstep | Governance-first platform: policy and compliance management, ModBot automation plus human queues with escalation and QC, user reporting, and DSA/OSA transparency reports and audit trails generated instantly | Platforms with EU or UK obligations | Alive | Steal: every decision producing a regulator-readable record. Guardian's audit chain should render as a human-readable statement of reasons |
| Discord AutoMod and Safety Setup | Server Settings to AutoMod: keyword filters (1 preset plus 3 custom, 1,000 keywords each), Commonly Flagged Words presets, Mention Spam up to 50, Spam Content. Responses: Block message, Send an alert, Time out user. The alert posted to the log channel shows the full message preview, who, what, where, the channel, and two buttons, Actions and Report Issues. Exemptions for roles, channels and words; wildcards; admins bypass | Server owners and admins | Alive | Direct input for the Discord surface: Guardian's alerts should slot into the same log channel and vocabulary. Avoid its ungrouped per-message alerts, which drown a busy server |
| Discord Family Center and Teen Safety Alerts | Family Center gives a connected guardian an activity feed and weekly email of recently added friends, servers joined and DM contacts, never content. Teen Safety Assist shows teens Safety Alerts on potentially inappropriate DMs and blurs or blocks sensitive media by default. Published copy principles: plain language, no idioms or metaphors, short sentences, alerts built "to feel more like guidance," "a guardrail versus a speed bump," restoring control by reminding the teen they can block or report | Teens 13-17 and guardians | Alive; Safety Alerts on by default globally | Steal: the writing rules for every user-facing string, and the new-contact and server-join feed as the parent-facing evidence layer instead of transcripts. Avoid: weekly-only cadence |
| Roblox age checks, Trusted Connections, Parental Controls, Sentinel | Facial age estimation (images deleted immediately after processing) or ID check assigns one of six groups (Under 9, 9-12, 13-15, 16-17, 18-20, 21+); chat is limited to similar groups unless users become Trusted Connections via QR scan or contact import. Parents get Trusted Connections and Connection Insights, Screen-Time Insights and Spending Notifications. Sentinel outputs are explainable via top positive and negative components and neighbor snippets | 100M+ daily users; parents; internal analysts | Alive; voluntary Nov 19 2025, mandatory Dec 2025 in AU/NZ/NL, global Jan 2026 | Steal: age-group labels as displayed bands, Connection Insights framing, and showing top exemplar neighbors next to a skew score. Avoid: making parents pull. Roblox does not proactively notify parents when a teen adds an adult Trusted Connection |
| GoGuardian Beacon (and Beacon 24/7) | Five Predicted Phases (Active Planning, Suicide Ideation, Self Harm, Help & Support, Suicide Research) with Active Planning notifications mandatory and the rest configurable. Alert card: student name and email, date, a Frequency Indicator counting recent alerts by that student, new-alert status shown as dark outline and light blue highlighting, phase badge, guardian contact, actions taken. Human Risk Level is a separate dropdown defaulting to "Select Risk Level" with No Risk grey, Low yellow, Medium orange, High red. Rationale tab shows screenshots from 15 minutes either side with the source highlighted and a Words on the Page section; Browsing tab lists URLs and timestamps. Decisions are actions taken (In-Person Conversation, Guardian Contacted, Plan Devised) plus a three-prompt notes template. Volume controls: snooze from 1 hour to indefinitely, grouping within a 15-minute window, per-category reminder cadence, separate school-hours and after-hours escalation lists, holiday pause, save as PDF. The 24/7 card has a four-stage status bar and result badges | School counselors and administrators | Alive; contested | The strongest precedent for the reviewer queue and operator alert workflow, and the most fully documented alert-to-decision UI in the space. Steal the model-versus-human split, the actions and notes template, the highlighted text evidence, the frequency indicator, and every volume control |
| Securly Aware and Securly Respond | Aware scores each student into a color-coded Wellness Level (All Clear, Concerning, High Risk, Critical) from flagged activity across web, email, Google Docs including drafts, and social posts. Respond auto-creates or appends a case per student with notes, assignment and status. On-Call adds a human team that verifies non-urgent cases and badges them. Think Twice shows the student a reconsider prompt while composing | K-12 districts | Alive | Steal: a per-actor case with append semantics, human-verification badges, and level counts as the dashboard's first row. Avoid: an unexplained composite score |
| Magnet Griffeye Analyze DI Pro | Thumbnail grid with filters, pre-categorization of known data via hash intelligence, duplicate stacking, a Griffeye Intelligence Database marking previously viewed media, the Brain CSAM classifier, face recognition, video tools, reporting and export. Built-in wellbeing features are a stated product pillar | Law enforcement ICAC and forensic units; 1,000+ agencies | Alive; Lite free to law enforcement | Steal: already-seen and already-categorized as the first sort key, dedupe before display, and a free tier for law enforcement as the partner wedge |
| Safer by Thorn (review tool) | Review Tool 2.9.0 added more powerful filtering and sorting plus configurable report fields aligned to the CyberTipline Reporting API; context labels let a reviewer prioritize without opening the file; a customer portal consolidates impact metrics, hashlist management and docs | 86+ platforms | Alive | Steal: make every review record's fields a superset of the CyberTipline API fields so export is a projection, and use context labels as a pre-open summary strip |
| NCMEC CyberTipline public form and ESP API | Public form: 7 steps, English and Spanish, a persistent Quick Exit, a note that most questions are not necessary, and 1-800-THE-LOST as a fallback. ESP API: POST /submit returns a report ID, then /upload, /fileinfo, /finish, with /retract to cancel; unfinished reports auto-delete after 24 hours; required minimum is incident type plus an ISO 8601 past-dated timestamp with timezone plus reporter name and email; incident types are a fixed enumeration; file-level flags record whether the ESP viewed the entire file and EXIF, public accessibility, upload timestamp and original URL | Public reporters, parents and registered ESPs | Alive | The bundle's target shape. Steal Quick Exit on any parent-facing evidence screen, draft the bundle as a filled seven-step walkthrough plus a finished XML, and copy the 24-hour draft auto-delete as the retention model |
| Reddit modqueue (and the CHI 2026 modqueue study) | Volunteer queue aggregating user reports, AutoModerator filters and spam detection, with author, report count, karma, excerpt, parent context and report reasons; subqueues as tabs; actions Approve, Remove, Spam, Ban, Lock, flair, Ignore; New Reddit adds a context panel, a user panel and real-time indicators of other mods' activity; mobile uses swipe cards. Study of 110 mods across 408 subreddits | Volunteer moderators | Alive | Steal: an explicit claim and lock with a visible "being reviewed by" state, keeping the item on screen after a decision until follow-ups are done, and a pattern view across alerts per actor. 74.5% of mods experienced collisions, activity indicators are too subtle to trust, and over 84% regularly leave the queue for context |
| Meta human review prioritization | Ranks content for human review by severity, virality and likelihood of violating; reviewers are assigned a queue of posts and specialize by policy area and language across 80+ languages, with escalation to subject-matter experts and supplied context such as reclaimed slurs | Meta's reviewers; published for regulators | Alive | Steal: order the queue by severity times likelihood times reach, and label that ordering on screen. Says nothing about reviewer tooling or wellness |
| TSPA curriculum: Setting Up a Content Moderator for Success | Tooling list: option to blur photos, option to turn images black and white, option to change audio frequency, a keyword highlighter for content with hundreds of words, variable video playback speed, all-thumbnails view, transcripts with optional translation. Training modes include practice tests with mock enforcement decisions | Trust and safety managers | Alive; free | Input: the checklist reviewers expect. Steal the keyword highlighter and transcript-first review as the text analogues of blur, and a training queue with mock cases before a reviewer can produce T3. Silent on shift length, exposure caps and rotation |
| Research on wellness tooling (Steiger et al. CHI 2021; Das et al. 2020; Karunakaran and Ramakrishnan 2019) | Literature review of moderator wellbeing plus the underlying experiments. Grayscale at Google improved positive affect while preserving detection of the most extreme images, but moderators were irritated by static blurring. Interactive blur (slider, hover, click) diminished emotional impact without compromising accuracy or speed. Microsoft's video tool has supported black-and-white and variable blur since Jan 2019 and Facebook gave moderators a blur and mute preference pane in May 2019 | Tool designers and T&S leads | Published | Input: the only quantitative basis for choosing reveal-on-demand over always-on redaction. All evidence is image and video; nothing studies text-conversation exposure |
| Zevo Health practitioner guidance | Micro-breaks of 2-5 minutes, a flexible break-taking system where moderators step away when impacted, task rotation across content categories, monthly EAP usage and absenteeism tracking, short regular surveys, plus a tooling list including automated grayscale, reduced media size, automatic mute, interactive blurring, storyboard video view, AI pre-screening and wellness-break reminders | Moderation leads and BPOs | Alive | Input: break reminders tied to exposure count rather than clock time, and rotation between review and lower-intensity tasks. Admits moderators opt out of protections for throughput |
| Stanford Internet Observatory 2024 ecosystem report | Examines the CyberTipline pipeline and what industry, NCMEC and Congress must fix | Platforms, NCMEC, law enforcement, Congress | Published; SIO wound down in 2024 | Input: the reason the bundle must be complete and deduplicated before it reaches CyberTipline or an ICAC unit. Treat "is this report actionable" fields as required even though the API makes them optional |

**Themes.**

Everyone splits what the model thinks from what the human decided, and the professional tools make that split visible: Beacon's Predicted Phase badge against a Risk Level dropdown that defaults to unset, Cinder's policy labels by keypress with undo, Hive's default action versus No Action. Nobody ships a button that names a person.

Evidence is always a window, not a message. Beacon shows plus or minus 15 minutes with the source highlighted, ToxMod ships clip plus transcript plus timestamp, and the Reddit study found over 84% of mods leave the queue for context while 74.5% collide on items. A card must embed the pair's conversation window, the actor's prior alerts, and a claim or lock state.

Decision recording that works is an action vocabulary plus a notes template, not a verdict. Beacon's three-prompt notes, Cinder's per-attribute guided review and Hive's appeals view showing the original decision are the working patterns.

Wellness tooling is solved for images and absent for text. Blur and grayscale by default, never rendering CSAM, hash pre-categorization so humans see only the residue, and interactive blur that keeps accuracy while static blur irritates. No product documents exposure counters, rotation or reveal-on-demand for grooming transcripts.

Alert fatigue is the universal complaint and the fixes are all throttles with a safety floor: Bark's All/Severe/None with severe always alerting and per-contact Ignore with an undo list; Beacon's 15-minute grouping, snooze durations, per-category reminder cadence, after-hours lists and holiday pause; Hive's stale-task and throughput alerts.

Copy that reads as professional describes behavior in plain words and offers options. Discord's published rules, Beacon's behavioral phase names, and Roblox's age groups and Connection Insights all avoid adjectives about people.

The money is in human escalation and compliance, not detection. Enterprise platforms publish no prices and sell seats plus SLA features; K-12 vendors sell 24/7 human phone escalation as the upgrade; parent apps sit at $5-$10 a month and earn one-star clusters for false positives and unanswered support.

Visual patterns that read as professional: dense mostly monochrome cards where color is reserved for one risk scale and outcome badges, unread as light fill plus dark outline, a four-stage status bar, keyboard hints printed on buttons, a live queue count and SLA in the header. What reads as cheap: consumer gradients and emoji-laden alerts, weekly-only digests for safety signals, screenshots as the only evidence, and help centers that never show the actual alert copy.

### 2.6 Reviewer tooling and wellness

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSPA Trust and Safety Fundamentals curriculum | Covers team models (vendor, in-house contractor, employee specialist, volunteer), resilience interface features, quality assurance including reverse quality sampling where pre-reviewed seeded examples are pushed through the normal queue, appeals analysis as a cheap false-positive detector, and metrics: SLA as a predefined target time, closes, actions, action rate, error rate split into false positive, false negative, wrong selection and technical error, and review or handling time | New and mid-career T&S staff; operations leads | Alive; there is no dedicated wellness chapter | Input: use its metric definitions and reverse-QA pattern verbatim, and treat its four-bullet wellness list as the floor. It warns that attaching a dollar cost to individual reviews creates a conflict between finance and user protection |
| Steiger, Bharucha, Venkatagiri, Riedl, Lease (CHI 2021) | Organizes moderator wellness into primary (prevent exposure), secondary (reduce exposure via affective interface design) and tertiary (treatment) interventions and reviews the evidence for each. Recommends risk disclosure at hiring, daily exposure limits, alternating exposure with other tasks, scheduled micro-breaks, a lifetime exposure cap, post-employment care, and decision support that lets reviewers decide from explanations without opening the content. Documents Accenture wellness time capped at 45 minutes a week | Researchers and tool designers | Alive | Input: the three-level model is the right organizing frame, and decide-from-explanation is exactly the text-timeline posture. Facebook's own clinical psychologist said nobody knows how much exposure is too much |
| Gauthier, Ali, Asim, Cornell-Maier, Zoellner (University of Washington, 2025) | Two-sample clinical study, 166 international moderators cross-sectionally and 45 US moderators prospectively with a comparison group. Probable PTSD 25.9-26.3%, probable depression 42.1-48.5%, moderate-to-severe somatic symptoms 68.7-89.5%, alcohol misuse 10.5-18.3%; US moderators 8.2 times more likely to have a current mood disorder. Hours per day of distressing content was not a robust predictor; organizational context, negative automatic thoughts, avoidant coping and intrusion-related distress dominated, with a conditional dose effect once exposure is held to roughly 2-4 hours a day | Platform wellness programs, tool designers, litigators | Alive | Input: sets the default session budget at 2 hours a day of T2 work and argues that a solo reviewer with no team is itself a risk factor. Recommends confidential mental-health monitoring not visible to the employer |
| AURA framework (Zhang, Amores, Gray, Czerwinski, Suh, Microsoft Research 2024) | Survey of 67 plus interviews and workshops with 14. Most useful protective tools were video and audio speed controls (4.1/5), automatic verification (3.9) and automatic warnings (3.9); usefulness of blurring and face blocking rose with contiguous exposure hours. Large shares lacked access to automatic clustering (38.8%) and automatic verification (34.3%); 35.8% of those who could turn off video never did. Workers objected to productivity measured by time or volume and wanted metrics weighted by content severity | Tool builders and T&S managers | Alive | Input: replace volume and handle-time dashboards with a severity-weighted exposure meter and make protective defaults on, since opt-in protections go unused |
| Mihalache and Szostak (Google, 2025) | Practitioner framework for wellbeing-centered UX: blur or obscure by default with reviewer opt-in reveal, a severity preview before the full item loads, visual separation of the decision area from the content area, enforced session endpoints and mandatory breaks, buffer time between consecutive high-severity items, reviewer control over reveal order, explicit microcopy explaining each decision category and its consequences, non-alarming notifications and visible escalation paths | Product designers and writers on T&S tools | Alive | Input: the most concrete UX checklist from an operator. Adopt severity preview, reveal-on-demand, decision-area separation and consequence microcopy directly |
| Interactive image blurring studies (Dang, Riedl, Lease, HCOMP 2018; Das, Dang, Lease 2020) | Gaussian blur up to sigma 14 with three reveal modalities: slider, hover, click-to-persist. Negative emotion highest for the unblurred baseline and lowest for fixed blur and hover; all blurred interfaces raised positive affect, with accuracy and speed uncompromised | Researchers | Alive | Precedent: the text analogue is collapsing high-severity spans behind hover or click reveal while leaving the surrounding timeline readable |
| HateBuffer (Park et al., KAIST, CSCW 2025) | Text-only protective tool: anonymizes hate-speech targets, paraphrases offensive expressions into milder forms, shows the original on demand. 80-participant simulated moderation study | Text moderators | Alive | Precedent with a warning. Accuracy was not compromised and participants rated severity lower, but there was no measured improvement in emotion or fatigue. Paraphrase alters evidence, so any span supporting a T3 decision must be shown verbatim and the softened rendering never persisted |
| Abdelkadir et al. (CHI 2026) | Survey of 134 and interviews with 15 African moderators. Higher distress than moderators elsewhere; handling-time quotas, constant monitoring, poor tooling forcing manual workarounds, inadequate breaks and generic wellness programs drive harm beyond exposure; former moderators remain elevated. Calls for quotas calibrated to content severity | Policy, labor and tool designers | Alive | Input: a review SLA must not become a per-case handling-time quota. Pace by severity |
| Sarah T. Roberts, Behind the Screen (2019) | Ethnography of commercial content moderation from Silicon Valley to the Philippines, establishing the 100,000-plus invisible workforce, the deliberate invisibility of the labor behind "algorithmic" moderation, and the industrial stratification that blocks peer support | Anyone writing policy or tooling for human review | Alive | Precedent: a lone reviewer at a small customer is the worst case in that literature, so the tool must supply the peer layer |
| Scola v. Facebook settlement (2020) | $52 million paid to US content moderators with PTSD and other long-term harms, with associated tooling commitments including a reviewer preference pane for blur and audio muting, screening, licensed counselor access and group sessions | Legal precedent for employer duty of care | Settled 2020 | Precedent: tooling changes are part of a legal remedy, not a perk. Any product that puts a human in a queue inherits this duty |
| Meta / Sama Kenya litigation | TIME (Feb 2022) documented a 50-second average handling time target (36-70s by workload), an 84% weekly quality target, an instruction to watch only the first 15 seconds of a video, manager-controlled wellness breaks and pay around $1.46-$2.20 an hour. Motaung sued May 2022; Sama closed the hub Jan 2023; 184 moderators sued. The ELRC enjoined terminations and ordered salaries paid; the High Court ordered proper medical, psychiatric and psychological care on 2023-07-31; the Court of Appeal held Meta can be sued in Kenya on 2024-09-20 | Precedent for platform liability for outsourced reviewers | Ongoing; no merits ruling as of Feb 2026 | Precedent: court-ordered psychiatric care is now a baseline, and the 50-second target is the documented cause. The SLA must be a queue latency target, never a per-case quota, and clinical support must be reachable from inside the tool |
| TikTok / Majorel Kenya (Odhiambo) | Former Majorel Nairobi moderator alleges PTSD from 250-350 graphic videos an hour across eight-hour shifts, 30 minutes a day with uncertified wellness counsellors, no ability to decline content, and dismissal after organizing; a June 2023 demand letter carried 14 demands | Precedent for BPO-side claims | Unknown after 2023 | Precedent: quantifies per-hour exposure that no wellness program offsets, and supports a hard per-hour cap on case openings |
| Meta / Majorel Ghana claims | About 150 moderators at a previously secret Accra hub; allegations of exposure to murders and CSAM with unhelpful support not delivered by doctors, personal disclosures circulated among managers, pay deductions for missed targets, sub-standard overtime, a moderator dismissed after a suicide attempt, and room-sharing in employer housing | Precedent | Suits in preparation as of 2025 | Precedent: wellness data must be confidential from managers by construction, and pay or rating must never key off case volume |
| Global Trade Union Alliance of Content Moderators (Nairobi, April 2025) | Cross-border alliance demanding eight protocols: exposure time limits, realistic quotas, 24/7 counseling, living wages, mental-health training, union rights, on-site psychological support and wellness breaks | Moderators employed by BPOs | Alive | Input: the eight protocols are a usable acceptance checklist for the review app |
| ROOST | Launched Feb 2025 with $28.5M in funding and in-kind contributions. Its April 2026 guidance defines investigation agents (gather evidence, no enforcement authority), assistant agents (reviewer-ready summaries, flag evidence gaps, never enforce) and production agents (narrow, high-confidence only, auto-escalate ambiguity), with reproducible auditing so a reviewer can reconstruct the evidence behind any recommendation | Small and mid platforms, fediverse, AI labs | Alive; 2026 priority is maturing Coop into an end-to-end human-in-the-loop platform | Partner and precedent: the agent-persona doctrine matches the rule that the model tops out at T2 |
| ROOST Coop (review console) | Queues with a Start Reviewing button, oldest-first serving, no job assigned to two moderators, starred queues with pending counts, per-queue Reviewer Access and Hidden Actions, a job page showing configured fields plus user account info, other content by the same user, report timestamp and type and reporter and reason and aggregated report count, default decisions Ignore / Enqueue to NCMEC / Move plus custom webhook actions, policies visible inside the job view, internal comments, a Require Decision Reason toggle, wellness settings (blur slider with hover unblur, grayscale, mute by default) as org defaults overridable per user, seven roles including Child Safety Moderator and read-only External Moderator, appeals reviewed as jobs with Uphold and Overturn, a Recent Decisions log filterable by policy, queue, moderator and date with export plus a separate export of skipped items, metrics for action volume and queue depth and automated-versus-human split and workload, and an investigation view. Child safety flow: HMA, Google Content Safety, user reports and manual enqueue all land in an NCMEC queue with user-centric aggregation and an open-case check, incident type and A1-B2 classification, file tags, an org minimum classification threshold, and storage of the NCMEC ID, CyberTip XML, media records and conversation CSVs | Platforms without a moderation stack | Alive; v1.0.1 June 2026 | Partner and precedent: the strongest argument for shipping as a signal plus bundle into Coop, and the clearest reference UI otherwise. Gaps: oldest-first only, no session limits or exposure accounting, no second-reviewer rule, no calibration, no undo, and wellness controls meaningless for text |
| ROOST Osprey | Real-time rules engine with an investigation UI: ~400M actions a day at Discord, 204 action types, 2,288 rules as of Dec 2025, rules in a Python-like SML, labels applied to users, servers and IPs, and a UI with event streams, time-series views and per-entity history. Bluesky runs 45M events a day with 100k+ automated actions | Safety engineers and analysts | Alive; 1.1.0 in 2026 | Precedent: entity history is the right mental model for a pair timeline, and shadow mode is the pattern for testing rules before they route to humans |
| Cove | Hosted moderation console with Notion as an early customer | Mid-size platforms | Acquired: IP acquired by ROOST and open-sourced as Coop, announced 2025-07-21 | Precedent: the generic console business did not survive as a standalone product |
| Cinder | Configurable queues and drag-and-drop workflows, signal aggregation, case cards with decision options, network-analysis case views, every decision logged with reasoning stored, RBAC and SSO, average-handle-time reporting, content blurring and grayscaling and moderator break prompts. QA: slice QA queues by content type, reviewer team, workflow or policy; grade-the-grader multi-layer review reconciling BPO, in-house and source-of-truth graders; per-moderator, team and policy scorecards; calibration loops that close in hours. Child safety: 10,000+ CyberTipline reports a week, reusable templates mapping platform data to CyberTipline fields, reviewer context added at decision time, related media bundled in one report, and NCMEC access requiring explicit admin authorization | Mid-to-large platforms and AI labs | Alive; joined IWF in 2026 | Competitor for the review surface, input for QA and bundle design. Its "every child safety report is a handoff" essay is the best articulation of what an investigator-ready bundle needs |
| Meta reviewer tooling and content-review-filters | Routing to reviewers with relevant expertise, ranking by potential offline harm and virality, 80+ hours of instructor-led training, sampled decision audits, a reviewer feedback tool for policy gaps, region-specific slur and dangerous-org highlighting, tooltips, and reviewer controls to mute videos, blur images or view in black and white. Also open-sourced content-review-filters: React components for configurable blur, grayscale, sepia, transparency, WebGL stylized illustration, playback speed, frame skipping, auto-mute on play, harm-type-specific filter intensity and persisted preferences | Meta's ~15k reviewers; any React review tool | Alive; library actively maintained | Input: reuse the highlighting-plus-tooltip pattern for lexicon hits and the harm-type-specific default model. No public session limits or exposure accounting |
| Discord safety operations and Moderator Academy | Blends user reports, image hashing and ML, network-analysis models, human investigation, server-level AutoMod, Teen Safety Assist alerts and NCMEC reporting; donated Osprey to ROOST. Moderator Academy guidance for volunteers covers burnout signs, permission to take breaks, rotating channel assignments, splitting large teams into specialized sub-teams and reviewing past mod actions for consistency | Discord's safety team and server moderators | Alive | Partner: the mod-channel alerts should follow Academy norms and never push imagery into a server |
| NCMEC analyst triage and Case Management Tool | 2025: 21.3M reports, 18.8M+ referred versus 4.5M+ informational, 53,000+ escalated as urgent. The 2024 report describes about 50 reports a day marked urgent by ESPs plus about 1,400 a day flagged by NCMEC systems as potentially time-sensitive, all manually reviewed. Analysts label files by content type, estimated age range, violence, bestiality and infants or toddlers: 14.6M files in 2025 and 77.1M cumulative, with hash matching preventing re-viewing and an image viewed at least three times before its hash is shared. The CMT gives law enforcement dashboards, customizable displays and entity-link visualization in eight languages | ESPs, the public, 170 countries of law enforcement | Alive | Partner and input: the hash-then-label pipeline is the canonical exposure-reduction design, and referral versus informational plus urgent flags is a de facto tiering scheme a T3 bundle must map to |
| ICAC task-force triage (per Stanford, April 2024) | Officers stretched incredibly thin; one local officer personally owns 240 actionable reports a year; a 100,000-population city gets 18-50 reports a year with about 12 active alongside homicides; triage is "more of an art than a science"; single-image tips rated lowest priority repeatedly produce hands-on-abuse arrests; officers prioritize familiar names and platforms where culpability is obvious; reports range "from bike theft to intelligence gold" and nothing says which. Blockers include needing a warrant to open files, 90-day retention expiring before warrants land, changing data formats and incomplete report fields. Rotation every year or so and high attrition | Investigators; platforms deciding what to put in a report | Report published 2024-04-22 | Input and partner: the bundle should carry linkage, a reviewer hunch field, timezone-explicit timestamps and a preservation flag, and the triage surface should rank by linkage and victim risk rather than arrival time |
| Altitude (Jigsaw / Tech Against Terrorism) | Apache-2.0 review console for terrorist and violent extremist content matched against TCAP and the GIFCT hash database: a list view for triage, a detail view per case, approve and block buttons, and blur applied by default | Small platforms without TVEC tooling | Alive | Precedent: a two-view console with default-obscured content is enough for a small operator, which is Guardian's target customer |
| Ozone (Bluesky) | Next.js labeling and moderation UI for AT Protocol: view, triage, escalate and action reports; takedowns and suspensions; label creation; browse removed content; templated moderation emails; a Ctrl-K quick-action modal | Bluesky and independent labelers | Alive | Precedent: escalate-to-second-reviewer and keyboard quick actions belong in the T2 queue |
| Tremau Nima | Moderation platform centralizing signals for single-click manual and automated moderation, with a dashboard showing pending reports, appeals and queue statistics | Mid-size platforms under the DSA; Bluesky, Pinterest, Alibaba | Alive | Competitor-adjacent; relevant only for EU platforms needing DSA artifacts |
| Checkstep | AI moderation with a customisable policy engine, ModBot automation with human-in-the-loop review, incident and review analytics and DSA transparency reporting; claims 90% lower human moderation cost and 78% faster moderation | Platforms seeking automation first | Alive | Irrelevant except as a pricing comparison; the automation-first pitch runs against the human-only T3 rule |
| Zevo Health | Moderator wellbeing services vendor. Its April 2026 analysis of the UW study concludes exposure hours were not a robust predictor while culture, supervision, recovery time and work design were, and recommends redesigning work around human capacity limits, supervisor training to spot deterioration, credible escalation before strain becomes illness, and interface changes to reduce intrusive-memory formation | BPOs and platforms | Alive | Input: supports building recovery time and supervisor visibility of load, not of content, into the tool |

**Themes.**

Everyone ships the same image kit: blur, grayscale and mute by default with a hover reveal, a per-reviewer preference pane, a decision log and sampled QA. None of it protects a text-only reviewer, and the one controlled text study showed no measured affect gain, so Guardian's text protections are unproven and must be measured in the eval harness.

Nobody ships session limits, exposure budgets, severity-weighted pacing, second-reviewer confirmation or in-tool calibration. Coop's QA is roadmap, its queues serve oldest-first, and it has no undo. The best evidence says organization and workflow design dominate outcomes once exposure is held to 2-4 hours a day, so enforcing pacing and a two-person rule is a differentiator no console currently offers.

Triage, not detection, is the bottleneck at every hop. NCMEC hand-reviews about 1,450 flagged reports a day; ICAC officers triage by feel because nothing in a report distinguishes bike theft from intelligence gold; over 10% of industry reports lack jurisdiction.

Where the money is: AI agents that auto-action, DSA compliance artifacts, CSAM reporting pipelines, and BPO seats for roughly 100,000 first-level moderators. Generic queues are being priced to zero by ROOST, and Cove sold its IP rather than survive as a console.

What practitioners complain about: quotas and handling-time targets, productivity measured by volume or time, protective features that exist but go unused, no control over intensity, unclear guidelines causing decision fatigue, wellness time of nine minutes a day, counselors and disclosures leaking to managers, tools that cannot link reports, 90-day retention expiring before warrants, and no undo.

Litigation and labor are converging on employer duties, from the $52M Scola settlement through Kenya's court-ordered care and the union alliance's eight protocols. Any operator who staffs a Guardian queue inherits that duty, so exposure accounting, breaks, confidential screening and a counseling pathway are risk reduction for customers.

Human review is being repositioned from production line to quality function, which matches the human-only T3 rule but raises the stakes on each decision. That is why reason taxonomies, second-reviewer confirmation and hash-chained audit are core rather than optional.

### 2.7 Legal and regulatory, 2026

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 18 USC 2258A as amended by the REPORT Act (Pub. L. 118-59, 2024-05-07) | Requires any provider to report to the CyberTipline as soon as reasonably possible after obtaining actual knowledge of an apparent violation of 2251, 2251A, 2252, 2252A, 2252B, 2260, 1591 (if a minor) and 2422(b) (enticement). Reporting facts indicating a violation may be planned or imminent is permitted but optional. Subsection (h): preserve report contents for 1 year, up from 90 days, with NIST CSF-conformant handling since 2025-05-07. Subsection (f): nothing requires a provider to monitor or affirmatively search, screen or scan. Penalties for knowing and willful failure: $600,000 under 100M MAU or $850,000 above, rising to $850,000 / $1,000,000 for subsequent violations | US ECS/RCS providers; NCMEC; law enforcement | In force | Input and precedent. Enticement and minor trafficking are now mandatory categories, which is exactly the T3 output class. The 1-year timer starts at submission, bundles should carry the statutory basis, and the audit chain should record that detection was the customer's own volitional choice under 2258A(f) |
| 18 USC 2258B / 2258C | 2258B gives providers and registrars civil and criminal immunity for actions arising from reporting or preservation responsibilities, except intentional misconduct, actual malice, reckless disregard of a substantial risk of physical injury, or unrelated acts, and requires minimizing employee access to reported depictions. The 2024 amendments extended immunity to NCMEC-contracted vendors and to survivors reporting their own exploitation. 2258C lets NCMEC provide hash values to providers | Providers, NCMEC vendors, survivors | In force | Precedent. Immunity runs to the provider, not to a third-party detection vendor, so the processor agreement should recite that Guardian performs reporting or preservation responsibilities on the provider's behalf, and Guardian should never hold depictions |
| 18 USC 2252 / 2252A | Criminalizes knowing receipt, possession, distribution and access with intent to view of CSAM. The only affirmative defense for possession is fewer than three items promptly destroyed or reported without allowing access. No detection, research or vendor exception exists for a non-provider | Everyone except providers within 2258A/2258B and law enforcement | In force, unchanged | Constraint. A non-provider vendor that receives bytes has no safe harbor, and hash-only also keeps Guardian out of the private-search chain, so the bundle should carry a per-media-hash flag recording whether an operator human viewed the file |
| TAKE IT DOWN Act (Pub. L. 119-12) and FTC enforcement | Signed 2025-05-19. Criminal provisions effective immediately: knowingly publishing nonconsensual intimate visual depictions including AI digital forgeries, and threatening to publish them. Section 3 platform duties effective 2026-05-19: a clear and conspicuous notice-and-removal process, removal within 48 hours of a valid request, and reasonable efforts to identify and remove known identical copies, with a good-faith safe harbor. The FTC enforces as a UDAP violation with civil penalties of $53,088 per violation, sent warning letters to at least 15 companies before the deadline, and runs TakeItDown.ftc.gov. First criminal conviction April 2026 | Victims of NCII; covered platforms; FTC and DOJ | In force since 2026-05-19 | Input. A sextortion threat to publish is now a standalone federal crime, which strengthens treating threat-template matches as critical. Do not build a takedown feature: identical-copy matching means handling bytes, which belongs on the operator side |
| Amended COPPA Rule (16 CFR Part 312) | Final amendments published 2025-04-22, effective 2025-06-23, full compliance 2026-04-22 except three safe-harbor provisions. Personal information now includes biometric and government-issued identifiers; separate verifiable parental consent is required for third-party disclosure unless integral to the service; a public written data retention policy must state purposes, business need and deletion timeframes with no indefinite retention; a written information security program with annual risk assessment is required, plus written assurances from third-party vendors. The 312.5(c) exceptions survive, covering a child's and parent's name and online contact information for child safety, security, liability, judicial process, and providing information to law enforcement or for a public-safety investigation. An FTC policy statement of 2026-02-25 permits collecting children's data for age verification without parental consent under conditions | Operators of child-directed services and their vendors | Enforceable in full since 2026-04-22 | Constraint. Customers must obtain a written vendor assurance from Guardian, the retention policy must be public and time-bounded per class, and NCMEC disclosure rests on the statutory reporting mandate rather than on a COPPA exception |
| Kids Online Safety Act (KOSA), S. 1748 | Reintroduced 2025-05-14 with 75 cosponsors; Senate Commerce reported it by voice vote on 2026-08-05 via a Blackburn substitute alongside the Youth AI Privacy Act, the CHATBOT Act and the Children's AI Toy Safety Act. Core provisions: a duty of care covering sexual exploitation and abuse, default safeguards including limits on communications from non-connected adults, parental tools, transparency reports and independent audits, FTC and state AG enforcement | Minors and parents; covered platforms including small games and messaging apps | Alive, not law; no Senate floor vote or House action found through late Aug 2026 | Input. If enacted, small games and messaging apps get a reasonable-measures duty, which is Guardian's market. The adult-to-minor contact default maps onto age-gap gating |
| STOP CSAM Act of 2025 (S. 1829) and the FY2027 Senate NDAA | Would create a civil cause of action for minor victims against providers and app stores for intentional, knowing or reckless promotion or hosting of CSAM; make reports of planned or imminent violations mandatory; set a 60-day outer bound on reporting; require annual transparency reports from providers above 1M monthly visitors and $50M revenue; impose civil penalties of $50,000-$250,000 for reporting violations and $100,000-$1,000,000 for missing annual reports; and state that end-to-end encryption cannot serve as an independent basis for liability. Hawley announced its inclusion in the Senate NDAA manager's amendment on 2026-07-14, the same day the motion to proceed was blocked 50-46 | Victims, providers, NCMEC | Alive, not law | Input. Would convert grooming-in-progress into a mandatory report category, and its reckless standard makes an ignored T2 alert a liability event for the customer, which is a sales argument for logging dispositions |
| Private search doctrine: Wilson (9th Cir. 2021), Maher (2d Cir. 2024), Lowers (4th Cir. 2026) versus Reddick (5th Cir. 2018), Miller (6th Cir. 2020); Ackerman (10th Cir. 2016) | Wilson: when a provider's hash match is the only search and no employee viewed the file, police opening it exceeds the private search and needs a warrant. Maher agreed but admitted the evidence under good faith, and found Google was not a government agent. Lowers joined the restrictive side. Reddick and Miller hold a reliable hash match gives virtual certainty. Ackerman holds NCMEC is a governmental entity or agent, so NCMEC opening attachments the provider never opened is a government search | Defendants, prosecutors, providers designing report pipelines | Split unresolved | Precedent. Whatever a human at the provider actually viewed defines what law enforcement may view without a warrant. Text evidence in a T3 bundle is human-viewed by definition; media needs a per-hash human-viewed flag, and T3 should never be producible from machine-only evidence |
| United States v. Rosenow (9th Cir. 2022) | Held that Yahoo and Facebook did not become government agents by searching accounts and filing CyberTips, because 2258A(f) says the statute shall not be construed to require monitoring or affirmative searching, so mandated reporting is different than mandated searching, and a private actor does not become a government agent by complying with a mandatory reporting statute. The agency test asks whether the government knew of and acquiesced in the search and whether the party intended to assist law enforcement or acted for its own independent purpose. Preservation requests under 2703(f) are not seizures | Providers and vendors deciding how much to coordinate with law enforcement | Good law; consistent across circuits | Precedent, and the correct citation for the no-tasking rule. Keep an explicit separation between the investigator surface and the kernel, and record in the audit chain that thresholds and lexicon changes originate from the operator or Guardian, never from a law-enforcement request |
| ECPA / Wiretap Act, state all-party consent, and vicarious consent (Pollock, 6th Cir. 1998) | Federal law is one-party consent. Twelve states are commonly listed as all-party consent (California, Connecticut, Delaware, Florida, Illinois, Maryland, Massachusetts, Montana, New Hampshire, Oregon, Pennsylvania, Washington) with nuances. Pollock adopted the Thompson v. Dulaney test: a parent may consent for a minor child where the parent has a good faith, objectively reasonable basis for believing it is necessary and in the best interest of the child, and warned it is not a blanket exception invoked with magic words and applies to one's own child | Parents, monitoring vendors, anyone recording communications | Unchanged | Constraint. Overt, child-visible, device-owner monitoring is the strongest posture, but the vendor exposure is 18 USC 2512 and FTC stalkerware precedent rather than vicarious consent, so the parent app must be architecturally incapable of covert use |
| Section 230 in 2026 | 230(c)(1) bars treating a service as publisher of third-party content. 230(c)(2)(B) immunizes any action taken to enable or make available to others the technical means to restrict access to objectionable material, and Zango v. Kaspersky (9th Cir. 2009) applied it to filtering-software providers; Enigma v. Malwarebytes (9th Cir. 2019) held "otherwise objectionable" does not cover blocking for anticompetitive reasons. Anderson v. TikTok (3d Cir. 2024) held algorithmic recommendations are the platform's own expressive activity outside (c)(1); Commonwealth v. Meta (Mass. SJC, April 2026) denied dismissal because harms stemmed from design features and deceptive safety statements; a California JCCP bellwether jury found design-defect liability in March 2026. 230(e)(1) preserves federal criminal law including chapter 110 | Platforms, safety-tool vendors, plaintiffs | Statute unchanged; case law narrowing (c)(1) for design and first-party statements | Precedent. (c)(2)(B) protects the blocking function of a filtering tool, but nothing protects a factual assertion about a person, so publish measured PPV ranges with dates and model versions and forbid customers from advertising Guardian as a safety guarantee |
| State App Store Accountability Acts and California AB 1043 | Texas SB 2420 effective 2026-01-01: app stores verify age and obtain parental consent for minors; developers accept age-category and consent signals, apply matching ratings and delete verification data after use. A district court enjoined it in Dec 2025; the Fifth Circuit stayed the injunction on 2026-05-28 and let the law stand pending appeal on 2026-06-05. Utah delayed obligations to 2027-05-06 with only a private right of action; Louisiana delayed to 2027-07-01; Alabama starts 2027-01-01. California AB 1043 (signed 2025-10-13, effective 2027-01-01) has operating-system providers give apps a self-declared age-bracket signal. The common bracket set is under 13, 13-15, 16-17, 18+ | App stores, OS providers, developers | Texas enforceable pending appeal; others 2027 | Input. A standardized four-bracket age signal will be delivered by the platform layer, so Guardian should carry a derived four-bracket field plus a provenance enum. Purpose limitation means safety use must be documented as in scope |
| State Age-Appropriate Design Codes and minor social-media laws | California AADC: the Ninth Circuit partially vacated the injunction on 2026-03-12, so the coverage definition, age-estimation requirement and some data-use provisions are in effect while the dark-patterns provision stays enjoined. Maryland's Kids Code took effect 2024-10-01; Nebraska's AADC 2026-01-01 with AG penalties from 2026-07-01; Vermont's 2027-01-01 with no size threshold and coverage at a 2% under-18 audience. Florida HB 3 is enforceable, Ohio HB 33 became enforceable after the Sixth Circuit vacated its injunction on 2026-06-18, California SB 976 was largely upheld in Nov 2025, and Utah and Virginia laws are enjoined | Online services likely to be accessed by minors | Patchwork | Input. Data protection impact assessments and reasonable-care duties reach small services, which creates demand for documented safety measures. Produce a DPIA-ready description of Guardian processing as a standard customer deliverable |
| UK Online Safety Act 2023 | Applies to user-to-user and search services with links to the UK wherever based. Illegal content risk assessments were due 2025-03-16 with duties enforceable from 2025-03-17, covering priority offences including CSEA and grooming. Children's access assessments were due 2025-04-16, children's risk assessments by 2025-07-24 and child safety measures from 2025-07-25, including highly effective age assurance, content moderation systems, easy complaints and recommender exclusions. Penalties reach GBP 18 million or 10% of qualifying worldwide revenue plus senior manager criminal liability. By October 2025 Ofcom had five enforcement programmes and 21 investigations; AVS Group was fined GBP 1 million plus GBP 50,000 on 2025-12-04 | Any service with UK users, including small games and chat apps | In force | Input. Ship an OSA-oriented risk-assessment annex describing which grooming risk factors Guardian mitigates, and support children's-default logic. Discord server owners are not the provider under the OSA; Discord is |
| EU interim ePrivacy derogation and the CSA Regulation | The derogation let interpersonal communications services voluntarily detect known CSAM, new CSAM and grooming without breaching ePrivacy. It expired 2026-04-03 after the co-legislators failed to agree on a clean extension, removing the legal basis for roughly four months. Regulation (EU) 2026/1881, signed 2026-07-24 and published 2026-07-28, entered into force 2026-07-31 and applies until 2028-04-03; it expressly covers solicitation of children, with stricter conditions: no systematic text filtering except to detect patterns pointing to concrete suspicion, limitation to key indicators and objectively identified risk factors such as age difference and likely child involvement, GDPR Article 36(2) prior consultation, and mandatory prior human confirmation before any report. It also excludes communications to which end-to-end encryption is, has been or will be applied. The permanent CSA Regulation remains in trilogue with the next session set for 2026-09-29 | Providers of number-independent interpersonal communications services in the EU | Interim cover restored July 2026 | Constraint. Per-customer jurisdiction and legal-basis fields on the ingest config, an EU flag governing private-DM text scoring, an end-to-end-encryption flag, and a documented public-versus-private channel distinction |
| Australia Online Safety Amendment (Social Media Minimum Age) Act 2024 | From 2025-12-10 age-restricted platforms must take reasonable steps to stop Australians under 16 from creating or keeping accounts: Facebook, Instagram, Kick, Reddit, Snapchat, Threads, TikTok, Twitch, X, YouTube. Services whose sole or primary purpose is messaging, gaming, professional networking, education or health are excluded, including Discord, Messenger, WhatsApp and Steam. Reasonable steps means layered age assurance, government ID cannot be the only method, and the OAIC enforces privacy limits on age-assurance data. Penalties up to AUD 49.5 million. 4.7 million under-16 accounts were removed or restricted by mid-December 2025 | Age-restricted platforms; Australian minors and parents | In force | Input. The cleanest real-world data point on displacement: under-16s pushed off mainstream social media land on gaming and messaging, exactly Guardian's surfaces, which carry no new duties |
| NCMEC CyberTipline ESP Reporting API | XML over HTTPS with basic auth; credentials must be requested from NCMEC with no self-registration. Production and test hosts; flow POST /submit, /upload, /fileinfo, /finish, /retract. Open reports auto-delete after 24 hours or one hour after last modification. Incident types include Child Pornography, Online Enticement of Children for Sexual Acts, Child Sex Trafficking, Child Sex Tourism, Child Sexual Molestation, Misleading Domain Names and Unsolicited Obscene Material Sent to a Child; a sextortion flag is an optional annotation. Legally only incident type and date and time are mandatory. Hive's integration requires the customer's own credentials | Registered ESPs | Alive; docs current as of 2026-08-26 | The reporting path. Text-only enticement reports need no file upload, and each ESP holds its own account, so Guardian stores per-customer credentials and submits as agent |
| Discord Privileged Intent review (from 2026-06-10) | The review trigger is 10,000 unique users who can see the app across all its servers, replacing the 100-server rule. Below the threshold, Message Content, Server Members and Presence are Developer Portal toggles; above it the app must apply within 90 days of notification, reapply annually, and keeps working during review. The application requires a specific use case and a data-handling statement: either why storage is necessary with a retention policy and security measures, or confirmation of in-memory processing. Bot verification at 100 servers remains separate | Discord app developers | In force | Constraint. One 10,000-member Roblox community server crosses the threshold alone, so the written retention policy is a week-1 artifact, not a week-10 one |

**Themes.**

Age signals are the one thing every regime is converging on: Texas app-store brackets now, California OS brackets in 2027, the California AADC age-estimation duty live since April 2026, UK highly effective age assurance, Australia's under-16 ban, and the FTC's February 2026 COPPA policy statement. The de facto US schema is under 13, 13-15, 16-17, 18+, so Guardian's six bands should carry a derived four-bracket field and a provenance enum.

Nobody mandates scanning; everybody mandates reporting and reasonable measures. 2258A(f) disclaims any search duty, the EU Council dropped mandatory detection orders, KOSA is a duty of care, and the OSA asks for proportionate measures. That volitional framing is what keeps providers and their vendors outside the government-agent test, and it means Guardian sells as a documented reasonable measure rather than a compliance mandate.

Liability has moved from third-party content to first-party design and first-party statements. For Guardian the legal controls are wording and accuracy: risk tiers not labels, measured PPV with model versions, and contract terms stopping customers from advertising Guardian as a guarantee. Section 230(c)(2)(B) and Zango still protect the blocking function of a filtering tool.

Human-viewed is the evidence question of the decade. Bundles should record exactly what the reviewer read and carry a per-hash operator human-viewed flag so downstream investigators know when to get a warrant.

Where the money is: compliance paperwork for the long tail. Small platforms now owe DPIAs, OSA risk assessments, COPPA security programs and vendor assurances, Discord intent applications with retention policies, and eventually KOSA audits. NCMEC's data shows the long tail is not reporting at all, which is Guardian's gap; enforcement so far targets adult sites and big brands, so the demand driver is customer due diligence rather than fear of fines.

What practitioners complain about is whiplash. Utah and Louisiana enacted then delayed; Texas was enjoined then un-enjoined; the EU lost its legal basis for four months and restored it through a procedural maneuver; Discord moved its threshold with a 90-day clock; NCMEC onboarding is an email. The design consequence is jurisdiction and legal-basis fields per customer, feature flags per surface, and no hard-coded assumptions about thresholds or effective dates.

Displacement cuts toward Guardian's surfaces. Australia's ban and app-store gating exempt messaging and gaming, so under-16s concentrate on Discord, Roblox and game chat, exactly the services with no new duties and, below Roblox's size, no detection.

### 2.8 Case files, 2025-2026

| Name | What it does | Who it serves | Status 2026 | Relevance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bloomberg Businessweek, "Roblox Is Fighting to Keep Pedophiles Away and Not Always Winning" | July 2024 investigation on 20+ current and former employees plus court records; at least 24 US arrests since 2018. Established that the word grooming did not appear in Roblox's 2022 moderation guide and that no automated proactive grooming detection existed before 2022 | Source material for state AG complaints, MDL 3166 pleadings and the Senate letter | Alive; the origin document for the 2025-2026 legal wave | Input. The richest public source of ground-truth trajectories: Robux bait, migration to Discord and Snapchat, image production, and in some cases abduction. Documents "abc for girl" solicitations and dark-web tactic sharing |
| Arnold Castillo (DoctorRofatnik) | A prominent Roblox developer groomed a 15-year-old on Roblox, moved her to Discord, then paid an Uber driver $1,000 to transport her across state lines, where she was held and assaulted. Sentenced to 15 years in 2023; a Feb 2025 civil suit names Roblox, Discord and Uber and alleges his behavior was publicly discussed among Roblox users for months before the abduction | Minor victim, 15F; offender was a trusted, high-status creator | Criminal case closed 2023; civil case in the MDL wave | Input, and the strongest argument for two features: role whitelists must raise rather than suppress review priority when a developer or moderator account shows stage 3 or 4 with a younger band, and operator-supplied community-report counts should be an actor-level graph feature |
| Shane Patrick Penczak | Obtained a 13-year-old boy's Roblox password on the pretext of loading gift cards, then signed in over three years to read his private messages and pay him thousands of dollars in Robux, receiving hundreds of images and videos. Sentenced to 13 years | Minor victim, 13M | Closed | Input. Adds credential and account-sharing solicitation as a discrete high-precision signal, and currency transfer direction as a first-class economic-bait event rather than a text mention. A three-year relationship needs a slow-trajectory detector |
| Clinton McElroy | A 48-year-old registered sex offender met an 8-year-old girl on Roblox in 2020 and traded thousands of Robux for 20+ self-produced explicit images and videos | Minor victim, 8F | Closed | Input. Validates the age-gap multiplier as the highest-leverage term: currency transfer plus band gap plus a media event should be T2 without any explicit sexual token |
| Louisiana AG v. Roblox (Murrill) | Filed 2025-08-14, the first state AG suit: unfair trade practices, negligence, public nuisance, unjust enrichment. Cites named user-generated experiences and a July 2025 Livingston Parish arrest of a man using voice-altering software to pose as a teenage girl while on Roblox | Louisiana minors | Alive; pending | Market driver. Voice-altering software is a documented in-the-wild evasion of any age or voice signal, and voice is a surface Guardian does not ingest |
| Texas AG v. Roblox (Paxton) | Filed early Nov 2025 under the Texas Deceptive Trade Practices Act and the SCOPE Act, alleging Roblox said it takes every precaution possible while failing to implement meaningful age verification | Texas minors and parents | Alive; pending | Market driver. The deception theory means the gap between marketing claims and actual capability is itself the liability, which is a direct buying trigger and an argument for the hash-chained audit log |
| State AG wave against Roblox | Rolling sequence Apr 2025 to Aug 2026: Florida subpoenas, Louisiana, Oklahoma outside counsel, Kentucky, Texas, South Carolina, Iowa, Tennessee, Georgia, Nebraska (Mar 2026, "playground" for predators), Indiana (May 2026, a 70-page suit naming Roblox and Discord), Connecticut, Arkansas (June 2026: Roblox "left the front door unlocked, and Discord handed predators a private room"), LA County, and an Ohio class action alleging investor deception and $12.5M state pension losses | State residents; functionally, the plaintiffs' bar | Alive and expanding | Market driver. The Arkansas and Indiana framing is the Roblox-to-Discord handoff, which is exactly the cross-platform pair the kernel is shaped for |
| Roblox state settlements | Nevada ~$12.5M (2026-04-15, including a law-enforcement liaison), Alabama $12.2M plus West Virginia $11.08M (2026-04-21), Mississippi $9M plus a $5M penalty guarantee (2026-06-22), South Dakota $8.6M or $15M depending on source (2026-07-13), requiring age verification for all users | State programs, ICAC task forces, school resource officers | Executed; more expected | Precedent. Sets the price of not having detection. South Dakota's all-user age-verification term is the first settlement to mandate an architecture, and Nevada's liaison term shows regulators want a named human in the reporting path |
| In re: Roblox Corporation Child Sexual Exploitation and Assault Litigation, MDL No. 3166 | December 2025 JPML transfer order centralizing Roblox child sexual exploitation cases in the Northern District of California before Chief Judge Richard Seeborg; 182 actions pending and 187 total as of 2026-09-01. The common allegation is contact on Roblox, trust, migration to Discord where parental visibility drops, and escalation there | Plaintiff families | Alive; discovery phase | Precedent and input. The uniform Roblox-to-Discord pattern across ~180 independently sourced pleadings is the empirical justification for a platform-agnostic, pair-centric kernel |
| Roblox v. Schlep | 2025-08-09: Roblox permanently banned and IP-banned the YouTuber and sent a cease-and-desist under a new vigilante-groups policy, on grounds of simulating child-endangerment conversations, soliciting or sharing personal information, moving conversations to Discord and other services, and delaying reports until after the confrontation video was produced. Stock fell over 10%; Rep. Khanna launched a petition; Chris Hansen released a documentary on 2026-02-27 | Nobody cleanly | Alive; Schlep announced intent to counter-sue | Precedent, and the defining one. Roblox published the exact taxonomy of prohibited third-party conduct, which is a free compliance checklist. Its own detection had not caught the people Schlep caught, which is the capability gap |
