| Roblox v. Benjamin Simon (Ruben Sim) | Roblox sued for $1.6M in Nov 2021 alleging he led a cult-like cybermob and posted false terrorist threats; settled at $150,000 with a permanent account ban. Simon had earlier pushed Roblox to act on the Sonic Eclipse developer | Precedent for platform-versus-critic litigation | Closed since ~2022 | Precedent. Guardian must be a vendor under operator authority, never an independent observer publishing findings |
| Senate Judiciary investigation into Roblox (Hawley / Durbin) | Letter to CEO David Baszucki dated 2026-08-12, announced 2026-08-13, disclosing that Roblox filed 65,381 CyberTipline reports in 2025, more than double 2024 (24,522). Frames four harms: trading CSAM, sexually explicit experiences involving children, bribing and extorting children into producing explicit content, and luring children into in-person meetings. Documents demanded by 2026-08-31 | Congressional oversight | Alive | Input. 65,381 reports against 100M+ DAU is a usable base-rate anchor for reviewer-minute budgeting, and the senators' careful "suspected, not confirmed" framing is the risk-tier-not-accusation posture |
| Roblox Facial Age Estimation and Trusted Connections | Announced 2025-07-17, launched 2025-11-18, mandatory in Australia, New Zealand and the Netherlands from 2025-12-01 and global from Jan 2026. A selfie video is estimated into one of six bands and images are deleted after processing; chat is gated by band. Trusted Connections lets age-checked users add people they know, with an adult adding a verified teen requiring a QR code or phone number. Persona reports ACCS-tested MAE of 1.4 years for under-18s | All Roblox users | Alive; now subject to a mandatory third-party audit under the Australian undertaking | Input and dependency. Guardian must accept a confidence or MAE alongside the band and accept an operator-supplied trusted-connection flag as a prior rather than an override, since Castillo-style offenders will become trusted connections |
| Roblox Sentinel | In production since late 2024, open-sourced Aug 2025 under Apache-2.0: a positive index of communications from users with no safety violations and a negative index of about 13,000 flagged child-endangerment exemplars, scored by cosine similarity over one-minute snapshots across 6B+ daily messages, tracking skewness rather than the mean. ~1,200 NCMEC reports in H1 2025 with 35% of detected cases proactive; v2 contributed to ROOST in Aug 2026 with six score-combining functions | Roblox internally; any platform willing to build exemplar sets | Alive | Input and partial competitor. It confirms the skew approach is what the largest deployment actually does, and publishes the only real production numbers. It has no ordered pair trajectory, and cold-start on 13,000 negative exemplars is the whole cost |
| eSafety Australia court-enforceable undertaking with Roblox | eSafety ran adversarial testing with accounts presented as adults and as young children, finding that adult strangers could send connection requests to young children without parental consent, that children's profile and connection information was broadly visible, and that adult-child interaction occurred in some forums. Roblox signed on 2026-08-17 (announcement 2026-08-19/20) and has three months to stop adults contacting unknown children without parental consent, make children's accounts private by default, and improve complaint reporting and outcome notification. It must hire an independent third-party auditor to assess safety-measure effectiveness including age estimation | Australian children; every regulator watching | Alive; three-month clock, external audit pending | Precedent and market driver. Adversarial red-team testing by a regulator is the methodology that will be applied to everyone next, and independent third-party audit of safety-measure effectiveness is a procurement category that did not exist before August 2026 |
| New Jersey AG v. Discord (Platkin) | Filed 2025-04-17 under the Consumer Fraud Act. The centerpiece is the Safe Direct Messaging setting with three options, whose default for new users scanned direct messages from everyone unless they were a friend. The complaint alleges Discord promised automatic scanning and deletion of explicit media in DMs while knowing not all explicit content was detected or deleted, that any user could send friend requests by default, that age gating was a self-entered birthdate, and that banned users including prior CSAM distributors simply re-registered with a new email | New Jersey minors | Alive; pending | Design validation. Grooming happens after the friend accept, so scanning everyone except friends scans the wrong population: Guardian must score accepted pairs at full weight, and alt-account clustering must survive email churn |
| Discord teen-by-default and UK age assurance | UK age verification live 2025-07-25 under the Online Safety Act via k-ID face scan or ID check. From early March 2026 teen-appropriate settings became default for all new and existing users globally: stricter privacy, limited DMs from non-friends, content filtering. Significant privacy backlash and widely circulated bypass guides | All Discord users; teen accounts | Alive | Dependency. Teen-by-default gives the Discord surface an age-band input it did not have, but the band must carry provenance and confidence and must never suppress a critical signal |
| United States v. Bermudez et al. (Greggy's Cult) | Indictment unsealed 2025-12-02 charging five defendants with conspiracy to produce and distribute child pornography and to communicate interstate threats, for conduct from Jan 2020 to Jan 2021. Members met on Discord servers, found victims on gaming platforms including Roblox and Counter-Strike, then directed minors who joined a video call to perform sexually explicit or degrading conduct and blackmailed them into self-harm | Minor and adult victims across multiple states | Alive; charges pending | Input and an honest limitation. Group-structured offending needs a cohort feature (fan-IN: many actors converging on one minor target), and escalation to video calls is where a text-only kernel goes blind |
| 764 / COM network | A loosely federated network across Discord, Telegram and Roblox with splinter groups. Members sextort minors into producing CSAM then coerce escalating violence, animal abuse and self-harm, with status earned through displays of sadism. By 2025 documentation shifted toward "cutsigns," images of a victim carving a member's name into their skin, and entry requirements moved toward vandalism, arson and real-world violence. 191 members arrested across 28 countries 2020-2025; FBI and DOJ classify it as a Tier One threat | Targets vulnerable minors, often those already disclosing self-harm | Alive and mutating toward real-world violence | Input. Forces a non-financial coercion class where the demand is self-harm or a fansign rather than money, plus a target-side vulnerability-disclosure signal. Fansign and cutsign templates belong in the critical-signal override list |
| Jordan DeMay / United States v. Samuel and Samson Ogoshi | Jordan DeMay, 17, of Marquette Michigan died by suicide on 2022-03-25, roughly six hours after first contact. The Ogoshi brothers of Lagos bought hacked Instagram accounts, posed as a young woman, obtained a reciprocal explicit image, then demanded payment under threat of exposure. They targeted more than 100 victims; both were extradited from Nigeria in Aug 2023 and sentenced on 2024-09-05 to 210 months | Victim was a 17-year-old boy, matching the 90%-male 14-17 profile | Closed | Input, and the strongest argument for the critical-signal override. Contact to death inside a day proves a batch or daily-cadence detector is useless, and hacked purchased accounts mean account provenance, not account age, is the signal: an established account with a sudden behavioral break beats a 72-hour-old account |
| Ryan Last / Alfred Kassi and co-defendants | Ryan Last, 17, of San Jose died by suicide in February 2022 after paying $150. Kassi, who allegedly posed as a 20-year-old woman, was arrested in Cote d'Ivoire on 2025-04-29 with three co-defendants; the scheme targeted thousands of victims across the US, Canada, UK, France, Spain and Italy. Cote d'Ivoire does not extradite its citizens, so all four face prosecution under Ivorian cybercrime statutes | Victim was a 17-year-old boy; thousands more targeted with the same script | Alive; prosecution proceeding three years after the death | Input. One script against thousands of victims across six countries is the justification for a MinHash script index, and a small specific demand ($150) makes payment-entity extraction tractable. Three years to arrest with no extradition means detection and interruption are the only levers |
| EFCC extraditions and INTERPOL Operation Jackal IV | On 2026-08-26 Nigeria's EFCC extradited two men to the US to face prosecution for sexually extorting American teenage boys in schemes linked to two deaths. INTERPOL's Operation Jackal IV ran Nov 2025 to Jun 2026 across 22 countries and identified a rise in West African organised crime groups using sextortion against minors, with victims as young as 14 | US teenage boys | Alive | Input. Confirms the pattern is organised-crime infrastructure rather than lone actors, so catfish account provenance, script reuse and payment-handle reuse across pairs and customers are the three joins that matter |
| United States v. Richard Billingsley | A 21-year-old sentenced on 2026-08-31 to 21 years for grooming three pre-teens. He targeted them on Roblox and moved them to private Discord conversations. The investigation began in August 2025 when NCMEC flagged a user who had uploaded CSAM to Discord | Three pre-teen victims | Closed | Input, and the clearest illustration of the value proposition: the only detection that fired was a hash match on the last artifact, months after the stage 1 to 5 sequence produced zero alerts on either platform |
| New Mexico v. Snap Inc. (and v. Meta) | Filed September 2024; an unredacted complaint exposed internal Snap messages. The AG's undercover investigation found minors report more online sexual interactions on Snapchat than any other platform, that more sex trafficking victims are recruited there than anywhere else, and a network of dark-web sites sharing stolen non-consensual images with 10,000+ related records in a year. Internal messages allegedly show ignored sextortion reports, no verifiable age verification, and awareness that recommendation features connect minors with adults | New Mexico minors | Alive; both pending | Input and market driver. Ephemerality is itself the finding: disappearing messages remove the evidence a detector and a prosecutor need. Snapchat is the number-one named migration destination, which makes the migration-ask signal on the source platform the only detectable moment |
| Meta Yahoo Boys takedowns and Instagram anti-sextortion tooling | July 2024: removed ~63,000 Instagram accounts in Nigeria engaged in financial sextortion, including a coordinated network of ~2,500 accounts run by about 20 people, plus ~7,200 Facebook assets (1,300 accounts, 200 Pages, 5,700 Groups) sharing scripts, guides and fake-profile photo sets; a further ~1,600 in October 2024. Yahoo Boys were designated under the Dangerous Organizations policy. Product: on-device nudity blurring in Instagram DMs on by default for under-18s, follower and following lists hidden from suspected sextortion accounts, screenshot blocking, a test alert when a message comes from a different country, and detection of accounts likely engaged in sextortion | Teen Instagram users, predominantly boys 14-17 | Alive | Precedent and input. 5,700 Groups selling scripts and photo sets is direct evidence the sextortion corpus is a commodity, which is why script matching works. Hiding follower lists attacks the leverage rather than the content, which suggests an action tier that is friction rather than enforcement or reporting |
| NCMEC CyberTipline 2025 data | ~1.4 million online enticement reports, a 156-158% increase over 2024; an average of 137 financially motivated sextortion reports a day, up 37%; child sex trafficking reports rising to 113,530, a 323% increase over 2024 attributed largely to the REPORT Act; more than 1.5 million reports with a generative-AI nexus | Law enforcement, ESPs, researchers | Alive | Input. These are the denominators for the base-rate simulation, and the trafficking jump is a reporting-duty artifact rather than a prevalence change, which is exactly the caveat dashboards need after a version bump |
| Kik, Telegram, TikTok Live and second-tier surfaces | Sen. Blackburn opened an investigation into Kik for child exploitation in June 2026; Kik remains a standard undercover surface. Ofcom opened a formal investigation into Telegram on 2026-04-21, French authorities arrested 55 men in a 10-month CSAM-group investigation, and the Philippines placed Telegram under observation in Feb 2026. Utah alleges TikTok Live functions as a venue where adults give currency to young users for sexual solicitation; Minnesota sued on 2025-08-19 and Florida in June 2026 | Minors on smaller or less scrutinised surfaces | All alive | Input and market shape. Kik-scale platforms are the buyers, Telegram and Snapchat are destinations Guardian will only see as a migration ask, and TikTok Live suggests a distinct signal: platform-native currency flowing from an older band to a younger band during a live session |
| Jamie Borne, the "Roblox programmer" case | A 30-year-old arrested in New Orleans in February 2026 after probation officers found a child-sized sex doll; he admitted possessing CSAM. Initially reported widely as a "Roblox programmer." Roblox stated he never worked for the company, though he did make Roblox games. Rebooked 2026-03-31 with 197 additional felony counts | Attribution cautionary case | Alive; charges pending | Precedent on the wording constraint. The public record itself mislabeled him within days, which is why every string must say what the account did, in the operator's namespace, and nothing about who the person is or works for |

**Themes.**

Everybody now gates reach and nobody detects behavior. Roblox shipped six-band facial age estimation, Discord shipped teen-by-default, Meta hid follower lists from suspected sextortion accounts. All of it constrains who may contact whom. eSafety's August 2026 adversarial test proved the point: age estimation was live and adult strangers could still cold-request young children. Every criminal case here happened inside a permitted pair.

The one universal pattern is source-platform recruitment and destination-platform harm, and it is nobody's job. MDL 3166 describes the same arc across roughly 180 pleadings; Arkansas put it as Roblox leaving the front door unlocked and Discord handing over a private room. Each operator sees one segment and correctly concludes it is not much, which makes the migration ask the last moment the source platform can act.

Detection fires at the end of the chain, on a hash, years late. Billingsley was caught by a CSAM upload after months of grooming; Ryan Last's arrests came three years after the death; Castillo was named by the community for months and stayed active. The only case where software caught behavior before the artifact is Sentinel, at 35% proactive share in H1 2025.

Financial sextortion is a commodity supply chain, not a set of offenders. Meta's takedown removed 5,700 Groups selling scripts and photo sets, the Ogoshis bought hacked accounts rather than making new ones, and Kassi ran one script against thousands of victims. Script matching works because the scripts are literally sold; account-age heuristics fail because the accounts are stolen and aged.

The money is in liability, not in safety. Roughly $53M in disclosed Roblox settlements across five states in four months, plus MDL 3166 and an Ohio securities theory. None of it buys detection.

Nobody publishes precision at production base rates, and the platforms now have the number. Sentinel's post gives 6.1B messages a day and 35% proactive; Roblox's 65,381 CyberTipline reports in 2025 is an output count. Report volume is the industry's key metric and it is the wrong one; reviewer-minutes per 1,000 users and PPV per tier are the ones nobody discloses.

The third-party safety actor has been legally defined, narrowly. Roblox's cease-and-desist enumerated the prohibitions, Ruben Sim settled for $150,000 and a permanent ban, a Pennsylvania judge dismissed vigilante-sting cases, and prosecutors hold that only sworn officers may pose as minors. That is a complete compliance specification handed over for free.

Practitioners complain about three things consistently: reporters get no outcome, moderators review context-free snippets, and ban evasion is trivial. All three are product requirements, not sentiments.

---

An Oct 2025 breach exposed up to 70,000 ID photos held by a third-party vendor, and NCMEC says Discord reported 20%+ fewer CyberTipline reports in 2024 than 2023 | https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/discords-age-verification-sparks-intense-164516983.html |
| Antigone Davis | Meta | Global Head of Safety; largest CyberTipline reporter; introduced report bundling in March 2024 and default end-to-end encryption on Messenger, which NCMEC blames for ~6.9M fewer reports; anti-sextortion features including screenshot blocking and nudity protection | A litigation witness: a New Mexico jury found on 2026-03-24 that Meta concealed child exploitation risks and imposed $375M, with her safety statements scrutinized. Quote the verdict and NCMEC numbers, not Meta safety claims | https://www.cbsnews.com/news/meta-harmful-to-children-mental-health-new-mexico-jury-finds/ |
| Jacqueline Beauchere | Snap Inc. | Global Head of Platform Safety since Sept 2021; WeProtect policy board and INHOPE advisory board; founding Lantern member; The Keys safety curriculum | The most policy-connected platform safety lead. Snapchat is the default migration target in grooming scripts, and a New Mexico filing showed ~10,000 sextortion reports a month by late 2022 with an internal finding that 70% of victims never reported because they expected no action | https://saferinternet.org.uk/jacqueline-beauchere-global-head-of-platform-safety-snap-inc |
| FBI Violent Crimes Against Children program | Federal Bureau of Investigation | Child Exploitation and Human Trafficking Task Forces, Innocent Images, and joint FBI-HSI-NCMEC financial sextortion alerts. IC3 logged 75,000+ sextortion submissions in 2025 | Federal alert language on sextortion (mostly boys, minutes-to-hours tempo, a dozen-plus suicides) matches the threat model. Not a reporting target for providers, since 2258A routes through NCMEC | https://www.fbi.gov/investigate/violent-crime/vcac |
| HSI Cyber Crimes Center (C3) and Know2Protect | DHS Homeland Security Investigations | Fairfax VA center with the Child Exploitation Investigations Unit and Victim Identification Program (section chief Stephen Reuther). 2025: 6,509 new investigations, 4,937 arrests, 1,478 victims rescued. Runs Operation Renewed Hope and the Know2Protect campaign and tipline | The victim-identification bottleneck lives here: roughly 7 to 10 analysts against 89,000 unidentified series. Federal partner access requires an MOU and a named custodian | https://www.hstoday.us/subject-matter-areas/cybersecurity/dhs-cyber-crimes-center-launches-fourth-annual-operation-renewed-hope-to-identify-online-child-exploitation-victims/ |
| Sgt. Corwin Battle (Arkansas ICAC); Matthew Stegner (retired NYSP) | ICAC Task Force Program, OJJDP | Battle published year-over-year counts in June 2026: sextortion 153 to 150, CSAM complaints 1,596 to 2,068, pre-travel enticement 1,661 to 944. Stegner documented vigilante harms including a Maryland assault case, a Missouri Predator Poachers arrest and evidence destruction, with ICAC funded at $32.9M against $60M authorized | Commanders publish concrete counts and talk to press, making them the easiest LE partner tier for a demo, and they consistently reject vigilante decoy operations | https://www.police1.com/investigations/justice-denied-why-vigilante-groups-are-stepping-into-the-fight-against-child-exploitation |
| Charlotte Willner | Trust & Safety Professional Association | Founding Executive Director (ex-Facebook, Pinterest); publishes the T&S Curriculum and runs TrustCon. Senior Program Manager Kristy Kosak spent 10 years on child safety including Thorn youth programs | Where Guardian's buyers learn and hire. No policy positions; the curriculum is general | https://www.tspa.org/about-tspa/team/ |
| Stanford Internet Observatory 2024 report authors | Grossman, Pfefferkorn, Thiel, Shah, DiResta, Perrino, Cryst, Stamos, Hancock | Dozens of interviews showing reports are incomplete, duplicative and poorly prioritized, and that the highest-value reports are under-investigated. Pfefferkorn's follow-up credits the REPORT Act, one-year retention and Tech Coalition Pathways | The least contested source on this list. SIO cut staff in June 2024 after founding grants ran out, so the authors are dispersed | https://cyber.fsi.stanford.edu/publication/how-fix-online-child-exploitation-reporting-system |
| Creator predator-hunters (Schlep, Chris Hansen documentary) | Independent YouTubers | Schlep used decoy minor accounts around Roblox and published confrontations; banned 2025-08-09 with a cease-and-desist, triggering a 100,000-signature petition backed by Rep. Ro Khanna and a Chris Hansen documentary on 2026-02-27 | Precedent to avoid and the demand signal Guardian answers lawfully. Every platform bans the tactic, and ex-ICAC investigators document evidence destruction, misidentification, assaults and interference with active operations | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schlep_ban_controversy |

**Cite carefully.** Safe to cite: NCMEC data, IWF, C3P, WeProtect, Tech Coalition, the Stanford report, TSPA, 5Rights. Cite with caveats: Thorn (Chat Control lobbying and a vendor conflict finding), Child Rescue Coalition (undisclosed CPS methods), Polaris and ECPAT-USA (withdrawn or disputed prevalence figures), Heat Initiative (dark-money critique), Common Sense's AI institute (industry funders). Do not cite Our Rescue. Never reuse the 300,000 or 100,000 trafficked-children figures. Leadership churned across 2025-2026 (IWF, Tech Coalition, ICMEC, Polaris, Our Rescue, and SIO's dissolution), so re-verify names and titles before any outreach.

## 4. Verified claims

Twenty-four load-bearing claims, each checked against primary sources, re-checked for 2026 staleness, and attacked by a skeptic pass. Six confirmed, eighteen partially confirmed. The correction column is what the primary text actually says; those corrections are folded into sections 5 and 8.

| id | Claim | Status | Correction |
|---|---|---|---|
| thorn-grooming-label | Thorn's Safer Predict added a grooming label on 2025-11-12 with four sub-indicators, returning per-message and conversation-level confidence in English and Spanish, and does not do per-pair trajectory scoring | partially | Six sub-indicators, not four: soliciting production or exchange of CSAM; planning or suggesting offline meetings; encouraging self-harm or emotional dependency; developing romantic or sexual relationships with minors; soliciting personal information or images; moving conversations to less moderated platforms. It analyzes "line-by-line and at the conversation level," which in a 1:1 DM is pair-scoped, and supports label stacking to narrow down problem accounts. Hive resold it from 2026-04-07, so distribution is wider than Safer's direct customers |
| sentinel-actor-not-pair | Roblox Sentinel scores per-source skewness against contrastive exemplar indices, aggregates per actor and never per pair, and ships framework code only with no weights and no exemplars | partially | v2 release commit 2026-08-06, last push 2026-08-18. Skewness is the default of six aggregators (mean_of_positives, top_k_mean, percentile_score, softmax_weighted_mean, max_score) plus a simulation harness. Aggregation is per source, and v2 adds no pair or conversation layer, though the API is grouping-agnostic and does not forbid one. No trained weights (embeddings come from off-the-shelf sentence-transformers) and no grooming exemplar index; Roblox's production negative index is ~13,000 human-flagged exemplars you must build yourself. The repo does ship an unrelated SPLC extremist demo corpus of ~16,100 rows |
| no-shipped-pair-trajectory | Nobody ships an ordered per-pair stage-transition score; the nearest gestures are Thorn's label stacking, ToxMod's escalation patterns and Community Sift's unshipped 2024 experiment | partially | Nobody ships an ordered stage-transition score where the order of transitions carries signal; ordered stage modeling exists only in academic work and research code. But two adjacent primitives are already shipped. ToxMod has had GA Player Risk Categories since 2023-08-21, explicitly longitudinal ("repeated patterns of behavior over time"), emitting a per-player risk score and detecting grooming-stage markers including repeated one-on-ones with younger participants and persistent platform-switch requests, firing before anything overtly sexual; its unit is the actor, not the pair, with no stage ordering. And Thorn scores at the conversation level, which in 1:1 DMs is pair-scoped. Only the Community Sift item is accurate as written: it was described at GDC 2024 as experimental tech and never shipped. The defensible differentiator is ordering, not pair-scoping or over-time analysis |
| community-sift-sunset | Microsoft Community Sift, with purpose-built grooming models and 22+ languages, is being phased out in 2026, with a top-5 gaming publisher at 750M+ MAU already migrated to Stream | partially | Two competing vendors selling migration services (Stream and Lasso) say this, with Lasso specifically claiming Microsoft told customers it will honor contracts to expiration but not renew. No primary source supports it: Microsoft's product page still markets it as active with no retirement notice, and it appears on no Microsoft Lifecycle end-of-support list. The 22-language figure is confirmed verbatim from Microsoft's page. Grooming models are attested by Two Hat heritage and a Microsoft GDC 2024 article, but are not listed on the current page. The migrating customer is described by Stream as an unnamed top-5 mobile gaming publisher running a six-month migration completing by mid-2026, so it is a vendor-reported in-progress migration. "Per-message filters" fits Stream's advertised taxonomy and is unsupported for Lasso |
| roost-coop-free-ncmec | ROOST Coop is a free self-hosted review console with queues, SLA status, policy actions, wellness defaults and built-in NCMEC reporting; production users Kyodo, Notion and Musubi; one adopter reports a ~95% cost cut; ROOST launched Feb 2025 with $27-28.5M | partially | Confirmed on the product: Apache-2.0, V0 2026-02-10, 1.0 2026-06-09, queues with counts and priorities and SLA status, manual and automatic policy actions with full audit logging, wellness defaults, CyberTipline reporting alongside HMA and Google and OpenAI integrations, billed as the first free end-to-end CSAM detection, review and reporting workflow. Kyodo is the only named production user in the 1.0 announcement, and the ~95% figure is an unnamed adopter's estimate of annual safety tooling costs. Notion is described as an early adopter of Cove, the acquired codebase, not of Coop; Musubi Labs is a vendor selling a hosted Coop, not an adopter. Funding is $28.5M in cash and in-kind, widely reported as $27M cash over four years |
| ncmec-credentials-per-esp | NCMEC ESP API credentials are issued only on request with no self-registration, each ESP holds its own account, and vendors such as Cinder and Hive submit using the customer's credentials | partially | Confirmed: "A username and password must be requested from and supplied by NCMEC." HTTP basic over HTTPS, the flow /submit, /upload, /fileinfo, /finish, /retract plus GET /status and /xsd, test host exttest.cybertip.org/ispws, doc last updated 2026-08-26 16:54:47 -0400. ESP accounts are per-registered-company, though the doc never says so explicitly and says nothing about delegation. Only Hive documents the credential model ("your NCMEC credentials are required"); Cinder's public materials do not describe credential handling, so that half is an unverified inference. The design implication stands: apps/report is a credential store plus agent submitter |
| discord-intent-threshold-2026 | Since 2026-06-10 Discord's Message Content privileged intent review triggers at 10,000 unique users, replacing the 100-server rule, with a 90-day application window, annual reapplication, continued operation during review, and a required retention or in-memory statement; bot verification at 100 servers stays separate | confirmed | None. All elements verified verbatim against Discord's developer docs and support announcement. The app also keeps joining servers and reaching new users during review, which is stronger than the old rule |
| panc-two-party-only | PANC contains only chats between exactly two authors, Vogt et al. state systems trained on it are not applicable to group chats, and the eSPD repo carries a research-only banner | confirmed | None. Verbatim: "PANC only contains chats between exactly two authors, so our systems are not applicable in group chats. However, grooming is very rare in group chats as predators depend on their actions staying unnoticed." The rarity assertion carries no citation and no data. The repo (on GitLab, not GitHub) says models trained on this data will not detect real grooming attempts. Current threat reporting contradicts the rarity assumption for exactly Guardian's first surface |
| grooming-dataset-availability | The public grooming-data chain is largely closed: Perverted Justice ceased in 2019 and removed logs, ChatCoder2's page 404s, SCoRL is README-only, PJZ/PJZC has no license, PAN-12 is free but 2006-2012, and no open grooming or sextortion weights exist on Hugging Face | partially | All of the access findings hold. PJ ceased decoy operations at the start of 2019 and directs legal and university-research requests to two email addresses. ChatCoder2's data page returns 404 as of 2026-09-03. SCoRL is README-only, marked "[Not Finished Yet]," last pushed 2025-06-01. PJZ/PJZC ships with GitHub license null. PANC is distributed only as preprocessing scripts requiring PAN12 and ChatCoder2, so it cannot be rebuilt from public sources today. PAN-12 is a free ungated 91.2 MB Zenodo download with no stated license. But several open ungated grooming classifiers do exist on Hugging Face (podoya/KoBART_Online_Grooming_Detection, erikaecl/hansen-grooming-distilbert under Apache-2.0, ShellyMelly/grooming-detector, kavyaballari/childsafe-grooming-detector), all low-download and mostly uncarded. No sextortion weights exist |
| roblox-pii-v2-usable | Roblox PII Classifier v2 is XLM-RoBERTa-large, 0.6B params, 189 languages, F1 88.82% public and 90.52% internal, with three published thresholds and a multi-turn input contract, plus a 39,202-conversation evasion benchmark | confirmed | Confirmed with one clarification that matters for implementation: the input is a single flat string, the instruction prefix followed by turns joined by the literal "</s>" separator with the target speaker tagged t and others s1, s2 in order of appearance, tokenized at max_length 512 with truncation_side="left". It is not JSON-encoded, so services/ml must build the delimited string. The benchmark is 39,202 fully synthetic English conversations (24,518 safe, 14,684 unsafe), adversarial by construction, usable as a normalizer regression test but English-only |
| scorl-precision-ceiling | SCoRL reports latency-F1 0.365 and precision 0.475 against 0.062 and 0.047 for the prior best and identifies the risky utterance in 85.5% of cases; the federated work reports 82% F1 at 3% FPR; both are measured at inflated positive rates | partially | The SCoRL numbers are on the original PANC test split, which is not inflated: 69 grooming against 11,733 normal conversations, a 0.58% positive rate. On the separate downsampled 10:1 split (9.1% positive) SCoRL reaches latency-F1 0.509 and precision 0.819. The federated and DP work does report 82% F1 at 3% FPR on a PANC segment split with 9-11% positives, and recommends re-thresholding to 1% FPR. PAN-12 is not an inflated corpus (~0.12% of users, ~2% of training conversations). Because 0.475 precision is at 0.58%, this paper does not by itself show a per-message model cannot hit 40% PPV; the defensible argument is the ratio between a 0.58% research corpus and live traffic orders of magnitude sparser |
| sextortion-known-offline | Thorn's June 2025 survey (n=1,200, 13-20) found roughly 1 in 3 sextortion victims knew the perpetrator offline, over half of those current or former partners and nearly half friends or schoolmates; NCMEC logged 50,000+ financially motivated sextortion reports in 2025 (137 a day, up from 36,000), 94% reported by the child or a parent | confirmed | Precise figures: victim subsample n=285, 36% knew the perpetrator offline and 61% only online. Among that 36% (n=99, a base Thorn flags as under 100 and multiple-select), 52% were current or former romantic partners and 47% friends or schoolmates. The 94% applies to public-submitted reports, not to all 50,000+, most of which come from ESPs. The two datasets measure different populations: only about 1 in 5 of Thorn's victims reported money demands. The design implication holds: no decisive Guardian signal fires on known-contact coercion |
| two-velocity-windows | Webster's European Online Grooming Project (36 convicted groomers, four countries) splits intimacy-seeking from hyper-sexualised offenders, and Seto's motivation-facilitation work splits fantasy-driven from contact-driven offenders with different tempos | partially | The EOGP interviewed 33 men across three countries (26 UK, 5 Norway, 2 Belgium), with Italy contributing chat logs only. It classifies across eight behavioural dimensions including speed of contact into three types: intimacy-seeking (no indecent images, own identity, long contact before an offline meeting, all met the victim), hyper-sexualised (extensive image collections, contact highly sexualised and escalating very quickly, meetings least prevalent), and adaptable (tempo varies with the young person's response). It reports grooming taking "minutes, hours, days or months." The fantasy versus contact split is Briggs, Simon and Simonsen (2011, n=51), not Seto, whose motivation-facilitation model makes no tempo claims; a 2018 systematic review found that distinction empirically ambiguous and recommended the EOGP typology instead. The two-window conclusion is unchanged and arguably stronger, and the adaptable type needs both windows evaluated at once |
| ios-screentime-no-content | Apple's Screen Time APIs expose usage, not content, Communication Safety is on-device image-only and never notifies the parent, so every shipping iOS parent product uses a workaround | partially | Screen Time exposes only application, category and web-domain activity, with app selections returned as opaque tokens. Communication Safety runs entirely on-device on photos and videos (not text), now covering gore and violence as well as nudity across Messages, FaceTime, AirDrop, shared albums and the Photos picker; Apple "doesn't receive an indication that nudity was detected" and sends no parent alert, though under-13s need the Screen Time passcode to unblur. Every shipping product surfacing message text therefore relies on a workaround outside these APIs. DESIGN's "Screen Time API plus charger-sync fallback" should be rewritten: the Screen Time half contributes no text signal at all, so charger or backup scanning, or a launcher or hardware partnership, is the entire iOS text path |
| eu-derogation-lapse | The EU interim ePrivacy derogation lapsed on 2026-04-03 for about three months, was relaunched by the Council on 2026-07-02, survived Parliament on 2026-07-09, and runs to 2028; sources disagree on whether grooming text is still in scope | partially | Regulation (EU) 2021/1232, as extended to 2026-04-03 by 2024/1307, expired on 3/4 April 2026 after the co-legislators failed to agree on the Commission's 2025-12-19 clean extension. The gap was just under four months, not three. The Council adopted a first-reading position on 2026-07-02; Parliament amended it at second reading on 2026-07-09 without blocking it (an amendment forbidding grooming scanning fell 15 votes short); the Council approved on 2026-07-22. Regulation (EU) 2026/1881, signed 2026-07-24 and published 2026-07-28, entered into force 2026-07-31 and applies until 2028-04-03. Scope is not ambiguous: it expressly covers solicitation of children, but with stricter conditions, including no systematic text filtering except to detect patterns pointing to concrete suspicion, limitation to key indicators and risk factors such as age difference, GDPR Article 36(2) prior consultation, and mandatory human confirmation before any report. It excludes communications to which end-to-end encryption is, has been or will be applied. The permanent CSA Regulation is still in trilogue with the next session on 2026-09-29 |
| take-it-down-effective | TAKE IT DOWN criminal provisions took effect on signing 2025-05-19; Section 3 platform duties became enforceable 2026-05-19 requiring notice-and-removal and removal of a depiction and known identical copies within 48 hours; the FTC enforces at $53,088 per violation, sent 15+ warning letters, and runs TakeItDown.ftc.gov | confirmed | One nuance: identical-copy removal is a reasonable-efforts standard, not strict liability. Failure to reasonably comply is enforceable as a violation of a rule defining an unfair or deceptive act, which is what makes civil penalties available; the $53,088 maximum is re-indexed annually. Chairman Ferguson sent letters to 15 named companies on 2026-05-11, eight days before the deadline, and the FTC sent a further round to about a dozen websites on 2026-05-20 |
| coppa-april-2026 | The amended COPPA Rule's full compliance date was 2026-04-22, adding a public retention policy, a written security program with annual risk assessment and written vendor assurances, with biometric and government identifiers now personal information; NCMEC disclosure rides the 312.5(c) exception | partially | Effective 2025-06-23 with full compliance 2026-04-22 except 312.11(d)(1), (d)(4) and (g). As of 2026-09-03 the amended text is in force with no extension or stay; an FTC policy statement of 2026-02-25 announced a review limited to age-verification mechanisms. The new obligations are confirmed. But the Rule does not address NCMEC, the CyberTipline or CSAM reporting anywhere in its text or Statement of Basis and Purpose. The 312.5(c)(5) child-safety and (c)(6) security and law-enforcement provisions are exceptions to prior consent for collection and are textually limited to a child's and parent's name and online contact information, and NCMEC is a private nonprofit rather than a law enforcement agency. The sound basis is statutory: 18 USC 2258A mandates reporting and 2258B grants immunity. Treat "no separate parental consent for a CyberTipline report" as well-supported but counsel-dependent |
| rosenow-not-wilson | The correct authority for "a provider does not become a government agent by detecting and reporting" is Rosenow (9th Cir. 2022), not Wilson (9th Cir. 2021), which is about the scope of the private search; Maher and Lowers agree with Wilson, Reddick and Miller do not, and Ackerman treats NCMEC as governmental | confirmed | None. Rosenow verbatim: "Mandated reporting is different than mandated searching. Our caselaw is clear that a private actor does not become a government agent simply by complying with a mandatory reporting statute." DESIGN section 2 row 5 currently cites Wilson for the agency point and should be corrected. Two nuances for counsel: Rosenow's holding rests on a broader state-action analysis, not 2258A(f) alone, and Rosenow assumed without deciding that NCMEC is a governmental actor rather than adopting Ackerman |
| section-230-c2b | Section 230(c)(2)(B) immunizes providing others the technical means to restrict access to objectionable material, applied to filtering vendors in Zango and limited by Enigma; meanwhile (c)(1) is narrowing for platform design and safety statements | partially | Confirmed with three refinements. Enigma is a competitor-animus exception, not a general carve-out: the court adopted Judge Fisher's Zango concurrence and rejected a broader anticompetitive-effect test. Anderson expressly declined to decide the question for algorithms responding solely to user inputs. Commonwealth v. Meta is a motion-to-dismiss ruling holding that 230 does not bar claims that Meta was affirmatively misleading consumers about safety, not a finding of liability, and the California JCCP verdict is a negligent-design verdict on appeal. Most important and omitted from the original claim: Enigma II (9th Cir. 2023, 69 F.4th 665) held that a security vendor calling a competitor's software "malicious" is "more a statement of objective fact than a non-actionable opinion" and potentially actionable. Guardian's tier output sits closer to the assertion than to the blocking mechanism, and Zango and Enigma are Ninth Circuit law only |
| pricing-floor-gap | Child-safety text detection is uniformly enterprise contact-sales while commodity moderation is self-serve and cheap; schools buy at $2.75-$3.90 per student per year with human review at $8.20; nothing is priced for a 40-person Discord server | partially | Confirmed for Thorn, Hive's CSAM detection, Cinder and ActiveFence (now Alice, which publishes no prices). Commodity pricing is confirmed: Hive text $0.50 per 1,000 requests and visual $3.00 per 1,000, Sightengine $29/mo for 10,000 ops with a free 2,000-op tier and Pro at $99/mo, ToxMod $5,000/mo for 50,000 voice hours with a $0 Starter tier. But Lasso Moderation is self-serve, not enterprise-gated, at $99/mo for 10K items and $499/mo for 100K. Consumer parent apps cluster at $50-$140/yr (Bark $99/yr). The school figures come from district procurement records rather than vendor pages and should be cited as such or dropped. Cheap and free entry points exist at the low end; what is missing is any self-serve price for grooming, CSE or CSAM text detection at that scale |
| roblox-report-volume | Roblox filed 65,381 CyberTipline reports in 2025, more than double 2024, per a Senate subcommittee letter of 2026-08-12, and attributes ~1,200 NCMEC reports in H1 2025 to Sentinel (35% of detected cases), rising to nearly 70% in the 12 months to 2026-08-07, over 6B+ chat messages a day | partially | 65,381 in 2025 against 24,522 in 2024 (2.67x). The figure comes from NCMEC's published 2025 reports-by-ESP data, not from a Senate letter; the Senate source is a full-committee release citing "over 65,000 reports." Roblox states Sentinel helped submit approximately 1,200 NCMEC reports in H1 2025 and that 35% of the cases it detected in H1 2025 came from that proactive approach (alongside 111M daily users and an average 6.1 billion chat messages a day), later stating nearly 70% for the 12 months ended 2026-08-07. The 35% and 70% are shares of Roblox-detected child-endangerment cases, not of total CyberTipline volume: 1,200 reports is roughly 2% of 65,381 |
| ncmec-2025-baseline | NCMEC took 21.3M reports in 2025 from about 300 companies with five ESPs filing over 75%; 18.8M referred and 4.5M informational; more than 10% lacked jurisdiction data (unchanged from 2024); 53,000+ escalated as urgent; online enticement ~1.4M up 156-158%; child sex trafficking 105,877, more than 1,100% above 2023, largely a REPORT Act artifact | partially | 21,181,300 from ESPs plus 170,193 from the public, just over 300 companies, five ESPs over 75%, more than 18.8M referred and more than 4.5M informational, more than 10% of industry reports with inadequate information to determine where the offense occurred, more than 53,000 escalated as urgent or imminent. Online enticement is 1,413,347, a 158% increase over 2024's 546,000. Child sex trafficking is 113,530, not 105,877, a 323% increase over 2024's 26,823 and roughly 556% above 2023's ~17,300, not 1,100%. The unroutable share is not unchanged: the 2024 report says more than 8%, so it worsened to more than 10%. NCMEC now names companies with reporting-quality gaps |
| age-band-provenance | Age bands are priors, not facts: Roblox's facial age estimation has a reported MAE of 1.4 years for under-18s, Wired called the rollout a complete mess, eSafety found adults could still request connections to child accounts, Discord's UK Persona test was terminated and its Oct 2025 breach exposed up to 70,000 ID photos, and statutory sources return four brackets | partially | Roblox's own August 2026 age-assurance documentation supports the premise: band assignment is a continuously updated estimate rather than a point-in-time verified check (338 million facial age-check records; a fused behavioral model at mean average precision above 0.9; more than 99% of users remaining in the same broad band on consecutive days). Roblox publishes no MAE, so the 1.4-year figure should not be cited as a Roblox or Persona number. The Discord breach was a September 2025 compromise of a third-party customer-service vendor disclosed in October, and 70,000 is Discord's estimate against an attacker claim of ~2.1 million. What is verifiable word for word is the statutory direction: Texas SB 2420 and California AB 1043 both define exactly four brackets, as does Apple's Declared Age Range. Roblox itself now emits both a six-band chat grouping and a three-tier account grouping (Roblox Kids under 9, Roblox Select 9-15, Standard 16+, June 2026), so "six bands matching Roblox" is already a moving target |
| liability-and-audit-demand | Roblox disclosed roughly $53M in state settlements in four months of 2026, faces MDL No. 3166 with ~170 consolidated cases in N.D. Cal., and signed an Australian eSafety undertaking on 2026-08-17 requiring an independent third-party auditor, which eSafety says is a first | partially | Settlements verified individually: Nevada 2026-04-15 (a $12.5M package: $10M payment plus $1M awareness and $1.5M law-enforcement liaison), Alabama $12.2M 2026-04-21, Mississippi announced 2026-06-22 with no amount in the release, South Dakota $15M on 2026-07-13 per the AG's own release (not $8.6M). A West Virginia settlement of ~$11.08M could not be verified from a primary source. The confirmed subtotal across three states is about $39.7M and the full figure is likely higher, so the "$53M in four months" total should be restated. MDL 3166 is before Chief Judge Richard Seeborg in N.D. Cal. with 182 actions pending and 187 total as of 2026-09-01, not ~170. The eSafety undertaking was announced 2026-08-19/20 (the 2026-08-17 signing date is unverified) and does require an independent third-party auditor covering the age-estimation technology within three months, with Commissioner Inman Grant saying it is the first time such auditing has been required under the Online Safety Act. The strategic point stands |

---

## 5. Gap analysis

Against the September 2026 landscape. Phase 1 (kernel v0 plus Discord bot) is the frame; anything scored high cost is a later-phase note.

Ranking key. **Help** is how much it moves a child's outcome or a reviewer's decision. **Legal** is fit with the DESIGN section 2 constraints (clean, conditional, blocked). **Cost** is the build on the existing phase-1 kernel (L is days, M is weeks, H is a phase).

Six corrections to DESIGN.md are folded in below and marked with a caret.

### 5.1 Being done by others that we are not

| # | Gap | Who does it | Surface | Help | Legal | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | Feed the Roblox PII v2 model its exact input contract: instruction prefix, speaker tags t / s1 / s2, turns joined by the literal `</s>`, 512 tokens, truncation_side left, thresholds 0.60 / 0.55 / 0.10 | Roblox model card, 2026-08-19, Apache-2.0 | Discord bot, services/ml | High | Clean | L |
| A2 | Adopt Roblox's 39,202-conversation synthetic evasion benchmark as the normalizer's regression test (homoglyphs, letter spacing, PII split across turns, reversed text) | Roblox/roblox-pii-classifier-benchmark, Apache-2.0, no user data | packages/schema/lexicon, scripts/eval | High | Clean | L |
| A3 | Carry age-band confidence and provenance, not a bare enum. Roblox now emits a calibrated probability distribution over bands, re-checked continuously: 338M age-check records, more than 99% band-stable day to day, mAP above 0.9 | Roblox age-assurance post, Aug 2026 | All | High | Clean | L |
| A4 | Emit into a free review console instead of building a generic one. ROOST Coop 1.0 (2026-06-09, Apache-2.0) ships queues with SLA status, policy-bound actions, an audit log, appeals and NCMEC submission | ROOST Coop 1.0; Osprey | apps/review | High | Clean | M |
| A5 | Alert-card grammar and volume controls. Beacon separates model output (a Predicted Phase badge) from human judgment (a Risk Level that defaults unset), shows a per-actor frequency indicator, groups alerts in a 15-minute window, and ships snooze plus after-hours escalation lists. Bark keeps a severity floor under every mute | GoGuardian Beacon; Bark; Discord AutoMod SEND_ALERT_MESSAGE | Discord bot, review | High | Clean | M |
| A6 | Shape the stored evidence record as a superset of CyberTipline API fields from day one, so export is a projection rather than a rewrite | Thorn Review Tool 2.9.0; Cinder pre-filled report metadata; Hive enrichment callback | cases, report | High | Clean | M |
| A7 | Report status and outcome trail back to the reporter. Australia's eSafety undertaking now makes outcome notification a binding obligation for Roblox, and the core Schlep grievance was tickets vanishing into a black box | eSafety undertaking; Tubefilter and Rolling Stone on Schlep | Discord bot, platform SDK | High | Clean | M |
| A8 | Text-native reviewer wellness. Everyone ships blur, grayscale and mute, which does nothing for a transcript queue. The 2025 UW clinical study found probable PTSD at 26% and recommends capping exposure at 2 to 4 hours a day, after which organization and workflow dominate | Coop, Cinder, Meta content-review-filters; Gauthier et al. 2025; AURA | apps/review | High | Clean | M |
| A9 | Voice and video are a blind spot. ToxMod has shipped a GA child-grooming risk category since Aug 2023 and Roblox Voice v3 publishes 61% recall at 1% FPR across 30 languages. Greggy's Cult recruited on Roblox and CS:GO and escalated in Discord video calls, entirely outside a text kernel | Modulate; Roblox Voice v3; DOJ indictment | Discord bot, later | High | Clean | H |

^ **Positioning correction.** DESIGN section 12 implies trajectory scoring is unoccupied. It is not: ToxMod ships longitudinal actor-level grooming risk, Sentinel ships per-actor skew, and Thorn scores line by line and at the conversation level, which in a 1:1 DM is pair-scoped and has been resold through Hive since 2026-04-07. Do not claim that nobody analyzes behavior over time or that nobody scores the pair.

### 5.2 Not being done that we can do

| # | Open lane | Why it is open | Surface | Help | Legal | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Ordered stage-transition scoring, where the order of stages carries signal. Sentinel v2 aggregates per source with six aggregators and adds no pair layer; Thorn's six grooming sub-indicators are unordered labels; ordered stage modeling exists only in academic work | The real wedge, narrower than DESIGN section 6.2 claims | apps/scorer | High | Clean | M |
| B2 | Two velocity windows, not one. DESIGN section 6.2 uses a single 24h window. The EOGP (n=33 offenders, UK, Norway, Belgium) splits hyper-sexualised offenders who escalate in under 4h from intimacy-seeking offenders who take weeks. Thorn 2025: 30% of sextortion demands land within 24h of contact, so 70% do not | A single window misses one population by construction | apps/scorer | High | Clean | L |
| B3 | A non-financial coercion class. 764, CVLT, Court, Kaskar and Greggy's Cult coerce self-harm, cutsigns and fansigns rather than money. The FBI names it a Category 1 threat (191 arrests across 28 countries, 2020-2025) and Discord is the venue. No vendor models it, and every sextortion heuristic keys on payment | The signal catalog and critical-override list have no path for it | Discord bot | High | Clean | M |
| B4 | Fan-IN inversion: many distinct actors converging on one under-16 target in a short window. Guardian already computes fan-out, and inverting the same graph catches the group-coordinated pattern that pair scoring understates | Greggy's Cult, five defendants, one victim set, ran a year undetected | apps/scorer | High | Clean | L |
| B5 | A known-contact coercion path. Thorn's June 2025 survey found 36% of victims knew the perpetrator offline; of those, 52% were current or former romantic partners, 47% friends or schoolmates, 54% were themselves minors, and threats were carried out 38% of the time versus 20% online-only. None of Guardian's decisive signals fire on that population | Either scope Guardian explicitly to the stranger and financial patterns, or build the path | Discord bot, review | High | Conditional | M |
| B6 | Report quality as the product. NCMEC 2025: 21.3M reports, five ESPs filing over 75%, more than 10% of industry reports lacking jurisdiction data (worse than more than 8% in 2024), with NCMEC now naming offending companies. Stanford found near-identical reports producing opposite outcomes | A complete, deduped bundle with human-viewed provenance is a defensible claim nobody else makes | report, investigator | High | Clean | M |
| B7 | Publish a model card with recall at fixed FPR and the base-rate simulation. ROOST's own leadership names PhotoDNA, Thorn Safer and Google CSAPI as undocumented, and only Roblox Voice v3 publishes an operating point | A free differentiator costing one document plus honest numbers | scripts/eval | Med | Clean | L |
| B8 | A victim-side branch in the T2 action set, plus a StopNCII or NCMEC Take It Down referral for sextortion targets. Patchin and Hinduja (n=5,568) found ~5% victimization and ~3% perpetration among US students, with perpetrators disproportionately former victims | Fan-out and threat-template detectors will tier minors, and timing one out is the wrong action | Discord bot | High | Clean | L |
| B9 | A self-serve price for child-safety text detection. Commodity moderation is cheap and self-serve (Hive text $0.50 per 1k, Sightengine $29/mo, Lasso $99/mo) while every CSE, grooming and CSAM text product is contact-sales | ^ The gap is a capability cliff at a price point, not an absence of low price points. DESIGN overstates this | Platform SDK, Discord bot | Med | Clean | M |

### 5.3 What we are doing that nobody else is

| # | Differentiator | Standing against the field | Surface |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Ordered stage progression on the pair, with 3 to 4 and 5 to 6 weighted | The unoccupied primitive once Sentinel's actor skew and Thorn's conversation labels are accounted for | apps/scorer |
| C2 | Human-only T3 on a hash-chained audit with model_version, lexicon_version and fusion_version on every score row, and reviewer-minutes per 1,000 users as a marketed metric | eSafety's August 2026 undertaking created a procurement category that did not exist before: an independent third-party audit of safety-measure effectiveness. The audit chain is the artifact that answers it. Child Rescue Coalition's CPS is the cautionary case, where prosecutors dropped cases rather than disclose the method | packages/audit, review |
| C3 | Hash-only by construction, not by policy. apps/ingest drops media bytes at the edge | Every peer either holds bytes (HMA, Griffeye, Safer) or carries a takedown obligation. This also keeps Guardian out of the private-search chain entirely | ingest, all |
| C4 | An evidence bundle designed backward from what the consumer needs: CyberTipline fields, jurisdiction, timezone-explicit timestamps, a per-excerpt human-viewed flag, a reviewer context field, and a warrant-ready discovery path | Skull Games ships the discovery-path template; nobody ships it joined to a detector | report, investigator |
| C5 | Built for the segment nobody serves. Roblox filed 65,381 CyberTipline reports in 2025, up from 24,522, against 6.1B chat messages a day. Everything below Roblox has the free floor (Coop, the Sentinel framework, OpenAI moderation) and no grooming model, because Sentinel ships no exemplars and Roblox's own negative index is ~13,000 hand-built examples | Discord bot, platform SDK |

### 5.4 What we must not do even though competitors do

| # | Prohibition | Who does it, and what happened | Cost of breaking it |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | No decoys or personas | Roblox's cease-and-desist to Schlep (2025-08-09) enumerates the exact prohibitions: simulated child-endangerment conversations, soliciting or sharing PII, moving conversations off-platform, delaying reports to produce content. Roblox settled with Ruben Sim for $150K plus a permanent ban. Our Rescue funded Washington Net Nanny stings that drew entrapment critiques. Even an officer-run sting was reversed on Fourth Amendment grounds in Ohio v. Deuble | Bans from the platforms Guardian needs to serve, plus suppressed evidence |
| D2 | No media bytes, and no takedown feature | TAKE IT DOWN Section 3 (enforceable 2026-05-19, $53,088 per violation) requires removal plus reasonable efforts on known identical copies within 48h. That duty is the customer's, and executing it means holding bytes and running copy matching. 18 USC 2252/2252A has no vendor exception, and Farid's 2025 NudeNet finding plus SIO's LAION-5B work show scraped corpora contain CSAM | Criminal liability with no safe harbor |
| D3 | Never label a person | Bark, MMGuardian, Troomi, Kidas and Aura all ship the word predator in consumer alert taxonomy. ^ Section 230(c)(2)(B) plus Zango protects the blocking function, but Enigma II (9th Cir. 2023, 69 F.4th 665) held that a security vendor calling a competitor malicious is "more a statement of objective fact than a non-actionable opinion," and actionable. Guardian's tier output sits closer to the assertion than to the mechanism. Commonwealth v. Meta makes affirmatively misleading consumers about safety independently actionable | Defamation and false-advertising exposure that 230 does not touch |
| D4 | No exposure feature, no catches feed | PredCord (~1,569 members) and Predator Catching (3,334 members, created April 2025) run predator catches, predator fights and amateur catches channels. Guardian's bot will be installed on servers whose members already do this, so alert copy and bundles must be unusable as raw material for a callout | Turns Guardian into the evidence supply chain for the posture every platform bans |
| D5 | One reporting path | Safe House Project's Simply Report routes anonymous tips straight to law enforcement. Guardian drafts; the operator or parent files at NCMEC. FBI staff embedded at NCMEC triage CyberTipline reports and route to field offices, so the federal path is already covered | Bypassing the statutory channel loses 2258B cover and the routing |
| D6 | Overt only, never sideloaded or covert | PETS 2025 compared 40 parental-control apps and found 8 of 20 sideloaded ones matched stalkerware indicators. Qustodio's deepest Android features need an APK sideload and BrightCanary captures keystrokes through a custom keyboard. ^ The vendor exposure is 18 USC 2512 and FTC stalkerware precedent, not vicarious consent, which is a parent's doctrine (Pollock: no magic words, own child only) | The parent app must be architecturally incapable of covert use, not merely configured against it |
| D7 | No law-enforcement tasking into the kernel | ^ DESIGN section 2 row 5 cites the wrong case. US v. Wilson (9th Cir. 2021) is about private-search scope: where a hash match is the only search and no employee viewed the file, police need a warrant. The government-agent authority is US v. Rosenow (9th Cir. 2022, 50 F.4th 715): 2258A(f) disclaims any search duty, so mandated reporting is different from mandated searching, and a provider searching of its own volition is not a state actor. Wilson, Maher and Lowers hold one way; Reddick and Miller the other; Ackerman treats NCMEC itself as governmental | Fix the citation, and record in the audit chain that every threshold and lexicon change originated with Guardian or the operator, never with a police request |

---

## 6. UX brief

Scope: `apps/review` (Next.js) and the alert and setup surface of `apps/discord-bot`. Phase 1 ships the Discord alert and the guild setup flow; phase 2 ships the queue and case detail. Both are specified here because the alert card and the case card must share one grammar, and because the wording rules are cheaper to enforce before strings exist.

### 6.0 The five rules that generate every decision below

These come from CLAUDE.md and DESIGN section 2, and they are why this product looks different from everything in the audit.

1. **The UI never asserts anything about a person.** It describes traffic and offers a tier. Every string is checked against 6.7.
2. **The model tops out at T2.** The boundary between model output and human judgment must be visually obvious, not merely true in the backend. Beacon's split, a model-assigned Predicted Phase badge next to a human Risk Level dropdown that defaults to unset, is the pattern to copy.
3. **No imagery, ever.** Not blurred, not grayscale, not behind a click. Guardian holds a hash and an operator-supplied verdict. Every wellness control in the audit is an image control, so Guardian needs text-native equivalents and has to invent most of them.
4. **The 4-hour SLA is a queue latency target, never a per-case stopwatch.** Sama's 50-second average-handling-time target is the documented cause of the Kenya litigation. Guardian shows time remaining on the case and breach forecast on the queue, and shows no per-reviewer handling-time leaderboard anywhere.
5. **The evidence bundle is a handoff, not a verdict.** Stanford's finding is that reports fail on completeness, not detection, so design the case detail backwards from the CyberTipline field set and make export a projection rather than a re-entry.

### 6.1 Personas, and the one job each does in under a minute

| Persona | Context | The one-minute job | What they must never be asked to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| Server owner or moderator (Discord, untrained, volunteer, on a phone) | Reads a mod channel between other tasks. Has never heard of stage models. May be 19 years old | Read one alert card and pick one of three responses: acknowledge, apply the friction the server already configured, or send to review | Judge whether someone is a predator. Read a transcript. Decide a tier |
| Trained reviewer (4h SLA, never sees imagery, may be the only reviewer at a 40-person customer) | Works a partitioned queue, two hours a day maximum on T2 work | Open the top case, read a stage-annotated timeline of at most 20 messages, and record a decision plus a reason | Hunt for context in other tabs. Absorb explicit text they did not choose to reveal. Produce T3 alone |
| Platform trust and safety lead (dashboard reader, weekly not hourly) | Answers to a regulator, an auditor or a board. Post-eSafety, may owe an external audit | Answer one of: are we inside SLA, what is this costing in reviewer minutes per 1,000 users, and is the audit chain intact | Read individual cases to understand system health |
| Investigator (later phase, partner-gated, ICAC or C3 analyst) | 75 to 100 files a year, single files consuming thousands of hours | Rank a batch of tips by likelihood of identifying a victim, and see what is already a duplicate of an open case | Re-review material already categorized elsewhere. Receive a lead with no discovery path |

Two constraints fall out. The Discord moderator and the trained reviewer are different people with different vocabularies, so the alert card and the case card share structure but not wording depth. And the platform lead never enters the queue, so queue health must be legible from aggregate metrics alone.

### 6.2 Discord mod-channel alert

The first thing Guardian ships and the only Guardian surface most people will ever see.

**Form factor.** One Discord embed per grouped incident, posted to the owner-selected channel, legible as a sibling of Discord's own AutoMod alert card because that is the format server mods already read. Deliberately un-styled: no color bar screaming red, no siren emoji. A left border in the tier color and nothing else.

**Anatomy.**

```
Guardian · Pair 4f2a · #general
Review suggested

Two signals in one thread, 19 hours apart.

  Older-band account asked about supervision at home,
  then asked to continue on another app.

Stage 3 to Stage 4       Age bands  16-17 to 9-12 (role-derived)
First seen 04:12 UTC     Messages in window  14
Prior alerts, this pair  none        This actor, 7d  2 other pairs

[ Open evidence ]  [ Apply configured action ]  [ Not an issue ]
```

The header names the pair, not the people; pair IDs are stable, short and pronounceable in a mod voice channel. The tier line is a verb phrase describing what the queue should do, never an adjective about a person. The two-sentence summary is the whole product for this persona, written as behavior rather than inference: what was asked, in what order, how far apart.

**No message excerpt in the channel.** This is the sharpest departure from Bark and from AutoMod. A mod channel is a semi-public space in a server that may contain the flagged account's friends and, in the vigilante-adjacent communities documented in the audit, people who will screenshot it. Excerpts live behind Open evidence, which requires a role.

The frequency indicator (this actor, 7d: 2 other pairs) is stolen from Beacon's per-student alert count and is the cheapest way to let an untrained mod distinguish a one-off from a pattern. Age-band provenance is printed (role-derived, Discord teen status, unknown) because bands are priors and the mod needs to know when the gap driving the alert rests on a guess.

Three buttons, three verbs, matching AutoMod's mental model without duplicating its keyword filtering. Apply configured action executes only what the owner set up in 6.10, and its confirmation dialog restates it: "This applies a 10-minute timeout. It is a service action, not a finding about anyone." Not an issue is a first-class button rather than a dismissal into nothing: it writes a labeled row that feeds the lexicon-mining loop in DESIGN section 6.5. Bark's failure is that a parent's only decision is read, saved or ignored, and nothing learns.

**Volume control, with a floor.** Alert fatigue is the universal complaint and the fixes are all throttles with a safety floor. Guardian's: one embed per (actor, channel, 15-minute window), since AutoMod's ungrouped per-message embeds are what not to do; cadence per tier, where T1 never posts, T2 posts once and reminds once at 2h if unacknowledged, and critical-signal T2 posts immediately and pings the configured role; quiet hours and an after-hours escalation role; and a floor where any critical signal (threat template, payment demand after a media event, meetup logistics with an age gap) bypasses every mute, quiet hour and severity filter. Bark's "suicide and predator alerts cannot be turned off" is the right shape, and Guardian's version is stated in the settings UI in those words.

**What the alert must never do.** No catch, no caught, no leaderboard, no running total of flagged users, no public channel posting option, no mention of the flagged account, no DM to the flagged account, no DM to the younger-band account. The audit found 1,500-member and 3,300-member Discord servers organized around channels named Predator catches, Predator fights and Amateur catches, so Guardian's alert must be structurally unusable as raw material for that.

### 6.3 Reviewer queue: list view

**Ordering.** Not oldest-first (Coop's default) and not arrival order (the documented ICAC practice, described by an investigator as holding a finger up to feel the wind). Rank by severity (tier and critical-signal presence) times an identifiable-victim signal (age band known, younger band confirmed) times fan-out (one actor across many younger-band targets), divided by SLA time remaining. Print the ordering rule at the top of the queue: Meta publishes its severity times virality times likelihood ordering, and a reviewer who cannot see why case A is above case B loses trust in the queue.

**Row.**

```
◆  T2   Pair 4f2a    Stage 3 to 4, 19h     2h 41m left    Actor: 3 pairs 7d    unclaimed
```

Unread state is a light fill plus a dark outline, not color; color is reserved for the tier scale. Claim state is visible and enforced, because 74.5% of Reddit moderators report collisions and Coop's answer (never assign one job to two moderators) is silent. Guardian shows "claimed by A. Rivera, 4m ago" on the row, and a second reviewer opening it gets a read-only banner with a Request handoff button. SLA is a countdown per row plus a queue-level breach forecast in the header, and never becomes a per-case timer displayed to the reviewer.

**Header.** `14 in queue · live · 3 breach risk · you have 47 minutes of session budget left`. The session budget belongs in the header rather than buried in settings, because the finding that matters most (UW 2025) is that once exposure is held to roughly 2 to 4 hours a day, outcomes are driven by workflow and organization rather than content volume. Making the budget ambient is the cheapest way to enforce it.

**Filters.** By tier, customer partition, critical-signal presence, age-band confidence, claimed or unclaimed, and breach risk, plus a saved "Needs second reviewer" view for the T3 two-person rule.

### 6.4 Case detail: information hierarchy

The reviewer's decision is whether a pattern is present, so the pattern goes above the fold and the raw text goes below it. Reddit's study found 84% of moderators leave the queue to gather context, and every one of those trips is a design failure.

**Above the fold, in order.**

1. **Severity preview strip**, rendered before the timeline loads. Tier, which critical signals fired, both age bands with provenance and confidence, message count, time span, count of media events with operator verdicts. The reviewer decides here whether to open the timeline at all, and can hit "Defer, need a buffer" without reading anything.
2. **Why this is here.** One sentence generated from the fusion output in the same behavior-not-person voice as the Discord card, plus the three highest-contributing features with their weights. Sentinel's explainability output belongs here for the skew component: show the two or three exemplar snippets the actor's messages resembled, so "skew is rising" is inspectable rather than oracular.
3. **Pair and actor context, side by side.** Pair: the stage vector as a six-cell strip with hit stages filled and transition arrows annotated with elapsed time. Actor: other pairs in the window, account age, fan-out, alt-cluster flag, prior Guardian alerts and their outcomes, elevated role if any. Elevated role (developer, moderator, staff) is displayed as a risk annotation, not a mitigation, with a one-line explanation. The Castillo case is a developer with 24 child employees and 18 months of public community reports; reputation is an anti-signal in most trust and safety systems and it was wrong there.
4. **Model and version provenance.** model_version, lexicon_version, fusion_version, score timestamp. This is what a customer's deceptive-trade-practices or eSafety auditor will ask for, and what a defense motion will attack.

**Below the fold.** The evidence timeline (6.5); related cases, appeals and prior decisions on this actor with the earlier decision shown inline; internal comments; and a pinned policy reference panel showing the operator's configured criteria for this tier.

**Anti-patterns explicitly excluded.** No screenshots of anyone's screen (Beacon's evidence unit, OurPact's gallery, Mobicip's periodic captures). No "risk score: 92" as a headline. No composite wellness score with no visible components. No photo, avatar or display name of either participant rendered large; identifiers are hashed handles at body-text weight.

### 6.5 Evidence timeline

The single most Guardian-specific screen. Nothing in the audit is a good model, because every reviewed product's timeline is a media grid.

**Structure.** A two-party vertical thread ordered by time, with elapsed-time gaps collapsed into labeled spacers ("4 days, no messages"). Each message row:

```
 t   14:02   older band   [stage 3 · 0.81]
     "does anyone check your phone at night"
                                   ^ supervision probe

 s1  14:04   younger band
     "no my mum works nights"

 t   14:06   older band   [stage 4 · 0.77]
     "add me on [ghost emoji] [snapchat]"     normalized from ghost emoji
```

Speaker tags follow the Roblox PII classifier v2 input contract: target speaker t, others s1 and s2 in order of appearance. Use the same convention in the UI that the ML service uses on the wire, so a reviewer reading the timeline is reading what the model read.

Stage annotations sit in the margin with the label and the confidence, not the raw logit. A stage label below the operator's precision threshold renders greyed with a low-confidence tag rather than being hidden, so the reviewer can see the model's near-misses.

**Normalization is shown, not hidden.** The normalized token appears inline and the original appears on hover and on focus. Roblox's own moderators and the Bloomberg reporting both describe filter evasion as the core failure, and a reviewer who cannot see that leVe fired the migration signal cannot tell a true hit from a lexicon bug. Meta's slur-highlighting-plus-tooltip pattern is the direct precedent. Lexicon hits are underlined, with the matching entry and version on hover, and a "report this token as a false positive" affordance that writes straight into the mining loop.

**Media events.** A media event is a row with no bytes and no placeholder image:

```
 t   14:31   media event   direction: older to younger
     hash sha256:9f3c...  ·  operator verdict: no match
     human viewed by operator: no
```

Three things matter. The direction field is what makes the payment-after-media temporal join legible. The operator verdict is supplied by PhotoDNA, Safer, Arachnid Shield or HMA on the customer's side; Guardian stores it and never computes it. The human-viewed flag is load-bearing legal metadata: in the Second, Fourth and Ninth Circuits a hash match with no human viewing does not extend the private search, so law enforcement needs a warrant. That flag has to be captured at ingest, displayed here and carried into the report bundle, and it cannot be retrofitted.

**Redaction and reveal, the text-native wellness layer.** The image-wellness kit does not apply, and the evidence for text is thin and honest: HateBuffer (CSCW 2025, n=80) found that paraphrasing and anonymizing hate speech preserved accuracy but produced no measurable improvement in reviewer affect, while interviewees reported it buffered emotional contagion. So Guardian ships reveal-on-demand and measures it rather than claiming it works.

Spans classified as explicit sexual content, threat-template matches or payment coercion render collapsed as `[sexual content, 14 words · reveal]`, revealed by hover, click-to-persist and keyboard; the slider, hover and click triad from the interactive-blur studies transfers directly. The collapse default is set at the queue level by the org and overridable per reviewer, not per case, so it cannot become a productivity choice the reviewer feels pressure to disable: the AURA finding is that 35.8% of workers who could turn off video never did, and opt-in protections go unused. Nothing softened is ever persisted; the bundle, the audit chain and the CyberTipline export carry verbatim text, and paraphrase is a display layer with no write path. Third-party minimization collapses message rows from participants outside the pair by default with an author-count summary, expandable. An exposure counter runs per session, weighted by severity rather than item count; at 60% of budget the header changes tone, and at 100% the queue stops serving new cases and offers lower-intensity work rather than logging the reviewer out mid-case. A 90-second skippable buffer pause follows any critical-signal case, with the next case not pre-loaded.

### 6.6 Decision panel

Visually separated from the timeline by a rule and a background shift, and fixed to the viewport so the reviewer never scrolls to find it.

**The four decisions plus two escapes.**

```
Cmd-1  Dismiss    no further action, retention drops to T0 rules
Cmd-2  Watch      hold at T1, retain 30d, raise pair priority
Cmd-3  Confirm    reviewer-confirmed T2, operator friction available
Cmd-4  Report     propose T3, requires second reviewer

Cmd-E  Escalate to second reviewer
Cmd-R  Request context from operator      Enter to apply · Cmd-Z to undo
```

Keyboard hints are printed on the buttons. Consequence microcopy sits under each: "Dismiss: the pair returns to normal scoring. It does not clear anyone of anything." The words clear and exonerate appear nowhere.

**Undo exists.** Coop has no native undo and requires a custom action to reverse, which is a defect. Guardian's undo emits a compensating audit entry rather than mutating the original.

**Report does not produce T3 by itself.** It moves the case into a Needs confirmation queue served to a different reviewer, who sees the first reviewer's reason and notes and can Uphold or Overturn. Only concurrence writes T3; disagreement writes a QA event. This is Cinder's multi-review setting applied to the one decision that carries a statutory consequence.

**Reason taxonomy, required on every decision.** Coop's Require Decision Reason toggle should not be a toggle. Reasons are always required, and the confirm and report sets map one to one onto CyberTipline incident types so the export is a projection.

*Dismiss:* same-band interaction with no age gap; teen romance, lawful, both bands 13+; trading, giveaway or in-game economy transaction; role-play, fiction or in-character content; both participants adult band; catcher or vigilante role-play (a real false-positive source, since the audit documents 3,334-member servers whose members role-play as decoys); trusted connection (parent, guardian, sibling, verified relationship); staff or moderator acting in role; lexicon false positive with a free-text token field; duplicate of case #____.

*Watch:* one critical signal with no progression; progression present with age gap unconfirmed; insufficient context window; awaiting operator age-band verification.

*Confirm (T2):* progression pattern, stages ___ to ___; migration ask with age gap; economic bait, adult to minor; coercion language, non-financial; actor-level pattern across multiple pairs.
