*Report (proposed T3), mapped to NCMEC incident type:* online enticement of a child for sexual acts; child sex trafficking; unsolicited obscene material sent to a child; child pornography (operator hash verdict, operator is the viewer); child sexual molestation. Plus a sextortion annotation flag and an imminent-danger escalation flag, the latter requiring a reason string.

**Notes.** Three prompts, not a blank box: what in the timeline supports this decision, what context you gathered outside the timeline, and what you are recommending the operator do. The third survives into the report as the reviewer's contextual note. Stanford's finding and Cinder's essay converge here: the field investigators most want is the reviewer's judgment about why this case is not like the other thousand, and no schema field captures it.

**Minutes spent.** Auto-timed from case open to decision, pausable, correctable before submit, with an explicit "this was interrupted" checkbox. It feeds reviewer minutes per 1,000 users per day, the DESIGN section 10 metric and a number worth marketing. It never feeds a per-reviewer productivity display, rating, pay input or comparative view; the Sama handling-time target and the Ghana pay-deduction allegations are why. The operator dashboard shows aggregate minutes and per-reviewer load so a lead can rebalance, never per-reviewer speed.

### 6.7 Wording rules

**The rule.** A tier describes traffic. A tier never describes a person. Every string in the product, every log line, every webhook payload key, every email subject and every button label is written about a pair, a conversation, an account's messages or a signal, never about who someone is or what they intend. Grammatically: prefer a noun phrase naming the pattern (migration ask after a supervision probe) or a verb phrase naming the action (review suggested) over any predicate adjective attached to a human.

**A bad alert sentence.** `PREDATOR DETECTED: user 8812 is grooming a 12-year-old in #general. Risk: 94% HIGH. Ban now?` Six failures: labels a person, asserts a crime, presents a model score as certainty, asserts the target's exact age from a probabilistic band, uses alarm styling, and offers a punitive action as the default.

**A good one.** "Review suggested. Pair 4f2a: an account in the 16-17 band asked who supervises the younger account's phone, then asked to continue the conversation on another app, 19 hours later. Age bands are role-derived and unverified. Two other pairs from this account in the last 7 days." Five properties: names the pair, describes what was said in ordered behavioral terms, states the elapsed time that makes it a pattern, flags the confidence of its own inputs, and gives actor context without a conclusion.

**Also good, for a parent surface later.** "A contact asked your child to move to Snapchat and asked about who checks their phone." Behavior, no adjective, no accusation, and it maps to the language DHS and Safe House Project already use with parents.

| Violating pattern | Why it fails | Replacement |
|---|---|---|
| Predator detected | Labels a person, asserts a crime | Review suggested / Progression pattern in this pair |
| Predator as a category chip (Bark, MMGuardian, Troomi, Kidas) | The category names the person, not the behavior | A stage name: Supervision probe, Migration ask, Coercion language |
| Groomer, pedophile, abuser, creep, sicko | Direct labels | the older-band account, this actor |
| Suspect, offender, perpetrator | Criminal-process nouns Guardian has no standing to use | flagged account, actor, the account in the older band |
| Bad actor | Industry idiom, still a character judgment | actor, plus a tier |
| Caught, catch, nabbed, busted | Vigilante vocabulary, the framing of the PredCord and Predator Catching servers | flagged, surfaced, raised for review |
| Rescue, raid, takedown, stop a predator | NGO fundraising register; implies Guardian acts | report drafted, bundle exported, operator action applied |
| This user is dangerous / high-risk user | Predicate adjective on a person | T2 review on a pair; actor skew rising across 3 pairs |
| Risk score: 94 as a headline | Presents a model output as a fact about a human | Tier T2. Top contributing signals: ... |
| X% likely to be a predator | Probability attached to identity | Stage 4 label, confidence 0.77 |
| Watchlist, flagged users list | A list of people is the public-list feature rule 4 forbids | T1 watch, retained 30 days, scoped to a pair |
| Grooming detected | Asserts the crime occurred | Signals consistent with stages 3 and 4 in this pair |
| Confirmed predator on a T3 | Even a human reviewer confirms a report, not a person | Reviewer-confirmed: meets criteria for an online enticement report |
| Victim in operator-facing UI | Asserts harm occurred; the younger account may be a peer or a mis-banded adult | younger-band participant. The word victim is permitted only inside the NCMEC report form, where it is the schema's own field name |
| Innocent, cleared, exonerated on dismiss | Dismiss is a routing decision, not a finding | No action. Insufficient signal |
| False alarm | Implies the alarm was about a person | Not actionable / Signal did not hold |
| Ban this predator | Both halves fail | Apply the action this server configured |
| Report to police | Wrong path | Draft a CyberTipline report |
| Report this person | Reports concern incidents, not persons | Report this incident |
| AI detected a predator | Attributes a verdict to a model that cannot produce one | The model assigned tier T2. A human reviewer decides T3 |
| Threat used as a noun for a person | Person as object | threat template match, critical signal |
| Sirens, warning emoji, ALERT: in caps | Alarm styling; a mod channel is not an emergency room | Plain sentence, tier word, tier-colored left border |
| Suspicious user | Adjective on a person | pair showing a progression pattern |
| Guardian caught 14 predators this month | Turns the product into a scoreboard and the output into an accusation | 14 pairs reached T2. 3 reports drafted. 47 reviewer minutes per 1,000 users |
| Your child is talking to a predator (parent surface) | The worst version of all of the above | A contact asked to move to Snapchat and asked about who checks the phone |
| Webhook keys like is_predator, threat_actor, guilty | The rule applies to the API, not just the pixels | tier, pair_id, signals[], stage_vector |

**Enforcement.** A lint rule over all string literals in apps/review, apps/discord-bot, packages/sdk-ts and every log message, with the left column as the deny list. It fails CI, and it is added in week 1, before there are strings to fix.

### 6.8 Wellness controls

Text-native, because the published evidence base is about images and none of it transfers cleanly.

**Structural, non-negotiable.** No imagery in any Guardian surface, which removes the largest exposure vector before design begins and is the reason Guardian can plausibly claim a better wellness posture than any product in the audit. A session budget of two hours of T2 case time per reviewer per day by default, org-configurable downward only, with a hard cap on cases opened per hour. A forced micro-break every 25 to 30 minutes and a 90-second buffer after any critical-signal case. Rotation off the T2 queue after a configurable number of weeks: Roberts's finding is that industrial dispersion destroys the peer support that protects moderators, and a lone reviewer at a 40-person customer is the worst case in that literature, which is why Escalate to second reviewer and internal comments are core rather than optional.

**Interface.** Severity preview before the case opens. Collapsed explicit spans with reveal on hover, click and keyboard, org default plus per-reviewer override. Reveal order controlled by the reviewer, never auto-expanded. An exposure meter weighted by severity, visible at all times. The decision area visually separated from the content area. A calm palette (6.13) with no red on the tier scale, no motion, no pulsing, no count-up animations, no sound.

**Organizational, surfaced in the product.** Wellness check-ins are confidential from the operator by construction: results reach the reviewer, and the operator sees participation, never content. The Ghana allegations, where personal disclosures circulated among managers, are why this is architectural rather than a policy line. Counseling and support links are reachable from inside the case view in one click, not from an HR portal. Exposure disclosure happens at onboarding, in the reviewer's own contract, per DESIGN section 9.

### 6.9 Operator dashboard

One screen for the trust and safety lead, weekly cadence rather than a live wall. It answers three questions and stops.

**Row 1, queue health.** Open by tier, median time to decision, SLA breach count over 7 days, oldest unclaimed, and cases skipped. Coop exports skipped items, and a rising skip rate is a difficulty signal rather than a laziness signal, so label it that way.

**Row 2, cost and calibration.** Reviewer minutes per 1,000 monthly active users per day is the headline number: DESIGN section 10 sets 2 or fewer as a pass, and it is also what a prospective customer needs to staff against. Tier rates as a share of scored pairs, trended, with a version marker wherever model_version, lexicon_version or fusion_version changed, since rising alert counts after a version bump are a measurement artifact until proven otherwise. Realized PPV per tier, computed from reviewer decisions, against the section 6.4 targets. Reviewer load distribution in cases and minutes for rebalancing, explicitly not a speed ranking. Agreement rate on double-reviewed T3 cases, plus reverse-QA results from seeded known-answer cases.

**Row 3, retention and audit.** Retention status by class: T0 raw text purged within 24h with counts pending and overdue, T1 30d, T3 one-year preservation timers with the earliest expiry. Deletion job last run, next run and failures. Audit chain status: last verification run, chain head hash, verification result, and a Verify now button that walks the chain and names any row that fails. The DESIGN section 10 test is that tampering with a stored evidence row makes verification fail and name the row, and the dashboard is where that becomes a customer-visible capability. An export button producing an auditor-ready package with chain verification, version history, tier rates, PPV and retention compliance, which is the artifact Australia's eSafety undertaking now asks Roblox for.

**Row 4, lexicon.** Pending candidates mined from confirmed cases and from lexicon-false-positive dismissals, with the case count behind each. Approve or reject, writing a new lexicon_version with the approver and timestamp in the audit chain. Per-customer extension so operators add platform slang, which is exactly what Stream's customers asked for alongside filter logs sliced by game and language. Shadow mode: a proposed lexicon or threshold change runs against the last 7 days of traffic and reports what would have changed before it goes live, which is Osprey's roadmap feature and the sandbox practitioners asked Stream for. The base-rate simulation and teen-romance control gate the merge; shadow mode makes the same idea visible to the customer.

**Not on the dashboard.** No map. No count of flagged people. No "predators stopped." No live-scrolling feed of alerts. No per-reviewer speed leaderboard.

### 6.10 Discord guild setup flow

Six steps, each skippable except the first two, resumable, completable on a phone in under five minutes.

**Step 0, install.** OAuth consent lists exactly what the bot reads, with one plain sentence above the scopes: Guardian reads messages in the channels you allow, cannot read DMs, and never sends messages to your members. The DM limitation is a feature rather than an apology, because the DM request happens in public channels first.

**Step 1, mod channel.** Pick an existing channel or let Guardian create #guardian-alerts with restricted permissions, then immediately post a sample alert card so the owner sees the exact format before anything real arrives. Warn if the selected channel is visible to everyone.

**Step 2, roles to age bands.** A two-column mapper: server roles on the left, the six Roblox-scheme bands on the right. Unmapped roles default to unknown, which is a real value rather than a gap, since an unknown band suppresses the age-gap multiplier rather than assuming adulthood. Alongside it, a provenance note the owner cannot dismiss: Discord's teen-by-default status gives teen or adult, not a band; role-derived bands are what your members told you; Guardian treats both as estimates and shows the source on every alert. The audit is unambiguous here, since Roblox's facial age estimation misclassified children as adults within hours of launch, verified accounts have been resold, and Discord's own age assurance has documented bypasses.

**Step 3, trusted roles.** The owner marks moderators, admins, verified adults and known parents. Stated on screen: a trusted role suppresses the fan-out signal firing on its own, because mods talk to many members. It does not suppress progression patterns or critical signals, and a trusted-role account that hits stage 3 then stage 4 is reviewed at raised priority rather than lowered, because adults with standing in a community are exactly the pattern the case files describe.

**Step 4, actions per tier.** T1: no action, ever, not configurable. T2: alert to the mod channel, always on, plus an optional and default-off auto-timeout with a duration picker, whose opt-in copy reads: a timeout is a service action you take on your own server, it is not a finding about anyone, and Guardian will apply it automatically at T2 if you enable this. It requires an explicit checkbox and re-confirmation. T3 is not available here, because only a reviewer produces T3 and a server owner's path is the drafted bundle in 6.11; show the row greyed with that explanation rather than hiding it, so the boundary is legible. Cadence covers the grouping window, quiet hours, escalation role, and a locked line stating that critical signals bypass all of it.

**Step 5, data and retention.** A plain-language statement of what leaves the server (features, hashed IDs, tier events, and for T1 and above only the excerpts needed for a bundle), what is never accepted (image and video bytes, dropped at the edge), and the retention clock per tier. This screen doubles as the artifact Discord's privileged-intent review asks for: since 2026-06-10 the trigger is 10,000 unique reachable users rather than 100 servers, and the application requires either a stated retention policy with security measures or a confirmation of in-memory-only processing. One kid-heavy server can cross that threshold alone, so this copy is a week-1 deliverable.

**Step 6, test.** Guardian posts a synthetic alert built from a fabricated pair, clearly labeled EXAMPLE with no real member data, so the owner sees the card and the buttons work before real traffic arrives. Schlep's core grievance was that reports vanished into a black box, so the setup flow ends by proving the loop closes.

### 6.11 Report draft flow

Two variants sharing one bundle.

**Platform customer (ESP API).** Guardian files as the customer's agent using the customer's own NCMEC credentials. There is no self-registration for CyberTipline API access; credentials are issued on request and each ESP holds its own account, so apps/report is a per-customer encrypted credential store plus an agent submitter rather than a Guardian-owned pipe.

The submit screen reviews a pre-filled form, never a blank one. Incident type is preselected from the reviewer's reason chip, changeable with a confirmation. Required minimums are highlighted (incident type, ISO 8601 timestamp with timezone), and everything above that is optional to NCMEC and mandatory to Guardian, because Stanford's finding and NCMEC's own 2025 data (more than 10% of industry reports lacking enough information to determine jurisdiction, up from more than 8% in 2024) are that optional fields are why reports die. Guardian's required-beyond-NCMEC set: jurisdiction-relevant identifiers, a complete conversation excerpt rather than a truncated one (NCMEC names arbitrary limits on enticement chat excerpts as a top complaint), a per-media-hash viewed-by-ESP flag set honestly, reviewer identity, decision timestamp, model and lexicon and fusion versions, an audit chain reference, and the reviewer's free-text context note. Plus the sextortion annotation and imminent-danger escalation with mandatory reason text.

Dedupe before submit: check for an open case on the same actor and offer to bundle rather than file a near-duplicate. On success, store the report ID, start the one-year preservation timer, write the audit entry, and show a four-stage status bar (Drafted, Submitted, Acknowledged, Preserved). The status trail is the answer to the ignored-ticket complaint that runs through the whole audit. Warn that an unfinished NCMEC report auto-deletes after 24 hours while Guardian's local draft persists independently. Retract is available and maps to the API's retract endpoint, with a reason.

**Discord owner or parent (drafted public-form bundle).** The owner is the reporter and Guardian drafts. The screen is a seven-step walkthrough mirroring the public form at report.cybertip.org, with Guardian's fields pre-filled and copyable, plus a one-paragraph explanation of why this goes to NCMEC and not to local police, because it is the question every owner asks and the answer (FBI staff embedded at NCMEC triage CyberTipline reports and route them to field offices) is the whole justification for the single reporting path. A persistent Quick Exit control, copied from NCMEC's own form. A preservation reminder in DHS's own words: save usernames and originals, do not alter, change or delete, and Guardian never invites a media attachment and says so. Resource lines: NCMEC 1-800-THE-LOST, Know2Protect 1-833-591-5669, and for a trafficking-pattern bundle the National Human Trafficking Hotline (1-888-373-7888, text 233733), plus NCMEC Take It Down for a sextortion victim, since it hashes on-device and never uploads the image, which is the same posture as Guardian's media rule. No option to post the bundle publicly, share it to a channel, or export it as a screenshot-friendly image.

### 6.12 Accessibility

Target WCAG 2.2 AA, plus a few things this domain requires.

Tier is never encoded in color alone: every tier badge carries its text label and a distinct shape or border weight, because reviewers work fatigued and often on secondary monitors. Contrast 4.5:1 for body and 3:1 for UI components carrying meaning, validated in both themes, since a near-monochrome palette makes it easy to ship a two-step ramp that fails. Keyboard first: the decision panel is keyboard-driven by design, and every reveal-on-hover span must also open on focus and via a persistent per-case reveal-all toggle, or the collapse feature becomes a barrier rather than a protection. Focus is always visible, including inside the timeline, and focus order follows reading order.

Screen reader semantics: the timeline is a list of messages, each announcing speaker, time and stage annotation once; collapsed spans announce their type and word count, not their content; queue count changes announce politely while individual new cases do not announce at all, because a queue that interrupts is a queue that gets muted. Reduced motion: no transitions on tier changes, no animated counters, no auto-scrolling timeline, with the reduced state as the queue default. No timeouts that move the user: the SLA countdown never navigates away, never auto-releases a claim without a warning and an extension, and never blocks input.

Language: non-English excerpts carry lang attributes, and machine translation renders as a clearly labeled secondary layer with the original always adjacent, never replacing it, because the evidence is the original. Target sizes at least 24 by 24 CSS pixels, with the three Discord alert buttons sized for a phone. Error recovery: every destructive or irreversible action is confirmed and, where possible, undoable with a compensating entry. Both themes are first-class, since reviewers working evenings will use dark and the tier ramp and collapsed-span affordance must be validated in both.

### 6.13 Visual direction

**The feeling to hit.** A well-made professional instrument, close to Linear, Cinder and Coop, nearer to a clinical records system than a security dashboard. The reviewer should feel that someone competent thought about their day, and the trust and safety lead should feel comfortable putting a screenshot in front of a regulator.

**What reads as professional and calm.** One grotesk throughout (IBM Plex Sans, already used in the DESIGN diagrams), 14px base, tabular numerals for all counts and timers, generous line height in the timeline and tight elsewhere. A near-monochrome palette of ink on a warm off-white ground, one accent used only for interactive affordances, and a restrained four-step tier ramp: neutral grey (T0), sand (T1), amber (T2), deep teal (T3). Red appears nowhere on the tier scale; it is reserved for destructive irreversible actions, so when a reviewer sees red it means something. Information-dense but quiet, with rules and whitespace rather than cards inside cards, and one status column rather than four badge clusters. Outcome badges for the report pipeline may use color as Beacon does, because those describe system state rather than a person. Essentially no motion.

**What reads as cheap or alarmist.** Gradients. Glow and drop shadows. Siren and warning emoji in system strings. Shield, badge, handcuff and magnifying-glass iconography. Dark hacker chrome with monospace everywhere. Animated counters. Progress rings. Any photography of children. Any avatar rendered large. Pulsing unread dots. Full-bleed red banners.

**Products that get it right, and what to take.** Cinder for the decision panel: number-key policy labels with the apply and undo hint printed on them, a live queue count, target handling time as a queue property rather than a reviewer stopwatch, wellness settings at the queue level, and every decision logged with its reasoning; also its framing that every child safety report is a handoff. ROOST Coop for the queue and its metadata: queues with counts, priorities and SLA status, a job never assigned to two moderators, per-queue hidden actions so a Discord-owner queue cannot reach the CyberTipline, org-default wellness with per-user override, and a decisions log with a skipped-case export. GoGuardian Beacon for the alert-to-decision workflow and the best-documented alert schema in the market: Predicted Phase beside Risk Level, behavioral rather than diagnostic phase names, a frequency indicator per subject, actions-taken chips and a three-prompt notes template, grouping windows and snooze durations and per-category cadence and after-hours lists, and unread as light fill plus dark outline with color reserved for risk. Discord AutoMod for the mod-channel embed, so Guardian's card feels like a sibling of the one mods already read. Magnet Griffeye for the triage philosophy in the later investigator surface: pre-categorize, stack duplicates, show the human only the residue. Meta's reviewer tooling for region-aware term highlighting with hover definitions. Roblox Sentinel for nearest-exemplar explanations as evidence content. NCMEC's public form for Quick Exit and the discipline of asking only what is necessary.

**Products that get it wrong, and what to avoid.** Bark's consumer alert aesthetic, its Predator category name, and the fact that a parent's only recordable decision is read, saved or ignored. MMGuardian and Troomi's Predator chip attached to a specific named contact, the most direct violation of the never-label rule shipping today. PredCord and Predator Catching's catches, fights and live channel structure, which Guardian's alert copy and bundle export must be structurally unusable for. Our Rescue's rescue and raid vocabulary and headline rescue counters; Guardian counts pairs, tiers and reviewer minutes, never people. OurPact and Mobicip's periodic screenshots as the evidence artifact. Aura's tone-only summary card with no path to evidence, which reviewers and parents both rate as unactionable. Net Nanny's settings buried behind avatar navigation.

### 6.14 Build order for the UI

**Phase 1, with the Discord bot.** The string lint rule from 6.7, which costs an afternoon and is unenforceable retroactively. The Discord alert embed with its grouping, cadence and critical-signal floor. The six-step guild setup flow, with the retention-and-data screen doubling as the privileged-intent application artifact. Event-schema fields that cannot be added later without re-tuning thresholds: age_band_provenance, age_band_confidence, band-as-captured-at-event-time, a derived four-bracket field for the statutory age signals, and the per-media-hash viewed_by_operator_human flag. The drafted public-form bundle export.

**Phase 2, with the review queue.** Queue list with claim locking, severity ordering and SLA countdown. Case detail with the severity preview, the stage-vector strip and the evidence timeline including normalization and lexicon reveal. Decision panel with the full reason taxonomy, notes template, minutes capture, two-person T3 rule and undo. Wellness: session budget, exposure meter, buffer pause, collapsed spans with three reveal modes. Operator dashboard rows 1 through 4, including audit-chain verification and shadow mode.

**Open question to decide before phase 2 starts.** Whether apps/review is a full console or a differentiated case view that also emits Coop-compatible jobs. Coop 1.0 is free, Apache-2.0, self-hosted, and already ships queues, SLA status, wellness defaults and CyberTipline submission. Guardian's defensible surface is the pair timeline, the stage annotation, the ordered progression display and the evidence bundle, not a generic queue. Building a connector alongside the queue costs little now and preserves the option of selling into customers who already run Coop.

---

## 7. Critic's notes

A completeness critique of the assembly above, run after the verification pass. The critic's session had an exhausted web-search budget, so verification was by direct fetch (Roblox model card, EU AI Act text, ROOST org and Coop repo and docs, Safer). Discord's developer policy returned 403 through every route tried. Items marked unverified are the ones a search would close; items marked checked were confirmed against a primary source.

### 7.1 Competitive landscape

**ROOST Coop is not a queue to emit into. It is the whole downstream product, free.** [checked] A4 frames Coop as a review console. Its own README calls it the first free end-to-end online child safety system, and the user docs list NCMEC reporting, appeals, strikes, bulk actioning, moderator wellness, audit logs, dashboards, and built-in free safety APIs from Google, OpenAI, Zentropi and NCMEC. Osprey is the free rules engine beside it. That is apps/review plus apps/report plus part of apps/scorer, at zero. The gap analysis prices the free floor as a queue when it is actually the floor under two of the five apps. To close it: read the Coop user docs end to end and the Coop and Osprey issue trackers before phase 2 is scoped.

**The ROOST Model Community is a detection library, and one of its models is a direct substitute for the phase-2 classifier.** [checked] Partner models: Roblox Sentinel, roblox-pii-classifier-v2, voice-safety-classifier-v3, OpenAI gpt-oss-safeguard, Mistral Shieldstral-1.0-3B, a Mila suicide guardrail, and Zentropi CoPE-B-A4B, a bring-your-own-policy safety model with open weights. A policy-conditioned open-weight model is exactly how you get a grooming-stage classifier without training one, and neither document mentions it. To close it: benchmark CoPE and gpt-oss-safeguard zero-shot against the PANC held-out set before any fine-tune is budgeted.

**No monitoring plan for the free floor.** ROOST is actively adding partner models. The day the library adds a grooming or CSE text classifier with weights, B1 and C5 change materially, and nobody owns watching the org.

**A whole competitor tier is missing.** Two Hat / Community Sift is the incumbent for small and mid-size game chat and is the direct occupant of C5's claimed segment. Also absent: k-ID (age assurance and compliance for games, selling to exactly Guardian's buyer), Yoti, SafeToNet and Privately SA (on-device grooming detection, which is prior art for the phase-5 parent app), GGWP, Checkstep, Cove, Tremau, Resolver/Crisp, Bodyguard.ai and ActiveFence. To close it: one capability-page sweep per vendor for the words grooming, minor and child safety, plus pricing pages to test B9's capability-cliff claim.

**Microsoft Project Artemis is unmentioned prior art.** [unverified] Microsoft built a grooming-detection technique from Xbox chat and licensed it free through Thorn from January 2020. It is the closest published ancestor of Guardian's core and may be patented. To close it: the January 2020 Microsoft post, plus Google Patents by assignee.

### 7.2 Modalities never searched

**Patents.** Ordered stage-progression scoring over a conversation pair is precisely the shape that gets claimed. Search Google Patents for grooming and chat-stage classification, assignees Microsoft, Thorn, Roblox, Bark Technologies and Two Hat.

**Discord bot directories.** Nobody checked whether a child-safety Discord bot already ships, what its reviews say, or what the App Directory verification bar is for a message-content app. Check top.gg and the Discord application directory for safety, child and grooming, and read the one-star reviews of any high-alert-volume bot.

**Name collision.** Guardian is crowded in this exact category. Check USPTO TESS in classes 9 and 42, plus top.gg. Cellebrite already ships a product called Guardian.

**Job postings and public RFPs.** Roblox and Discord trust-and-safety job descriptions are a free read on what the buyer specifies; SAM.gov and state ICAC grant solicitations are the same for the investigator surface.

**Conference talks.** TrustCon and the Dallas Crimes Against Children Conference are where both buyers sit, and neither was searched.

**Adversarial modality entirely absent.** Neither document asks what an offender who has read Guardian's published model card and open lexicon does. B7 (publish recall at fixed FPR) and DESIGN section 6.5 (a versioned extensible lexicon) are in direct tension, and the tension is unresolved.

### 7.3 Claims asserted without a checkable source

The one claim the critic could verify held exactly: Roblox PII v2's contract is as A1 states. That raises confidence in the author but does not transfer to the rest. Everything else in the critic's pass was single-sourced or unsourced at the time of writing, including the 39,202-conversation benchmark, the 338M age-check records, voice v3's 61% recall at 1% FPR, SCoRL's 0.475 at 0.58%, UW 2025, HateBuffer, AURA, the Discord threshold change, TAKE IT DOWN's $53,088, Commonwealth v. Meta, Lowers, Maher and Enigma II. Section 4 above closes most of these; the remainder need a citation pass against primary sources only (EUR-Lex, CourtListener, the Federal Register for the FTC penalty figure, the Hugging Face dataset card for the benchmark count).

One date looked internally wrong to the critic: the EU interim derogation ran to 2026-04-03, and a successor in force 2026-07-31 leaves roughly four months with no derogation covering grooming detection in EU private messaging. Section 4's eu-derogation-lapse entry confirms this is exactly what happened, and the four-month gap is real.

### 7.4 An expert literature that contradicts the central recommendation

**The differentiator rests on a stage model much of the field says is not sequential.** B1 and C1 make ordered progression the wedge, with 3 to 4 and 5 to 6 weighted 2x. But Williams, Elliott and Beech (2013) found grooming themes overlap and recur rather than progress; Black et al. (2015) found compression and reordering; Winters, Kaylor and Jeglic (2017) found many contact offenders reach sexual content almost immediately, skipping trust and probe entirely; and Kloess et al. (2017, 2019) found wide behavioural heterogeneity. O'Connell's 2003 six-stage model, which DHS, Child Rescue Coalition and DESIGN section 3 all inherit, is decoy-derived and heavily criticised. If order is not reliably present, an order-rewarding score systematically under-fires on the fast, hyper-sexualised population that B2's second velocity window exists to catch, and the 2x transition bonus is an untested constant. To close it: read those four papers plus any 2024-2026 systematic review, then measure order adherence on PANC and PJZ before the claim is marketed.

**Guardian forbids decoys but trains on decoy corpora.** PANC, PJZ and PAN-12 are all adults performing children. Stage order in those transcripts is partly an artifact of the decoy's script, which means the distribution shift lands squarely on the differentiator rather than diffusely on the classifier. DESIGN section 12 acknowledges the corpus problem but does not connect it to B1 and C1.

### 7.5 Legal

**The EU AI Act is absent from both documents.** [checked against Regulation 2024/1689] Article 5(1)(d) prohibits AI risk assessment predicting a natural person's likelihood of committing a criminal offence based solely on profiling or on assessing personality traits and characteristics. Guardian's per-actor skew score (embedding drift toward a grooming centroid, plus fan-out, account age and alt clustering) detached from a specific conversation is close to that shape. The carve-out is systems used to support human assessment of a person's involvement in criminal activity that is already based on objective and verifiable facts directly linked to a criminal activity, so Guardian can probably live inside it, but only if the actor score is never the sole basis for any output and the audit chain evidences the conversational facts. Separately, the investigator surface falls under Annex III point 6(d) (risk of offending) and 6(e) (profiling in detection or investigation), which would make Guardian a high-risk provider with conformity assessment, technical documentation, logging, human-oversight and registration duties. Nothing in the build order budgets for that. To close it: Articles 5, 6, 16-27 and Annex III, plus the Commission's February 2025 guidelines on prohibited practices.

**Discord's Developer Policy has not been read, and phase 1 depends on it.** [unverified, 403] Both documents assume a bot may retain message excerpts for 30 days at T1 and a year at T3 and hand them to a third party for a law-enforcement-adjacent report. Discord's developer terms have historically constrained message-content retention, third-party sharing and surveillance-shaped tooling. This is the single most product-threatening unverified assumption in the wedge, and it sits upstream of the 6.10 Step 5 retention screen and the privileged-intent application. To close it: read the Developer Policy, Developer Terms of Service and the Message Content privileged-intent FAQ verbatim before that screen's copy is written.

**2258B immunity probably does not reach Guardian, and NCMEC agent submission is asserted without a source.** Section 6.11 has Guardian filing as the customer's agent using the customer's own credentials, but no source says the ESP API permits agent submission. And if Guardian, a non-provider, obtains actual knowledge of apparent exploitation, its own position under 2258A(a) and under state mandatory-reporter statutes is unaddressed. To close it: NCMEC ESP API registration terms and the statute text.

**The mod-channel alert is publication to third parties.** D3 analyses Guardian's own labelling but never asks who is liable once a volunteer moderator screenshots the embed, in servers the audit itself says overlap with catches communities. The mitigations (role-gated evidence, per-viewer watermarking, an operator terms clause forbidding redistribution) are absent from the brief.

**The UK Online Safety Act and the Ofcom children's codes are unmentioned in the gap analysis.** UK customers carry children's risk-assessment and highly-effective-age-assurance duties that role-derived Discord bands do not satisfy. That is both an exposure and an unclaimed sales wedge.

**Reviewer and operator mandated-reporter status is unaddressed**, as is what a reviewer does when text describes abuse in progress and the 4-hour SLA has not elapsed.

### 7.6 UX patterns the brief recommends that carry documented harm

**Beacon is the most-copied and the most-criticised product in the brief.** The model badge, phase names, frequency indicator, notes template and after-hours escalation all come from GoGuardian Beacon, whose alerts have been reported to produce wrongful police welfare checks and to out LGBTQ students to school staff. The failure mechanism is identical to Guardian's mod-channel card: an untrained adult receives a model-generated behavioural claim about a specific named minor with an action button attached. To close it: read the investigative coverage, and check whether GoGuardian has since retired or reworded the phase model.

**Bark's unmutable severity floor is recommended without checking how users route around it.** A floor a moderator cannot mute becomes a channel the moderator leaves. To close it: Bark app-store and subreddit reviews, plus top.gg reviews of high-volume safety bots.

**The Not an issue button is a poisonable write path.** Section 6.2 sends it into the lexicon-mining loop. Anyone with mod permissions can write to it, including an actor who is a moderator, which is the exact pattern in the Castillo case file. No rate limit, reviewer gate or anomaly check is specified. That is a security defect, not a UX nit.

**An internal contradiction voids the product for its stated market.** Section 6.1 says a reviewer may be the only reviewer at a 40-person customer, and section 6.6 requires a second reviewer for T3. Under both, a small customer can never file a report, which is precisely the segment C5 claims. It needs a designated escalation path (a Guardian-side or partner reviewer pool), and that path has its own 2258A and AI Act shape.

**Build-versus-Coop is filed as an open question to decide before phase 2 starts.** Given what Coop verifiably ships, it determines whether phase 2 exists at all, and deferring it costs a phase.

---

## 8. What to build next

Five items, reordered after the critic. Four of the original five survive; one does not survive intact, and one is blocked until a document is read.

| # | Item | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Roblox PII v2 contract and evasion benchmark | Survives, verified. The only load-bearing claim the critic could independently check, and it checked out exactly |
| 2 | Non-retrofittable schema fields | Survives, and should expand. Add an AI Act field (was the actor score the sole basis for this output) and a feedback_source on lexicon candidates |
| 3 | Ordered stages plus two windows plus fan-IN plus coercion class | Does not survive as one ticket. Two windows, fan-IN and the non-financial coercion class are cheap and well supported; ordered progression is contested by the literature and is currently carrying the entire differentiator. Split it |
| 4 | Discord alert card | Blocked pending the Developer Policy read, and it also needs the Beacon-harm review before the grammar is copied wholesale |
| 5 | Base-rate simulation, model card, privileged-intent application | Survives. The application half is more urgent than its original position suggested, since it gates the wedge and shares an artifact with the retention screen |

**The reordered list.**

**0. Decide build-versus-Coop now, not before phase 2.** ROOST Coop 1.0 is free, Apache-2.0 and self-hosted, and ships queues with SLA status, policy-bound actions, appeals, strikes, bulk actioning, wellness defaults, audit logs, dashboards and NCMEC CyberTipline submission, alongside Osprey as a free rules engine. That is apps/review plus apps/report plus part of apps/scorer at zero cost. Guardian's defensible surface is the pair timeline, the stage annotation, the ordered progression display and the evidence bundle, not a generic queue. This decision determines whether phases 2 and 3 exist in their current shape, so it cannot wait.

**1. Wire Roblox PII v2 to its exact input contract and adopt its evasion benchmark as the normalizer regression test.** It is the single load-bearing detector in phase 1, the weights are Apache-2.0 and cover 189 languages, and encoding the input as JSON instead of a `</s>`-joined string with speaker tags t, s1 and s2, 512 tokens and left truncation silently throws away the measured F1. The 39,202-conversation synthetic benchmark contains no user data and exercises exactly the homoglyph, letter-spacing, reversed-text and split-across-turns evasions the lexicon must survive, though it is English-only and does not test the 189-language claim.

**2. Read Discord's Developer Policy and Terms of Service, then file the privileged-intent application.** The application shares an artifact with the section 6.10 Step 5 retention screen, and it gates the only shipping surface. Since 2026-06-10 the trigger is 10,000 unique reachable users rather than 100 servers, with a 90-day clock from notification, annual reapplication, and continued operation during review; one large Roblox community server crosses that threshold alone. The application requires either a stated retention policy with security measures or a confirmation of in-memory processing, and Guardian's tier retention model (T0 raw text purged within 24h, T1 30d, T3 one year) is exactly the answer the form asks for. The same read settles whether a bot may retain excerpts and hand them to a third party at all, which is the most product-threatening open assumption in the wedge.

**3. Add the non-retrofittable schema fields.** age_band_confidence, age_band_provenance (facial estimate, government ID, OS bracket, server role, platform default), the band as captured at event time, a derived four-bracket field for the Texas and California statutory signals, a per-excerpt and per-media-hash viewed_by_operator_human flag, per-customer jurisdiction and legal basis with a private-versus-public channel distinction and an end-to-end-encryption flag, an AI Act field recording whether the actor score was the sole basis for an output, and a feedback_source on lexicon candidates. Six bands matching Roblox is already a moving target: Roblox added a three-tier account grouping in June 2026, the statutory signals are four brackets, and Regulation (EU) 2026/1881 permits grooming detection in private messaging only with human confirmation before any report and only using risk factors such as age difference. These fields are compliance evidence, not metadata, and none of them can be added after thresholds are tuned.

**4. Ship the two velocity windows, the fan-IN inversion and a self-harm and fansign coercion class in the critical-override list.** All three are cheap arithmetic on features the kernel already extracts, and all three are well supported: the EOGP typology says a single 24-hour window cannot serve both a sub-4-hour hyper-sexualised escalation and a weeks-long intimacy-seeking ladder; Greggy's Cult shows five actors converging on one victim set for a year undetected; and 764-style groups coerce self-harm rather than money, so every payment-keyed heuristic misses them. Ordered stage-transition scoring is split out of this ticket and gated behind item 5, because the field is not agreed that grooming is sequential.

**5. Stand up the base-rate simulation, publish the model card, and run an order-adherence evaluation on PANC and PJZ before the ordered-progression wedge is marketed.** SCoRL's best published turn-level precision is 0.475 at a 0.58% positive rate, so the honest argument for the trajectory gate is the ratio between that and live traffic rather than a claim that the classifier fails. The model card should carry recall at fixed FPR (Roblox Voice v3 is the only published precedent), the base-rate simulation, and the model, lexicon and fusion versions, which is a free differentiator against every vendor ROOST's own leadership names as undocumented. The order-adherence measurement decides whether B1 and C1 are a real wedge or an untested constant, and it should run before any marketing claim rests on ordering.
